Members see: the **decision-making tree** (recursive `organizations/OrgMemberTreeNode.vue`, nested by REPORTS_TO, per-seat switch on `is_me`), **sub-organizations** band (+ admin create form w/ lead-mask select), **rules** band, **resource tree** band (ElementMinis **keyed by acting id** — wearing the mask remounts them so grants re-resolve; a `SlotRefPicker` share panel feeds `POST /:id/share`), and the admin **invite panel** (entity search + role title + reports-to mask + admin toggle + message). Outsiders get the locked panel (membership travels by invitation) |
| `/*` (catchAll) | `ErrorNotFound` | The 404 — **Talavero's page** (2026-07-31): the same picture-banner pattern as `AccessDeniedBanner` (landscape shot full-bleed on desktop, portrait in flow on mobile, copy on the slate field) with the `404` code and "Path not found in the pathchain". Root is a **plain div on purpose**: this route mounts OUTSIDE any QLayout, and a `q-page` without a layout renders nothing (the pre-Talavero page had exactly that bug — the 404 was blank) |

## Key components

| Component | Role |
|-----------|------|
| `layout/FriezeHeader.vue` | The platform's **crown strip**, mounted by BOTH layouts: a thin band fixed edge-to-edge at the top of every page (`position: fixed`, `top: var(--media-tabs-h, 0px)` — 0 until a media viewer is parked, when `MediaTabsBar` claims the band above it and the whole top chrome steps down together, 2026-08-05 — `width: 100vw`, `z-index: 3000` — above the footer's 2600 and every dock, `pointer-events: none` so it never steals a click). Height = `var(--frieze-h)` (**2.1vh**, `_tokens.scss` — 3vh until **2026-08-02**, when the WHOLE frieze family went **30% thinner** on a user ask: the bands read right on the main layouts but sat a touch heavy, and since every one of them states its thickness as this token, one number moved them all — header, footer, every in-box `FriezeBar` incl. its `slim` variant and the flyout's grey-9 restating, and both indigo vertical bars) so it scales with the device; MainLayout/AuthLayout pad their page containers down by the same token. The **left drawer is the one thing that outranks it** (2026-07-24, `aside.q-drawer` z 3050): it starts at the screen's top-left corner and paints OVER the strip, supplying its own `FriezeBar` band in that band's place — so the strip is only actually visible to the right of the drawer's column. Two white-on-transparent SVG masks from `assets/frieze/mercury-wave-a/b.svg` (Mercury Playground board exports, extension-script stripped) stack as layers painted Quasar brown-3 `#BCAAA4` / brown-4 `#A1887F`, `mask-repeat: repeat-x`, `mask-size: auto 99%`; carve = paired **1.05px** drop-shadows (`#0b0c10` up-left, `#ffffff` down-right, full+half offsets — 1.5/0.75 until 2026-08-02, stepped down by the same 30% as the band because the offsets are absolute px and a groove wider than the stroke it carves turns the motif to smear; the identical step was made in `FriezeFooter`, `FriezeBar` and both vertical bars); base = flat Quasar brown-1 (`#EFEBE9`) — the metallic sheen gradient and glass overlayer were removed (2026-07-23) so the strip is one consistent color like the other plaques, with only the wave carve as relief. A thin brown-3 lip on the **bottom edge only** — `border-bottom: 1px solid #BCAAA4` (Quasar brown-3), the same color as the drawer's `border-right` and the footer nav bar's `border-top` (2026-07-23) — makes the strip read as a slightly thick plaque. Motif geometry still baked from the playground session — retune there, then transcribe here. |
| `layout/FriezeFooter.vue` | **RETIRED 2026-08-04** (user ask: make the floor band "like the clear one on top of the footer nav bar, and remove that one"). Was the crown strip's bookend at the bottom of the window (2026-07-25 → 2026-08-04): `FriezeHeader` with the waves MIRRORED VERTICALLY — `assets/frieze/mercury-wave-{a,b}-vmirror.svg`, the originals wrapped in a `translate(0 143) scale(1 -1)` group baked into the assets (a CSS `scaleY(-1)` would have mirrored the drop-shadow carve too; the assets stay in the tree, unused) — parked ON the nav bar's top edge at the crown strip's own z 3000, with the header's brown-1/brown-3/brown-4 palette, its carve, and its lip turned into a `border-top`. Deleted because the window-floor **FriezeBar floor band** (NavigationBar's `.nav-floor-frieze`, continued by the drawer's `.drawer-floor-frieze` and the pins column's `.pins-floor-frieze`) wears the crown strip's pale palette itself now — through the `--frieze-bar-base`/`--frieze-bar-wave-{one,two}` dials, the same seam the chat dock's green band uses — so the page is closed by ONE band and nothing rides the bar's top edge. **`--dock-bottom` (`_tokens.scss`) went with it**: the token once stood docks one `--frieze-h` higher to clear this opaque band, and every dock now anchors to `bottom: var(--nav-footer-h)` (the base `.dock-window` states that value as its default). |
| `layout/FriezeBar.vue` | The crown strip's **box-insertable sibling** (2026-07-24): same geometry as `FriezeHeader` — height `var(--frieze-h)`, 1px vertical pad, `mask-repeat: repeat-x` at `mask-size: auto 99%`, the identical paired-drop-shadow carve — but a normal in-flow block (`width: 100%`, no fixed positioning, no z-index, `pointer-events: none`) meant to sit INSIDE other boxes. Uses the **horizontally mirrored** mask exports `assets/frieze/mercury-wave-a/b-mirror.svg` (the originals wrapped in a `translate(231 0) scale(-1 1)` group) and the **inverted palette**: flat brown-4 (`--brown-4`) plaque base with brown-2 (`--brown-2`) + brown-1 (`--brown-1`) waves — the negative of the header's brown-1 base / brown-3+4 waves. **ALL THREE TONES are dials** — `var(--frieze-bar-base, var(--brown-4))` (2026-07-26) plus `var(--frieze-bar-wave-one, var(--brown-2))` / `var(--frieze-bar-wave-two, var(--brown-1))` (2026-08-03) — so a host box outside the brown colorway sets them on the element it mounts the bar on and the band follows; every placement below leaves them unset and keeps brown. Consumers: `skeletons/SkeletonFlyout.vue` feeds the base `--grey-9`, and `chat/ChatDock.vue` (2026-08-05, hue-swapped from a full-height green band to `slim` lime — see the `slim` prop below) dials base `--lime-10` with a `--brown-1` wave two. The wave dials REVERSE a note that stood here — that re-toning them "would be a different bar rather than this one dressed" — and what settled it is that the platform already HAS a band in a second family: `FriezeBarVertical` is FriezeHeader's tone mapping in indigo, so a whole colorway of this band is a thing the design does, not a costume. What must NOT move with the tones is the RECIPE (masks, `auto 99%` fit, carve offsets) — and the waves must stay a step or two ABOVE their base, since the carve lights them from the top left and reads as a groove only when the ink is lighter than the plaque it is cut into. One interaction: `slim` drops layer one, so a host dialling only `--frieze-bar-wave-one` dresses a band that isn't drawn. **`flip` prop** (2026-08-02, user ask) — the band with its meander running the OTHER WAY, for the three bands in the stack/pins right-edge column, so that column reflects the left drawer's bands instead of repeating them. It swaps the two layers' masks for the **un-mirrored `mercury-wave-{a,b}.svg`**, which by construction ARE this bar's masks flipped (each `-mirror` file is its original wrapped in `translate(231 0) scale(-1 1)` on the same canvas), so the flip costs no asset. Masks ONLY — plaque, tones and carve stay put, and pointedly **not** `transform: scaleX(-1)`, which mirrors the drop-shadow carve along with the motif and lights that band from the wrong side (the same trap `FriezeBarVerticalB` ships baked mirror assets to avoid). **`slim` prop** (2026-07-26) — the HALF-HEIGHT variant, and a whole recipe rather than a knob, because at `calc(var(--frieze-h) / 2)` (**9.45px** at a 900px viewport, inner 7.45px, since the 2026-08-02 thinning — 13.5px/11.5px before it) four things have to move together or the motif goes sub-pixel: **(1) height** halves, the 1px vertical pad stays (the only margin left at that size); **(2) ONE WAVE** — layer one (brown-2, mask `a`) is `v-if`'d OUT, not hidden, leaving the brown-1 `b` mask alone. The two masks are the SAME meander offset by half a tile — `a` carries one upper + one lower spiral, `b` carries the other pair PLUS the **full-width centre rule** — so `b` alone still tiles as a frieze, at half the spiral density and with the rule running unbroken through the middle (the one mark that survives at any size); two interlocking waves in 11px would have read as a texture, not a pattern; **(3) MASK FIT** — both masks are a 13-row grid (231×143, 11px cells) whose **top and bottom rows are EMPTY** (ink runs y 11→132 = 84.6% of the file), dead margin that reads as breathing room at full height and costs a sixth of the band when halved, so the slim bar scales the mask PAST its box at **`auto 117%`** and lets the blank rows fall outside — `0.846 × 117% ≈ 99%`, so the INK fills 99% of the inner height, exactly the fraction the full bar gives the whole FILE (same 1% breathing, moved where it can be seen; nothing is clipped). Worth ~**0.67px per motif row** against the ~0.57px a plain `99%` leaves, vs ~1.3px on a full-height bar (1.04/0.88/1.9 before the 2026-08-02 thinning — both slim figures are sub-pixel now, and the `117%` is what keeps the centre rule reading as a line rather than a haze); **(4) CARVE** — the drop-shadow offsets halve with the box (**0.5/0.3**, half of the full bar's post-thinning 1.05/0.5; it was 0.75/0.4 under the 1.5/0.75 original), since a groove WIDER than the ~1px stroke it is carving and the band would read as a black-and-white smear with a brown tint. The TONES are not part of the recipe — those are the three `--frieze-bar-*` dials, orthogonal and set by the host. Consumers: `skeletons/SkeletonFlyout.vue` and, since 2026-08-05, `chat/ChatDock.vue`'s header band (base `--lime-10`, wave two `--brown-1` — no wave-one dial, since slim never renders that layer). First placement: MainLayout's left drawer, where FOUR of them are the section dividers (at the very top above Back, then after Back, before the EXPLORE header, before the IDENTITY header — they replaced the spaced q-separators; `.drawer-frieze` — `margin: 8px 0`, NO horizontal margin so each runs the full drawer width edge to edge in both mini and expanded states; the q-list only pads vertically, so nothing insets them). The **top one is a special case** (2026-07-24): the drawer now overlaps the crown strip, so `.drawer-frieze--top` **replaces** it across the drawer's column rather than sitting under it — it is hoisted OUT of the `q-scroll-area` to be the drawer content's pinned first flex child (`flex: 0 0 auto`, `margin: 0`; the old `margin-top: -8px` q-list-padding hack is gone) so it can never scroll away from the corner, and it wears `FriezeHeader`'s **exact box**: `height: var(--frieze-h)` plus the same `border-bottom: 1px solid #BCAAA4` brown-3 lip — which, border-box, also eats the same 1px off the carve area, so both motifs are drawn at an identical size and the band steps nowhere at the drawer's right edge (verified: both boxes measure `top 0, h 27, border-bottom 1px, padding-top 1px`). Only the **palette** differs by design — the drawer's band keeps FriezeBar's inverted brown-4 base. Second placement (2026-07-24): the stack/pins side widgets carry **three between them**, full widget width in both presentations — the stack's **top edge** (`.stack-frieze`), the stack's **inner** band between its well and its bottom header/head glyph (`.stack-frieze--inner`), and the pins' single band between its **top header/pin glyph and its well** (`.pins-frieze`). Only the stack's top band is the **same special case as the drawer's**: that widget sits at `top: 0` over the crown strip, so its band **replaces** the strip across its column and is likewise matched to `FriezeHeader`'s exact box (`height: var(--frieze-h)` + the 1px brown-3 bottom lip, with the widget's own `border-top` dropped). The two INNER bands stand in for nothing, so they take the plain `height: var(--frieze-h)` with **no lip** — border-box resolves all of them to the same 27px box either way (verified in place: bands at `top 0` / `top 509` on the stack, `top 640` on the pins, each `h 27`). The pins widget's old **bottom-edge** band (against the nav footer) was removed the same day — that seam would cut the pinned column off from the tack in the bar, which should read as one strip. **ONE TILING, always** — the two layers span the box (`inset: 0`) and the masks anchor `left center`, so the motif runs one way across the whole band, every placement. A **`mirrored` prop lived here for a single day** (2026-07-27, added and removed) for the flyout's inner band: the band cut in half about its centre, each half an absolutely-positioned tiling running OUTWARD from the seam — the right the usual `-mirror` masks from the centre rightward, the left the **un-mirrored `mercury-wave-{a,b}.svg`** (by construction the exact horizontal mirror of the `-mirror` pair — each `-mirror` file is its original wrapped in `translate(231 0) scale(-1 1)` on the same 231×143 canvas, so the mirror cost no new asset) at `mask-position: right`. It came out because the centre SEAM is a visible event — two motifs meeting nose to nose in the middle of an 11px strip, in the one spot the eye lands — where the flyout's band is there to be a quiet rule between the head and the well; the right half simply extends across the box instead. Kept here in case a centred band is ever wanted again, with the one trap: **NOT `transform: scaleX(-1)`** — the carve is a `drop-shadow` filter and a mirrored element mirrors its filter with it, putting the dark edge up-RIGHT on one half and up-LEFT on the other, two light sources in one band (`FriezeBarVerticalB` ships baked mirror assets for exactly this reason), and no `overflow: hidden` on the halves, since the carve paints up to 1.5px outside the ink and clipping shears the groove off both sides of the seam. **THREE ADDITIONS ON 2026-08-07** (user ask, for the post card's frieze pair — `posts/FeedStream.vue`, the platform's fourth `FriezeBar` consumer and the first to mount TWO). (1) **`vflip`** turns the meander round the HORIZONTAL axis, the counterpart to `flip`'s vertical one, and by the same mechanism — an ASSET SWAP, never a `transform: scaleY(-1)`, which would take the drop-shadow carve with it and light one band from below. The masks are now the four corners of one square: plain (`flip`), `-mirror` (the default), `-vmirror` (`flip vflip`) and the new `-mirror-vmirror` pair — the originals wrapped in `translate(231 143) scale(-1 -1)`, i.e. turned 180° — which `vflip` alone selects; the compound rule `.frieze-bar--flip.frieze-bar--vflip` must stay MORE SPECIFIC than the single-class ones or the cascade answers a `flip vflip` bar by source order. It is NOT the removed `mirrored` variant described below (that one cut ONE band about its centre; this turns a WHOLE band over so two of them can bracket something as a reflected pair). (2) **The wave dials gained an IMAGE form** — `--frieze-bar-wave-{one,two}-paint`, `none` unless set, laid over the flat tone: the layers are a mask over a painted box, so a gradient fills the motif exactly as a colour does and the plaque underneath (a different element's background) is untouched. One ramp spans the WHOLE bar however many times the 231px mask repeats, since gradients have no intrinsic size, and the carve reads ALPHA, which paint does not touch. (3) **The height is a dial** — `--frieze-bar-h`, defaulting to `--frieze-h` (`slim`: half it). The mask fit follows the box for free (its `99%`/`117%` are fractions of it); the carve does NOT, its offsets being absolute px, which is both deliberate (a groove is a fixed width) and the floor on the dial — trim far enough and the 0.5px groove is wider than the strokes it cuts. |
| `layout/FriezeBarVertical.vue` | **The crown strip turned 90° CLOCKWISE, in the INDIGO colorway** (2026-07-25). Everything that made `FriezeHeader` is rotated with it, so the two read as the same object seen on its side: thickness is `width: var(--frieze-bar-v-w, var(--frieze-h))` (the header's height, same token, now measured across — a **published dial** since the `slim` variant, which is how `FeedPage` runs the feed container's two edges at `0.8 ×` it on a 2026-08-07 user ask, and how `slim` states its own half; it drives the `flex-basis` too, so a host never restates the geometry); the motif is the SAME mercury waves exported **pre-rotated** — `assets/frieze/mercury-wave-{a,b}-rot90.svg`, the originals wrapped in a `translate(143 0) rotate(90)` group with the canvas swapped 231×143 → 143×231 (the same asset trick as the `-mirror` pair FriezeBar uses) — tiled `mask-repeat: repeat-y` at `mask-size: 99% auto` (the header's `auto 99%` on its side), `mask-position: center top`; padding is `0 1px` (the header's `1px 0`); and **the carve rotates too** — a vector `(dx,dy)` turned 90° CW becomes `(-dy,dx)`, so the header's dark up-left + light down-right read here as dark up-RIGHT + light down-LEFT, making the render pixel-identical to physically rotating the strip. The **lip** follows the same turn: the header's `border-bottom` lands on the LEFT edge, which is the default `lip="left"`; `lip="right"` mirrors it so a PAIR of bars can face their lips inward around a box. The one thing that is NOT a rotation is the **palette** — and as of the end of 2026-07-25 it is not even a variation: the bar wears **`FriezeHeader`'s OWN tone mapping in Quasar's indigos**, so it is a true colorway of the crown strip. **INVERTED on 2026-08-07** (user ask): base **`--indigo-8`** under wave layer one **`--indigo-1`** (the lit face) and wave layer two **`--indigo-3`** (the dark) — a DEEP plate under PALE waves, where the crown strip and every setting of this bar since 2026-07-25 ran a pale plate under deeper ones. The four-role MAPPING is untouched and wave one is still the lit face; only its DIRECTION flipped, and the pair kept its **TWO-LEVEL SPREAD** through the flip, because the spread is the carve and not the depth. Two things change ends with it and must be re-read: the **headroom** (a pale pair is capped by the family's lightest step, `-1`, so open the spread DOWNWARD — take wave two to `-4`, never lift wave one) and the **carve's white flank**, which now falls on a near-white wave, so the lit side of the groove reads much softer than the dark side (the same asymmetry raking light gives, and the reason a pale wave must not be pushed paler even if a step existed — at pure white the groove would have one visible flank). The plaque also brings the bar LEVEL WITH the feed head box's two inner posts, `--indigo-8` since 08-06, so all four frieze edges on that surface are one object in four places. The plaque's own walk: `--indigo-1` (⇔ brown-1, 2026-07-25) → `--grey-4` (2026-08-05, the floating media windows' coat) → here; and before the inversion the waves were **`--indigo-4`** (lit) / **`--indigo-6`** (dark) — **TWO LEVELS APART**, where every setting from 2026-07-25 on had them one apart. That pair is the end of a four-step walk on 2026-08-05: `-3/-4` → `-4/-5` (*"stronger by 1"*) → `-5/-6` (*"one more tone"*) → **`-4/-6`** (the light one back up alone), which required **adding `$indigo-5` (#5c6bc0) and `$indigo-6` (#3f51b5)** to a family that had stopped at -4. The first two moves are the tail of the same day's swap to a neutral plate: these tones were chosen to carve a groove on `--indigo-1`, where wave one sat at 1.19:1 on its own coat, and `--grey-4` is a whole hue away and reads flatter under them. **The third move is the one to learn from** — stepping both waves together held them one level apart the whole way, so all three settings had the same internal contrast and only got darker as a block (more ink on the plate, not more relief); opening the pair to two levels is what let the motif read in DEPTH. A frieze is two waves interleaved, not one wave in two shades, so **the SPREAD is the carve** and the spread is the dial to reach for. `-6` is the family's Material 500, its pure hue and the darkest thing on the plate short of the ink at `-8` — which is also the wall, since at `-7` wave and ink are neighbours and wave two starts reading as a written line. All of this costs the level-for-level tie to the crown strip's browns (which stop at -4), and that is the right thing to give up: the bar is a colorway of `FriezeHeader` in its **tone MAPPING** — plaque / wave one / wave two / lip — and every one of those four has left the browns' indices now. **Keep the mapping in step, not the numbers.** One relation genuinely differs as a result: the crown strip's waves are one level apart and these are two, so the vertical bars carve deeper than the horizontal ones by design. And the **lip = THE PLAQUE'S OWN TONE, so the bar draws NO inward line at all** (2026-08-06, user ask, last of four settings that day: `--indigo-6` → -5 → -4 → `--grey-4`) — and it TRAVELS with the plaque, so it is `--indigo-8` since 2026-08-07. Its inner edge is where the plate stops against the box. That **breaks** the header's rule that a lip is a wave tone, and the justification is the same ask's other half: the well's side padding went to `0`, so the post cards ran flush into the bar with their own border and a second line a pixel away read as a doubled edge — the lip lost its JOB, not an argument. A `3px` sliver came back a moment later ("just a little little") and `8px` on 2026-08-07, and the lip stayed plaque-toned through both: that gap does the separating. This note used to end *"if the cards are ever pushed further off the bars, the lip wants `--indigo-4`"* — they were, and it does NOT, because the plaque went `--indigo-8` in the same pass and a deep plate stopping against a pale field is the strongest edge on the surface. The lip's job comes back only if the plaque goes pale again. The history: it was `--indigo-3`, the wave-ONE tone, for the bar's whole life, then one afternoon at `--grey-6` and one evening at `--indigo-6`, wave TWO: when the plate went neutral that morning the lip went neutral with it, on the argument that a coloured hairline down the INSIDE of a piece of metal reads as trim rather than as where the metal stops. That argument was really about a lip left behind at -3 while the plate moved, and the wave walk answered it — with the waves at `-4`/`-6` the colorway's dark end is a tone of the plate's OWN motif rather than a leftover from the old coat, so the lip could come home, first onto the dark wave and the next day onto the lit one. What the round trip settled is the WEIGHT: a full-height hairline down BOTH sides of the box reads at -6 as a frame drawn around the feed and at -4 as where the bar's own material stops. (The media tabs strip's edges walked to the same level on the same day, which is the cross-check — `--indigo-4` is this colorway's line tone on a light field, whatever surface draws it. It does mean lip and wave one now name ONE tone, so the lit wave and the edge beside it no longer separate: re-check that first if the waves are ever walked again.) **BOTH long edges are FLAT since 2026-08-07** — the bar used to state them differently (OUTER = a rolled gradient facing the page, INNER = the plate itself). The motif survives every plaque swap on the same terms it always had — the waves need only sit a step or two off their plaque, in EITHER direction, for the carve to read as a groove — but "clears it" and "carves on it" turned out to be different bars, which is exactly what the wave step above went on to fix. That lip draws the feed container's SIDE BORDERS. The **container's background used to be the same plaque coat**, so box and frieze edges read as one continuous material — `--indigo-1` on both from 2026-07-25, `--grey-4` on both from 2026-08-05, when the bar went neutral in the morning (user ask) and the box, its head band and its scroll bed followed the same evening (second ask); for the length of one pass between those asks the rule was simply FALSE on screen, a neutral band edging an indigo box. **That rule is RETIRED as of 2026-08-07**: the plaque left a second time and the box did NOT follow, so the surface is `--indigo-8` bars around a `--grey-4` field. The bar is no longer trim on the box's own coat but a DARK FRAME standing on it — the relation the head box's inner posts have had since 08-06. Retired, not broken: a different and deliberate arrangement, not a rule stated in one place and contradicted in another. The rule covers the BOX only: the scroll bed went `--grey-3` on 2026-08-06 (user ask) and is not part of it — bar and container are the material, the bed is what lies inside, and the HEAD stopped being part of it later that day (`FeedHeadBox`, `--grey-3`: an object on the plate, whose four corners now flare INTO these bars through `MediaTabsBar`-style fillets, which is the one place the bar's inner edge is met by another box's line). **THE OUTER EDGE WAS A ROLLED PLAQUE from 2026-08-05 to 08-07, and is now FLAT** — *"remove that round effect… they look cylindrical"* (user ask), which is exactly the failure: the shading was right and described the WRONG SOLID. Two rolled edges on a 19px strip with the motif running between them reads as a **CYLINDER**, a rod standing beside the feed rather than a band lying on it. Both `::after` blocks are deleted (A's and B's), along with the `--lip-*::after` placement rules and `slim`'s `content: none` cancellation. If a roll is ever wanted again: take it from git, put it on ONE edge only, and re-tune the stops against the plaque of the day — they were measured over `--grey-4`. What it was really buying, SOFTNESS against the near-black page canvas (hence a silhouette running almost to black), the `--indigo-8` plaque of the same ask now gives for free; a pale plate is what needed the dissolve. The profile, for the record: an `::after` 8px wide on the edge **opposite the lip** — keyed off the same `--lip-*` class, so the roll is always on the bar's free side wherever it is placed — carrying two overlaid gradients and **no border at all**. A bullnose seen orthographically from above does not end in a line; the last pixels of its face are the surface turning away, and what the eye reads there is a run of tone. The profile is deliberately ASYMMETRIC, which is what separates it from a stripe: the fall OUTWARD from the crest is short and steep (~4.6px, `rgba(11,12,16,.9)` at the silhouette down to 0), the rise INWARD is long and gentle (a `rgba(255,255,255,.68)` crest at 4.6px settling to 0 at 8px). Both are WASHES, never opaque fills, so the carved waves keep running out through the roll and are shaded BY it — the engraving curves over the edge with the metal, which is the tell. **Tune it by reading back rendered pixels, not by eye**: a browser samples a gradient at pixel CENTRES, so the outermost column reads the stop at 0.5px, and a first setting that asked for `.88` at 0 put #474748 on screen — 47 levels above the near-black canvas, a visible step, the one thing a soft edge may not have. Holding the deep end nearly flat for the first 0.9px is what lands it. The measured ramp, canvas outward to plaque, is **24 → 46 → 100 → 157 → 215 → 243 → 237 → 230 → 224** (dissolve, climb, crest, settle) and mirrors exactly on variant B. It got there through two earlier passes the same day, both **inverted** (deep -8 plaque under light waves) and both since retired: first the limes (base auditioned lime-10 → 9 → 7 → 8 over lime-2 + lime-1 waves), then the teals at the same tone numbers. Re-hueing is a **two-file edit** — swap the three Quasar hexes in `_tokens.scss` and the var names in `FriezeBarVertical.vue` (+ its mirrored sibling `FriezeBarVerticalB.vue`, which shares every token) + `FeedPage.vue`; nothing else in the app reads them. Flows in-page like `FriezeBar` (no fixed positioning, no z-index, `height: 100%` of its parent, `pointer-events: none`). First placement: the LEFT edge of `FeedPage`'s feed container — its mirrored sibling `FriezeBarVerticalB` takes the right. **DIALS + `slim`, 2026-08-06** (for the feed head box's two inner posts): the plaque, both waves and the lip now resolve through `--frieze-bar-v-base` / `-wave-one` / `-wave-two` / `-lip` (and the thickness through `--frieze-bar-v-w`), defaulting to exactly what they were — the same contract `FriezeBar` has had since 2026-08-03, so a host recolours a bar with properties instead of a modifier. `slim` is the vertical reading of `FriezeBar`'s: HALF thickness, ONE wave (layer one is `v-if`'d out — `b` alone still tiles as a frieze, it carries the full-width centre rule, and two interlocking meanders in ~9.5px read as texture), mask scaled PAST the box at `117% auto` (the rotated form of `auto 117%`, so the file's two empty rows fall outside instead of eating a sixth of the band), and the carve halved to 0.5/0.3 with the rotated signs. There was a fifth: **the rolled outer edge was dropped here** — that profile was 8px of a 19px bar tuned for an edge facing the page canvas, and a slim bar's job is to stand INSIDE a box where neither long edge faces the page. The slim variant outlived it (the roll came off the full bar on 2026-08-07), so this block no longer cancels anything. `FriezeBarVerticalB` took every one of these in the same pass.  **REPAINTED FOUR TIMES ON 2026-08-07** (user asks), and only the PLATE is still indigo. Where it stands: base **`--indigo-8`**, **`--grey-5`** wave one, **`--brown-1`** wave two, OUTER side edge `--indigo-8` and the **LIP `--indigo-5`**. The outer edge travels with the plaque (a margin left at the old level becomes the line the device exists not to draw); the lip no longer does — the bar is ASYMMETRICAL as of the day's last ask, the lip being each bar's INNER-FACING edge (A stands left with `lip="right"`, B right with `lip="left"`) and getting its JOB back to match the feed head box's own `--indigo-5` bottom rim, so the surface has one stated tone for "the inner line of the chrome" across two objects. The `--lip-*` classes restate ONE side after the base rule drew both, winning on source order at equal specificity — which decided nothing for the day and a half the two tones matched. In order: the inversion (base `--indigo-8`, waves indigo-1/-3) → lit wave to `--grey-4` → base to `--indigo-9` + dark wave to `--brown-1`, meant to put the feed container's bars on exactly the plate and warm motif the head box's inner posts already wore → wave one to `--grey-5` → **base BACK to `--indigo-8`**. The round trip settled the point: the argument for -9 was to match those posts, and THE POSTS ARE `--indigo-8`, so -8 is the level that lands all four frieze plates together and -9 was a step away from what it was chosen to match. **That last step turned the mapping over**: Quasar's `brown-1` (#efebe9) is lighter than `grey-4` (#e0e0e0), so wave TWO had silently become the lit face and the pair had collapsed to ~1.1:1 with its names inverted; a step down restores ~1.6:1 and states it honestly. The layer names are MASK FILES (`a`/`b`), not roles — "wave one is the lit face", true since the bar was built, is no longer true. **SIDE BORDERS** in the same batch: 1px of the plaque's own tone down each long edge, drawing no line and reserving a dark margin the `99%` mask cannot reach, which keeps a now-pale motif off the silhouette; `border-box`, so it costs the motif field 10% on a full bar — **and 21% on a `slim` one, which is why `slim` CANCELS it** (`--frieze-bar-v-edge-w: 0`, the fifth thing that variant drops after layer one, the roll, the mask fit and the carve offsets). It had taken the feed head box's posts from 7.5px of motif to 5.5px and their masks from ~0.68 to ~0.50 px per row, under the ~0.7 floor — reported as "thinned horribly … the pattern looks distorted", which is exactly what that number predicts. `slim`'s width became diallable in the same pass (**`--frieze-bar-v-slim-w`**, default `--frieze-h / 2`) because a host CANNOT safely restate `--frieze-bar-v-w`: the component's own slim rule sets that property on the same element, and two scoped rules of equal specificity are decided by bundle order. |
| `layout/FriezeBarVerticalB.vue` | **Variant B of the vertical frieze bar** (end of 2026-07-25) — `FriezeBarVertical` with the wave pattern **MIRRORED HORIZONTALLY** and *nothing else changed*, so a pair framing a box reflect each other across its centre line instead of repeating. A goes on the LEFT edge, B on the RIGHT (`FeedPage`'s feed container). Identical to A in thickness (`width: var(--frieze-h)`), palette (INVERTED on 2026-08-07 and re-toned again the same day: `--indigo-8` plaque and lip, a `--grey-4` LIT wave over an `--indigo-3` dark one — the lit face left the colorway on the day's second ask, so the pair is told apart by hue as well as by value; it read `--grey-4` plaque + `--indigo-4`/`--indigo-6` waves from 2026-08-05 until then — see A), tiling (`repeat-y` at `99% auto`, `center top`), `0 1px` padding, and the same `lip` prop contract (`left` default; B's placement wants `lip="left"`), so the two are drop-in siblings. Two deliberate NON-changes: (1) **the carve does not flip** — B keeps A's dark up-right / light down-left drop-shadows, because a scene has one light source and mirroring it would read as a second sun on the box's right-hand side; this is the same call `FriezeBar` makes, masking with the `-mirror` waves while keeping `FriezeHeader`'s own carve. (2) **the mirror is baked into the ASSETS, not a CSS transform** — `assets/frieze/mercury-wave-{a,b}-rot90-mirror.svg`, the rot90 exports wrapped in one more `translate(143 0) scale(-1 1)` group (composite: source `(x,y) → (y,x)`, canvas unchanged at 143×231). A `transform: scaleX(-1)` on the layer would have mirrored the drop-shadow carve along with the mask, which is exactly what (1) rules out — and it is why the repo already keeps a `-mirror` pair for the horizontal bar. **B also carries A's outer-edge ROLL** (2026-08-05) — the same 8px `::after`, the same stops, keyed off the same `--lip-*` class, so on B's usual `lip="left"` it lands on the box's right-hand OUTSIDE and the pair reads as one plaque turning over at both long edges. That element **is** mirrored with `transform: scaleX(-1)`, and it is the ONE place in the frieze family where that is allowed: the rule in (2) exists because a `scaleX` flips a layer's drop-shadow carve, and the roll has no drop-shadow — only two gradients, whose direction *should* flip, because the edge they describe has flipped. Re-hueing B is part of the same two-file edit as A. |
| `layout/RgbHairline.vue` | ⭐ **The RGB hairline — a divider drawn as a VERTICAL SANDWICH** (2026-08-07, user ask): a thick grey rule, a gradient band, a thick grey rule, `2px + 2px + 2px = 6px` in all. It stands BESIDE the frieze family rather than inside it, and the distinction is the reason it exists: a frieze band is a MOTIF carved into a plaque and needs height to read (~0.8px per motif row is the floor — see `FriezeBar`'s `slim` and [specs/gotchas.md](../../specs/gotchas.md)), while three flat lines hold their reading at any size, so the divider closest to a reading area can be the thin one. The filling is the only HUE in the rule — a `--cyan-11` → `--indigo-11` ramp running DOWN it, so the divider states a direction as well as a division; both are their family's **A100**, the third accent at that index in service (with `--teal-11`), because a mark made of two hues has to be built from ONE index across two families or the ramp reads as bright fading to dull. The bread is `--grey-7`, a step UNDER the host's ordinary hairlines (a compound rule is read as one object, so its own lines should not be mistaken for two of the host's) — on the feed it sits one step above the frieze band's `--grey-8` plaque and one under the `--grey-6` every LINE on that card is drawn in — the card's two dividers are a plate group of their own, which is what lets both stand out of a box whose lines never move. **Four dials**, in the frieze family's idiom (`var(--x, default)` inline, never a default declared on the element — a host class and a component rule on the SAME element are settled by source order, which is not a contract): `--rgb-hairline-edge` / `-edge-h` / `--rgb-hairline-fill` / `-fill-h`. Reversing the ramp is a re-stated `--rgb-hairline-fill` (CSS cannot read a gradient backwards, so there is nothing to flip). Two implementation notes worth keeping: **`background-clip: padding-box`** is load-bearing — a background paints under the BORDER box by default, so the ramp would run over all 6px and the filling would show only its middle slice, two muddy blues instead of cyan → indigo; and **`flex: 0 0 auto` lives in the component**, because every host is a column with a ceiling somewhere and the failure is silent (the rule survives as a coloured line and nobody reads it as squashed). Consumer: the post card's pit↔foot divider (`posts/FeedStream.vue`), which dials nothing — the defaults ARE that card's. ⚠ **It is a ONE-INSTANCE consumer again since 2026-08-10**: the card carried three for part of that day (a thin `--rail` pair bracketing the label strip, the lower one `--flip`ped) and the day's last ask deleted the pair with both modifiers when the strip took a rim of its own. Nothing on the platform sets `--rgb-hairline-edge-h` or restates `--rgb-hairline-fill` today — the dials are still the contract, they just have no live user. |

The strip stays a lifted sibling because the footer was a z-2600 stacking context and the band was opaque at 3000, so nothing inside the footer could paint over it (the footer is 3110 now and the band is retired, but the strip's own 3045 is what keeps parked tabs clickable over every expanded dock (3010+) while staying under the drawer (3050)). (From 2026-07-25 until then the strip sat INSIDE the footer on the band's top edge — `bottom: calc(100% + var(--frieze-h))`; before that on the bar's own top edge until that opaque band moved in, which would have swallowed every parked tab whole) (teal edit_note = maker, purple upload = uploader, slate architecture = schema builder); the stack/pins side bars **narrow to thin icon columns** on the right edge instead (each is ONE element with two presentations, `is-parked` ⇄ expanded panel at the same screen edge — 2026-07-24 third pass, the separate rail tree is gone). `.nav-bar` is exactly `var(--nav-bar-h)` (**32px** since 2026-08-02) tall — **its own ROW, not the whole footer**: since 2026-08-02 a **FLOOR FRIEZE BAND** stands under it inside the same `q-footer` (user ask, "a dark brown frieze bar below the nav footer bar, the ones from inside the left drawer") — and since **2026-08-04 it wears the crown strip's own PALE palette** (`.nav-floor-frieze`: `--frieze-bar-base` brown-1 + `--frieze-bar-wave-{one,two}` brown-3/brown-4, the same dial seam the chat dock's green band uses; it wore the drawer's dark brown-4/-2/-1 recipe for its first two days, when `FriezeFooter` riding this bar's top edge was the pale mirror and the page was closed by two bands — that band is deleted and **this is the page's one closing band now**). It is mounted INSIDE the footer rather than as a fixed sibling because the footer is what Quasar measures to pad the page, so the page's bottom inset grows with it and nothing can slide beneath the strip. `--nav-footer-h` is therefore the TOTAL (`calc(var(--nav-bar-h) + var(--frieze-h))`) and keeps its exact old MEANING — how far up from the window floor the chrome reaches, still this bar's TOP edge — so **nothing anchored to it needed touching**; only `.nav-bar` itself, which wants the row. The drawer and the pinned column run PAST this footer to the window floor and so repeat the band themselves (`.drawer-floor-frieze`, `.pins-floor-frieze`), making the line unbroken edge to edge. The **docks and the minitab strip**, which sit at `bottom: var(--nav-footer-h)`, merge with its top edge — the pinned column and the left drawer no longer stop there at all, both running past it to `bottom: 0`, owning the floor band's pixels at the two ends and repeating it themselves (`.drawer-floor-frieze`, `.pins-floor-frieze`) so the line runs unbroken. (`--dock-bottom`, the token that once stood bottom docks one `--frieze-h` higher on the retired `FriezeFooter` band, is gone since 2026-08-04.) The pins widget stood on the bar's top edge (`bottom: var(--nav-footer-h)`) in **both** presentations until 2026-08-02; it runs to `bottom: 0` at z 3120 now, lying OVER the bar's last 42px and carrying the tack itself, and the bar reserves that width (`.nav-right--railed`) so it still covers no buttons. The **minitab strip** shares that band, so it shifts left by `windows.footerPanelInset` (`PANEL_W` when the pins panel is expanded at the screen edge, `RAIL_W` when parked) so parked maker/uploader tabs stay clear of the pins column. **DASHBOARD BUTTON (2026-08-10, user ask)** — a `sym_o_empty_dashboard` pebble in its OWN section of the right cluster, hairlined on both sides, between the chat button and the blue create bundle: chat is where entities meet, the bundle is where elements are made, and this opens a box that only LOOKS at the platform, so it is a third thing rather than a pale member of either group. **The one GREY control on this bar** — a `--grey-3` face inside a `--grey-4` rim with `--grey-9` ink, which is exactly the plaque-and-border pair of the flyout it opens (active/hover step the face to `--grey-4` and the rim to `--grey-6`: the pressed state of a grey object is a darker grey, not an accent). It reads `stores/dashboard.js` STRAIGHT, exactly as the chat button reads its own store, and toggles in the same three steps (closed → open, parked → restore, standing → close). Its flyout pass took a `dashboardOpen` prop and emitted `toggle-dashboard`; both are gone — the panel became a dock that rises from this bar and parks on it, so the bar is one of the two surfaces that OWN it and has no business being told about it from above. **Its minitab lives in `parkedTabs` beside chat's** (2026-08-10, user ask: "for the dashboard, make it a tab on the bottom nav footer bar") — the strip was already the parking lot for every window rising from this bar, so the destination cost one entry and a tint, `.minitab__icon--dashboard` at `#424242`, the only grey mark in a row of teal/violet/slate ones and the same ink the pebble four pixels above it letters its glyph in. ⚠ `sym_o_` prefixed: `empty_dashboard` exists only in Material SYMBOLS and an unmatched ligature renders as the literal word (specs/gotchas.md). Measured at 375px: search 104–142, chat 146–176, dashboard 180–210, bundle 214–334, tack 334–375 — nothing overlaps and ~104px of slack remains. |
| `layout/StackPanel.vue` | The navigation stack — a docked right side widget that hangs from the screen's **very top-right corner** (`top: 0`, 2026-07-24 — **over** the crown strip, which its z 3100 already outranked; it sat below the strip at `top: var(--frieze-h)` for part of that day) and grows downward toward the middle (mirror of `PinsDrawer`), wearing **TWO mirrored `FriezeBar` bands that BRACKET its scroll well** (`flex-shrink: 0`, both `--frieze-h` = 18.9px @900 since the 2026-08-02 thinning — 27px before it — both rendered in **both presentations** — at rail width they show a small wave slice; **both carry `flip` since 2026-08-02**, so this column's meander runs opposite the left drawer's — see the FriezeBar row): `.stack-frieze` at the widget's own **top edge** and `.stack-frieze--inner` between the **well and the header/head glyph** at the bottom (2026-07-24 — the mirror of the one band `PinsDrawer` carries). Only the top band **stands in for** the crown strip across the widget's column rather than dividing the list from it, so it wears `FriezeHeader`'s **exact box**: `height: var(--frieze-h)` + the same `border-bottom: 1px solid var(--brown-3)` lip, which (border-box) trims the same 1px off the carve area so both motifs draw at an identical size and the band doesn't step at the widget's left edge; the widget's own **`border-top` is dropped** for the same reason — the band, not a brown-4 hairline, is what meets the screen edge (verified: band and strip both measure `top 0, h 27, border-bottom 1px, padding-top 1px`, parked AND expanded). Only the palette differs by design — the band keeps FriezeBar's inverted brown-4 base, like the left drawer's top band. The **inner** band takes no lip (plain `height: var(--frieze-h)`) — it stands in for nothing, so border-box resolves it to the very same 27px box and the two read as a matched pair; both run **full bleed** across the widget while the well between them keeps its margins. **ONE element with two presentations** (2026-07-24, third pass — the separate rail tree of the second pass is gone): expanded = the 300px panel, parked (`is-parked`, when the window is minimized) = the **same element narrowed to a thin icon column** (`--dock-rail-w`, 42px, shrink-to-fit height); the list is the **one and only item scroller** — the same DOM node in both presentations, so the **scroll position carries through the park/expand morph** (carried as a ratio — the item faces differ in height), and the shared `.dock-window` width transition animates it. The `.dock-bar` **info box** sits at the **bottom** of the expanded panel (below the list): layers icon + title left-aligned and **both in `--brown-8`** (2026-07-24, 8th pass — one consistent chrome ink; the old `.dock-bar__icon--stack` ink-2 tint is deleted), the step count meta in the lighter **`--brown-4`** at the right, and a **`.dock-bar__action` history button** (flat brown-8 `history` clock glyph, tooltip "Navigation stack history") at the very right end — `@click.stop` so it doesn't park the widget through the bar; **its destination landed 2026-07-31 (Thread H)**: it routes to the user's NAVIGATION skeleton viewer (`GET /nav/skeleton` → `/#/skeletons/:id`) — the on-chain element whose PATH_REF path IS the visit history. The **amber traffic-light dot is gone** (2026-07-24 7th pass — the whole bar is the park control now (`.dock-bar--park`), which was only ever a touch affordance since hover parks the widget on the way out; it shares the brown-1 coat with **no top/bottom border/hairline** so it merges into the list body above it); parked, the **head glyph** (`.dock-side-head` — a **brown-8** icon sitting flat on the plaque, 20px and zero-padded since the 8th pass so it fills its band right under the well; it was a tiny brown-4 mark before) takes that bottom spot, **exactly `--dock-bar-h` (30px) tall like the header it replaces**, so the list box below it keeps its offset across presentations. The list runs **oldest→newest top-to-bottom** (the natural history order — oldest at the top, **newest at the bottom just above the header/head**) with the scroll pinned to the **bottom** (newest) in both presentations. The list (`.stack-list`) is an **inset brown-2 scroll well** (2026-07-24 — the floor spent part of that day at brown-3 and came **back to `--brown-2`**, reading as a soft recess under the brown-1 plaque rather than a dark trough; the rim stayed brown-4): **`--brown-2`** background behind the rows (and behind the scrollbar track), margin all around (brown-1 reveal of the plaque — **8px against each frieze band**, 6px on the sides, since a carved wave needs more air than a flat plaque edge; the numbers are identical in both faces or the unravel stops landing in place), a **1px `--brown-4` border** rimming the box — the same ink as the widget's own outer border — and **`--radius-md` rounded corners** that also clip the scrolling rows; its own `is-parked` modifier narrows it to the column (centered chips, hidden scrollbar — the vertical padding and `gap` are shared with the expanded face). Expanded: slim fully-rounded scrollbar (**brown-2 pill track — matching the well floor so only the thumb reads — + brown-4 pill thumb**, webkit-pseudo-only — see the Chrome 121+ scrollbar gotcha; `overflow-x: hidden` — rows ellipsize, never a horizontal bar). The panel **shrinks to fit its step bundle in BOTH presentations** (2026-07-24, 7th pass — expanded used to take its full cap as a FIXED height with the steps bottom-aligned via `margin-top:auto`, which left a dead field between the frieze band and the items): it is only as tall as its steps need and stops growing at **`--dock-stack-h` (63vh)**, where the list scrolls instead — the stack's **70% share** of the 90vh side band it splits with the pins widget's 30% (`--dock-pins-h`, 27vh; both were an even 45vh until 2026-07-24 — the history is the long fast-growing list of the pair, so it took the lion's share). Verified at 900px tall: cap 567px, well `top 33 → bottom 506`, bands at `top 0` and `top 509`, pixel-identical parked and expanded. Both lists are **flex columns in both presentations** with the shared `gap: var(--side-item-gap)` — never block flow, whose margins would collapse where flex gaps do not (that mismatch is exactly what threw the two faces off each other's rhythm). The mid-gap to the pins widget below is preserved by the 70/30 split adding up to the same 90vh (measured 42px at 900px tall — the two never touch). Flat brown-1 plaque (`--brown-1`, header `.dock-bar` included — one uniform brown-1 coat) with a **thin classic 1px Quasar brown-4 outer border** (`--brown-4`, 2026-07-24 — the same ink as the well's rim inside it, so widget and well are drawn with one line over the brown-2 floor) on its **left and bottom** edges in both presentations — the **top is bare** since the widget moved to `top: 0` (its frieze band, wearing the crown strip's own brown-3 lip, is what meets the screen edge there); its right edge sits bare at the very **screen edge** (`right: 0`). Beside the downward drop shadow it also casts the shared **`--shadow-side-edge`** off its **left** edge (2026-07-24) — a slight leftward shadow with no vertical offset, in **both** presentations (the rule is state-agnostic), matching the pins widget below and the nav bar's `.tack-slot` so the whole right-edge column reads as one raised strip lying on the page. Presentation is **hover-driven** (2026-07-24 — the `.dock-side-tab` folder handle is gone): resting the pointer anywhere on the parked column **expands** the panel after a **150ms intent delay** (so a pointer flung at the screen edge doesn't pop it open), and **mouse-leave parks it back**; the head glyph and the header bar itself keep their taps for touch screens, where hover doesn't exist. **Minimize-only — no close** (it has no nav-bar opener, so it docks as a permanent top-edge fixture, defaults to parked, and can only park, never be dismissed). The crown strip runs on unbroken to the LEFT of the widget (the stack covers only its own column), so nothing needs to inset for it. Each entry is the **ONE mutating side-bar item** (`layout/SidePanelItem.vue`, shared with the pins widget, 2026-07-24), flipped by the widget's presentation. Expanded face: `MicroChip` + remembered real title + "visited x ago" + a minimal "by <username>" author line (resolved once per element step via `refs/summary`, expanded only), with the square kind button **palette-inverted** (solid kind-color fill, brown-1 icon) on the right and the checkpoint flag on the badges slot; the step you are **at** wraps block + button together in a **kind-colored bubble** (soft accent fill + accent rim, rounded rectangle). Parked face: a flat rounded **Quasar button** (32px wide × `--side-item-h` tall — a vertical key, not a square, since the parked column only clears ~35px of well (28px until the 8th pass tightened the well's side margins)), tinted with the step's own system color (page steps fall back to ink) on its outline + icon over a brown-1 face; the current step (`.is-current`) inverts to a solid chip filled with that color + a brown-1 icon. **Both faces are exactly `--side-item-h` tall**, so a step unravels at the very level its parked chip sat on (see `SidePanelItem`). The stack layers its own tints on both faces (`is-past` fades, `is-future` italic-soft, checkpoint titles coral — expanded only, the chip has no title). Click-to-jump routes through `navStore.jumpTo` (return halo fires); an expanded row also parks the widget so the destination lands in full view, while a parked chip jumps without changing the presentation. |
| `layout/PinsDrawer.vue` | Pinned list — a docked right side widget that **RUNS TO THE WINDOW FLOOR OVER the nav bar** (**`bottom: 0`, z 3120** since 2026-08-02, user ask: "make sure the pin button is contained inside it and that the sidebar is on top of the footer" — it stopped at the bar's top edge at `bottom: var(--nav-footer-h)`, z 3100 before, and 3120 is what clears the bar's own 3110; it is the left drawer's number, the other column that deliberately covers an end of the bar, and the two never overlap). The **amber traffic-light dot is gone** (2026-07-24 7th pass — the whole bar is the park control now (`.dock-bar--park`), a touch affordance only, since hover parks the widget on the way out; it shares the brown-1 coat with **no bottom border/hairline** so it merges into the list body); parked, the **head glyph** (`.dock-side-head` — a **brown-8** icon sitting flat on the plaque, 20px and zero-padded since the 8th pass so it fills its band right under the well; it was a tiny brown-4 mark before) takes that top spot, **exactly `--dock-bar-h` (30px) tall like the header it replaces**, so the list box keeps its offset across presentations.
