Capped at `--dock-pins-h` (**27vh** — the pinned set's **30% share** of the 90vh side band, against the stack's 70%; both were an even 45vh until 2026-07-24, and a short curated list needs far less room than the history), shrink-to-fit, its list scrolling inside the cap. Verified at 900px tall after the 2026-08-02 move: `top 625 → bottom 900` (the window floor), the `.pins-footer` row `868 → 900` = exactly the 32px bar's box, tack chip 24px centred at x 1407.5…1431.5 inside the 42px column, pixel-identical parked and expanded. Same flat brown-1 plaque (header `.dock-bar` included) with a **thin classic 1px brown-4 outer border** (`--brown-4`, 2026-07-24 — the same ink as the well's rim inside it) on the top and left edges only in both presentations; the bottom AND right sit bare — the widget is at the very **screen edge** (`right: 0`). Beside the upward drop shadow it also casts the shared **`--shadow-side-edge`** off its **left** edge (2026-07-24) — a slight leftward shadow with no vertical offset, in **both** presentations (the rule is state-agnostic), matching the stack widget above and the nav bar's `.tack-slot` right below, so the column reads as one raised strip from the screen's top corner down through the footer. Presentation is **hover-driven** like the stack's (2026-07-24 — the `.dock-side-tab` folder handle is gone): hovering the parked column expands the panel after a 150ms intent delay, mouse-leave parks it back; the head glyph and the header bar itself keep their taps for touch screens. **Minimize-only** (no close — with no nav-bar toggle left, a full close would strand it) and it **defaults to** parked. The widget covers no bar buttons in either presentation (it lies over the bar's right end, and `.nav-right--railed` reserves that width so every control stays clear). Each pin is the **ONE mutating side-bar item** (`layout/SidePanelItem.vue`, shared with the stack widget, 2026-07-24 — the per-pin `ElementMini` cards are gone), flipped by the widget's presentation. Expanded face: micro chip + summary title + "pinned x ago" (the API's `pinned_at`, now the pin moment's `time_utc`) + "by <username>" (the summary's `author`), the square kind button **palette-inverted** on the right, and hover-revealed copy-`[[pathos:…]]` + unpin micro-buttons on the actions slot; the pin you are **currently viewing** wraps in the **kind-colored bubble**. Parked face: a flat rounded **Quasar button** (32px wide × `--side-item-h` tall — a vertical key, sized so the chips keep just a hair of padding inside the well) tinted with the element's own **system color** (from `kinds.js`) on its **outline + icon** over a brown-1 face; the current pin inverts to a **solid chip filled with that color** + a brown-1 icon, so "you are here" reads as solid-vs-outlined while everything stays flat. A cheap reactive route match (`isCurrent`, no extra request) against the loaded pin rows drives the state. Activating routes to the element's real viewer via a cached `refService.summary` lookup (a pinned post's `target_type` is `skeleton`, but its summary routes to `/posts/:id`); an expanded row also parks the widget so the destination lands in full view (a parked chip keeps the presentation; with no resolvable route it expands the panel instead). Reloads on the `refresh-key` prop so tack pins/unpins show up live. |
| `layout/SidePanelItem.vue` | ⭐ The **ONE mutating stack/pins side-bar item** (2026-07-24) — fuses the old expanded-row and collapsed-rail-chip renders into a single per-kind component. `collapsed` prop picks the face (driven by the host widget's `is-parked` presentation since the 2026-07-24 third pass): **parked chip** (flat rounded `q-btn`, **32px wide × `--side-item-h` tall** — a vertical key rather than a square, because the parked column (`--dock-rail-w`, 42px) only clears ~35px of well while the height has to match the row's; kind color from `kinds.js` on outline + icon, `current` inverts to solid fill + brown-1 icon, tooltip) or **informative row** (`MicroChip` — plain type icon for kind-less page steps — · title if available · time-ago · "by <username>", with the same button palette-inverted on the right at 26px; `current` wraps the whole row in a **kind-colored bubble**: rounded rectangle, soft `rgba(accent, .14)` fill + accent rim, derived from the kind's hex accent in-component). **Both faces are locked to `--side-item-h` (36px)** (2026-07-24, 7th pass — they were 28px vs ~45px, so a list re-flowed as it unravelled): the row is `height: --side-item-h` with `box-sizing: border-box` and its **text compacted to fit** (head line `line-height: 1` around the 18px chip, 1px info gap, `.side-item__meta` at `0.64em`/`line-height: 1` ≈ 9px — 28px of content inside 3px padding + 1px border) while the chip was **grown** to meet it. Items carry **no vertical margins** — the host list's `gap: var(--side-item-gap)` (4px) spaces them in both faces, since flex gaps don't collapse the way block-flow margins do. Net effect: item *N* occupies the identical band parked or expanded (verified Δtop = 0 across every row of both widgets). `badges` slot (stack's checkpoint flag) + `actions` slot (pins' copy/unpin) let each panel keep its extras; emits `activate` — the row/chip's single click surface (the chip is deliberately non-routing here). |
| `layout/navTypeIcons.js` | Nav-entry type → icon / MicroChip-kind maps. Read by `StackPanel` (history rows); `NavigationBar` dropped its import with the current-title readout on 2026-08-02. |
| (left drawer, in `MainLayout.vue`) | Runs from the screen's **very top-left corner**, OVER the fixed crown strip (2026-07-24): `aside.q-drawer` is raised over the frieze (3000) and its `padding-top: var(--frieze-h)` is gone — the pinned `.drawer-frieze--top` band supplies the strip in that band's place. **Since 2026-08-02 it does the same at the bottom** (user ask): **`bottom: 0` at z 3120**, the full window height, lying OVER the nav bar (3110) instead of stopping at its top edge — safe because the bar's left cluster is empty whenever the drawer stands (the burger is `v-if`'d out of `.nav-left` and drawn by the drawer's own `.drawer-footer` instead). The content is a flex column: pinned band + `.drawer-scroll` taking the rest (`min-height: 0`) + the pinned **`.drawer-footer`** — the nav bar's row rebuilt inside the drawer (brown-1 plaque, brown-3 `border-top` on the bar's own pixel, 41px brown-4 `.drawer-burger-slot` + 1px hairline, the 24px inverted chip at the same x the bar's burger takes), whose chip only CLOSES the drawer. In the 42px mini state the content box is 41px so the hairline falls outside it (`overflow: hidden`) and the drawer's brown-3 `border-right` stands in — same ink, same pixel. Quasar's modal `.q-drawer__backdrop` rides at **3115** (over the bar, under the drawer) so a phone's dimmed page doesn't leave the bar lit and clickable. Flat brown-1 mercury plaque like the other chrome surfaces; item icons, labels and the EXPLORE/IDENTITY section headers are all `--brown-8` ink (2026-07-24), the active route stays the inverted smoke chip with `--cake-lemon` text, and the pinned-top Back button is the inverted brown chip — solid `--brown-8` fill with `--brown-1` icon/label (hover lifts to Quasar brown-7 `#5D4037`). **Styled from an unscoped block** — the `pathos-drawer` class lands on Quasar's `.q-drawer__content`, which doesn't carry the SFC scope attribute (see specs/gotchas.md). Routes can opt out entirely with `meta: { hideDrawer: true }` (currently only `/mercury-playground`) — the layout `v-if`s the drawer away on those routes. |
| `media/MediaViewerHost.vue` | **The floating media viewer mount** (2026-08-04, `docs/plans/floating-media-viewer.md`) — MainLayout mounts it once; it renders every live `MediaViewerWindow` (`v-show`, not `v-if`: a parked video keeps playing through minimize/restore) + `MediaTabsBar` while ≥1 viewer is parked. `display: contents` — every child is `position: fixed`. |
| `media/MediaViewerWindow.vue` | ⭐ **One floating media viewer** — the rounded box born in the arena and free to roam the screen: a SINGLE `--grey-4` coat inside a soft `--grey-6` rim, header included, house `.dock-bar` with the docks' traffic lights (red close / yellow minimize / green fullscreen) left, centred **kind glyph + file name** as one group (`--grey-9` ink over `--grey-8` glyph, set in `--font-display`/Nasalization at 0.72em — the wide face buys back with a smaller size what it spends on width, and the ellipsis lives on the label so the glyph can never be what gets truncated; both the name and the glyph come from `utils/mediaKind.js` — `titleOf` and `iconFor`, the same two the parked tab reads, so a window is its tab at full size and web media reads `PROVIDER :: name`) — the header carries nothing else now (the action cluster moved to the foot, 2026-08-05 user ask) — the side padding that used to mirror that cluster went ASYMMETRIC to match (2026-08-05, second user ask that day: the title was clipping), `70px` on the left (just clears the traffic-light cluster) and `12px` on the right, so the title gets the rest of the bar instead of being boxed into a padding sized for an icon cluster that isn't there anymore; under the header the flyout's band on a `--frieze-bar-base: var(--grey-8)` plaque with `--frieze-bar-wave-two: var(--brown-1)` (both stated rather than defaulted); then the well (4px pad, `container-type: size`) holding `MediaViewerBody`, whose `.mv-body` draws **nothing of its own** — no rim, no floor, the `--grey-4` coat straight through, so the MEDIA is the only thing in the window that is not the coat and the only edges left are the window's rim and the frieze band. It got there by elimination over five user asks the same day: `--grey-8` rim → `--grey-6` rim → `--grey-4` floor + `--grey-5` rim → a solid topless `--grey-5` plate → uniform. Each pass was smoother than the last and the end of that line is no step at all ("uniform look"); the caption ink went `--grey-9` → `--grey-10` while the floor was dark and back to `--grey-9`; and last the **foot**, a 22px ledge (grown from 18px, 2026-08-05) carrying the **action cluster** (`--grey-8`, `--grey-10` on hover) at its left — `adjust` (the drawer's own NODES glyph) → `/nodes/:id`, a 1px `--grey-6` hairline, then copy-link share, `content_copy` → the **pathchain path** (`nodes/<hash>`), and `push_pin` → a `pinService` toggle wearing `--accent` while held and emitting `pins-changed` up through the host to MainLayout's `pinsRefreshKey` (moved down off the header and share swapped with the node-page button, both 2026-08-05 user asks) — and the node's tally at its right end — flat glyph + count in `--font-display`, and unlike NodeMini's read-only pair these VOTE (`nodeInteractionService`, the node page's own toggle rule, `--positive`/`--negative` ink on your own vote, `navStore.recordAction` so it lands in the pathchain like any other). `FOOT_H` in `utils/mediaFit.js` keeps the fit engine in step with the ledge: a window sized by the old constants clips its media by exactly the height of what was added (a `BODY_RIM` lived there too while the content box had a rim). **The tones moved THREE TIMES on 2026-08-05** (three user asks). First the header's darker `--grey-10` cap was dropped and its tone pushed down into the band, turning two stacked plates into one `--grey-9` field with a single dark line across it. Then the coat crossed to the LIGHT half of the scale — `--grey-4` (#e0e0e0) — and every light-on-dark decision in the window inverted with it: the name, the share glyph and the body's embed caption are dark neutrals now. Last, the RIM followed: `--grey-8` was a half-step line while the coat was dark and a 3.2:1 black outline on the light one — the darkest mark on the window, drawn round the outside, competing with the band it is supposed to sit under — so the border went to a newly declared **`--grey-6`** (#9e9e9e, 1.9:1 on the coat), an edge one shade off the face rather than an outline painted on it. That is what the user's "bold smooth metallic" ask comes to: brushed metal has no outline, and the drop shadow is what separates the box from the page, so the rim only has to say where the metal stops. The band kept its job through all four states: one dark strip, warm wave, doing alone what a coat change used to do. Geometry = inline style off `viewer.rect`; spawn placement = `probeNaturalSize` → `fitRect` (maximize the binding dimension in the arena, cascade by index); an **arena watcher re-clamps stale rects** on viewport/rotate. `is-max` = true fullscreen (100vw/100vh, **z 3200** over the drawer's 3120; normal z = `windows.zOf('media:<id>')`, interleaving with the docks) with a ~240ms geometry ease that never animates spawns or gestures. Trigger: **NodeMini's header corner button** (the old decorative `open_in_new` glyph, now `open_in_full`) → `mediaViewers.spawn(node)` on every NodeMini surface — feed-card block embeds, the flyout, LabelUsages, FileTreeNode, path/link viewers. |
| `media/MediaViewerBody.vue` | The viewer's **per-kind face** (one `face` computed): embed → `EmbedFrame` with the caption-corrected height budget (`--media-max-h: calc(100cqh − 22px)` — the caption line would otherwise overflow the well by exactly its own height) and the caption recolored light for the dark floor (player mode centres box + caption; page mode fills); image/video contained + centred (a ≤300px-long-side image renders at natural size instead of stretching); audio = 90% centred bar; **markdown = a light `--grey-1` reading pane** rendering `MarkdownBody :plain-refs :breaks="false"` under the global `.md-rendered` skin, with a tiny mono corner chip flipping **raw ⇄ pretty** (raw = verbatim `<pre>`); non-md text and bare NOTEs = the raw pane; binary/url-less/over-1MB-text = a centred download card (kind glyph, name, `.ext · size`, download link). |
| `media/MediaTabsBar.vue` | The **minimize band** — a `--grey-4` strip **`--media-tabs-band`** tall (`--frieze-h`/5) under a 1px `--indigo-4` rim, standing ABOVE the crown strip, z 3105 (above the stack's 3100, below the nav footer's 3110), `pointer-events: none` so the paint never deadens what's under it; one rounded-bottom **name tab** per parked viewer hangs from it, click = restore; TransitionGroup slides tabs out of/into the band. **ALWAYS MOUNTED** (2026-08-05, second pass, user ask) — it is the rail the viewers hang from, so it stands there empty as readily as full; both layouts mount it (MainLayout via `MediaViewerHost`, AuthLayout directly, since the space is reserved on every page and an unmounted band would float the frieze on nothing). **It reserves SPACE rather than painting over the strip** (same day, first pass — laid on the crown's top fifth, it cut the frieze motif off mid-carve across the whole width): `--media-tabs-h` (face + rim) is a constant in `_tokens.scss`, and FriezeHeader, both page containers, `aside.q-drawer`, `.stack-window` and `.dock-window--creation` all offset by it — that is the complete list, and anything new pinned to the window's top must join it or it will stand out of line with the crown. A constant, not a runtime claim: the first pass had this component set the token on mount and clear it on unmount, which worked and hopped the whole page 4px on every park. The arena's ceiling became the crown's real `getBoundingClientRect().bottom` rather than its height. The **rim is the tabs' own border continued across the window** — the tabs hang from `top: 100%` (the face's underside, `border-box` + the `+1px`), so each tab and its flares paint over it and the line breaks exactly where metal meets metal. **The tabs are the window's material** — `--grey-4` face, `.nasalization` (`--font-display`) — but only the FACE is neutral since **2026-08-06** (user ask): every EDGE on the strip is `--indigo-4` (the band's rim, the tab's border and the rim stops inside both flare gradients — one continuous line, so the three always move together) and every MARK is `--indigo-10`, a level ADDED to the family for this (Material 900, a step past the colorway's -8 ink, because this writing sits on a NEUTRAL face rather than the feed cards' own coat, which gives a tone less help; the glyph gave up its `--grey-8` step-under-the-ink and now matches the name exactly). They were `--grey-6` rim / `--grey-9` ink before. The edge was asked for at `--indigo-6` — the feed container's lip — and walked UP through -5 to -4 in one sitting: a short lip read against the feed's own coat can sit at the motif's dark wave, but 1px of hue laid the full width of a light band at the very top of the screen RULES the page there and merely states the colorway at the lit wave. Note the two halves moved in OPPOSITE directions from where the ask started, the line toward the light end and the writing past the ink; that spread is what keeps a 1px rim and a 12px word both legible on one light face. Each tab flares out of the band through a pair of **concave fillets**: 9px radial-gradient corners (an inverted radius has no `border-radius` spelling) that overlap 1px INTO the tab so the side border's stub is covered, with two extra colour stops carrying the rim along the arc, which meets the tab's own border exactly where its tangent turns vertical. `--mtab-face` is a per-tab variable, because the gradients cannot inherit `background` and must follow the face through hover (`--grey-3`, +3px reach) and press (`--grey-5`, 1px dip). Glyph per media kind via `utils/mediaKind.js#iconFor` (page embeds `public` vs players `smart_display` — what restoring the tab gives you, not who hosts it), and its LABEL from the same module's `titleOf`, the one naming seam the window header also reads: web media is **`PROVIDER :: name`** in caps (2026-08-05, user ask) — "WIKIPEDIA :: Talavera pottery", "YOUTUBE :: 6gm9IWUuus0" — the caps sorting a strip of tabs by source at a glance and the name telling two tabs from the same site apart. The name is dug out of the URL (`webNameOf`: last non-routing path segment, `_`→space, `?v=` when the whole path routes), because the embed descriptor's `title` is the RULE's generic label ("YouTube video player") and would make every YouTube tab read the same; a node's own `title` outranks both. **The row fills right→left** and stops at **50vw**: `store.parked` reversed (newest leftmost, so every older tab keeps its slot and the newest is the end that stays visible), pinned by `margin-left: auto` on the first tab — NOT `justify-content: flex-end`, which strands overflowing content past the scroll container's start ([gotchas.md](../../specs/gotchas.md)) — tabs `flex: 0 1 auto` shrinking to a 46px icon floor, and only then a hidden-scrollbar horizontal scroller. **IT IS NOT ONLY THE VIEWERS' BAND SINCE 2026-08-10** (user ask): the feed's skeleton flyout parks here too, so the row is built from a NORMALIZED `{ key, icon, name, restore }` list instead of straight off `mediaViewers.parked` — each tab carries its own restore and the strip never learns what kind of window it is holding, which is what let a second family join it in six lines. The flyout leads the row (there is at most one of it, so a fixed slot at the growing end costs the viewers nothing and gives the one tab that can appear on any route a place that does not move under the pointer); media viewers follow, newest first. `aria-label` is "minimized windows" now, not "minimized media viewers". |
| `posts/PostCard.vue` | Card surface for a post |
| `posts/FeedHeadBox.vue` | ⭐ **The public feed's head, as a box you can move** (2026-08-06, user ask) — what `.feed-stream__head` became. **THREE changes and one addition.** (1) **It is grabbed by its own inner header.** The box opens with a `.feed-head__bar` — grip glyph pinned left, `Public Feed` centred in `nasalization` and `--indigo-10` (the tone the media tabs write in, since this chrome borrows their corners rather than the cards' ink) — and that bar is the ONLY drag surface, the division of labour every floating window here already makes (`.dock-bar`, `MediaViewerWindow`). `touch-action: none` on it is load-bearing: without it a phone claims the vertical gesture for a scroll and the pointer stream dies mid-drag. The bar is also `tabindex="0"` with **↑/↓ (12px), Home and End** — a box that can only be placed by dragging cannot be placed at all without a pointer. (2) **It slides up and down between the frieze bars.** `position: absolute` against a now-`relative` `.feed-stream-pane`, which IS the field between the two bars, so `left/right: 0` spans it lip to lip and the pane's own height is the travel — no measuring, no arena. Vertical only (there is no horizontal freedom to give), clamped to `EDGE = FLARE + 1 = 12px` off each end, which is exactly the daylight the corner sweeps need before `.feed-container__body`'s `overflow: hidden` would clip them — it must CLEAR the sweep's reach, so it moved with the fillets when they grew. Bounds are re-measured per gesture and by a `ResizeObserver` on the box AND its parent, so a restored offset that is illegal at the current window size is pulled back rather than left off-screen. Gesture hygiene is `useMediaWindowGestures`': pointer capture on the bar, body `userSelect`/`cursor` parked and restored, one idempotent teardown that release, cancel and unmount all reach. (3) **Its four corners flare into the bars** — `MediaTabsBar`'s concave fillets, four of them: the face sweeping into the bar through a quarter-disc carved out of the corner DIAGONALLY OPPOSITE the joint, with two extra gradient stops drawing the rim along the arc in the rim's own tone, so one continuous `--indigo-4` runs bar → flare → edge → flare → bar. **The geometry is DERIVED, not copied** (2026-08-06, second ask — thickening the rim 1px → 2px broke every corner until it was re-solved). With the border's outer edge at `y=0`, the box's side edge at `x=0`, `R` the sweep radius and `T = --fhead-rim-w`: the ring runs `R−T/2 → R+T/2`; its circle sits at `(R, T/2 − R)`, both coordinates forced — the bottom-most point must land on the border's **centre line** (`y = T/2`), not its outer edge, or the arc *touches* the border instead of continuing it, and the left-most must land on `x = 0` so the line dies at the bar's face, half the stroke clipped, which is what a line disappearing behind an edge looks like; so the element is `width: R`, `height: R + T`, `top: -(R + T)`, gradient centred `(R, T/2)`. **The offset names the border it reaches over** — an absolutely positioned child is placed against the PADDING box, which is why 1px → 2px moved it by two ([gotchas](../../specs/gotchas.md)). Verify by measurement, not by eye: at `x = R` the ring must cover exactly `y ∈ [0, T]`. `R` went **9 → 11** with the thickening (a heavier line wants a longer arc, or the sweep reads as a blunt notch — and `EDGE` had to follow), and **the stop ramps 0.2px → 0.6px, which is what stopped the curve stair-stepping**: hard stops do not antialias, so a 1px arc broke into dashes exactly where it ran near-parallel to the pixel grid. The weight survives because what reads as the edge is the MID-ramp (9.9 → 12.1 = 2px). `--fhead-face`/`--fhead-rim`/`--fhead-rim-w` exist so the gradients follow the face and the derivation follows the thickness (a gradient cannot inherit `background`). **Material:** `--grey-4` — **THE PLATE'S OWN** (user ask) — with `2px --indigo-4` top and bottom, NO side edges (the fillets carry the line round), a **discrete cast** (`0 7px 14px -7px` at .45 plus `0 -5px 12px -8px` at .28: mostly down, a hint up because the stream passes on both sides of a box that parks in the middle of it, and **both spreads are negative by exactly half the blur so lateral reach is ZERO** — the box's side edges ARE the frieze bars and any sideways travel would smear onto their motif), lifting ONE step to `--grey-3` with a deeper cast while held. It spent the first ask of that day on the post card's `--grey-3`, on the argument that a thing you can pick up cannot be the plate it moves over. Depth answers that better than tone: a heavier edge and a shadow say *above the plate* where a lighter coat only said *a different plate* — and the gain is at the CORNERS, since face and plate being one tone makes the carved and filled quarters indistinguishable, leaving only the swept line to state the shape. The box flows out of the bars instead of sitting between them, which a `--grey-3` patch could never do. (Knock-on: the chat log's floor was `--grey-4` and would have drawn nothing, so log and composer are both `--grey-1` now — one pale surface in two parts, told apart by their rims.) **(4) THE BOX IS THREE THIN BANDS** — handle 18px, a ONE-LINE body 30px, a label lane 19px, **71px all in, down from 119** (2026-08-06, three compression asks in a row). The body was two rows a side; both are one row now — the seat stopped stacking over the input, and the caption (`.feed-stream__sub`, "Every POST instance, newest first.") went entirely, the last of the head's prose and a claim the box would have had to keep in step with a SORT control on the same line. **(5) TWO INNER VERTICAL FRIEZE BARS** (user ask) stand at `FLARE` from each side — precisely where each corner sweep's tangent turns horizontal and the flat border begins, so the sweep comes off the container's big bar and LANDS on a post and the box reads as a framed span rather than a slab wedged between two bars. They are `FriezeBarVertical` + `FriezeBarVerticalB`, the same mirrored pair `FeedPage` frames the container with (A left, B right — the `-mirror` wave assets, so the two edges reflect instead of repeating), running the new **`slim`** variant and dialled through the new `--frieze-bar-v-*` custom properties to **`--indigo-8` plaque under a `--brown-1` motif**: the platform's first DARK frieze plate, which is the only way an edge reads inside a light box standing on a light plate — a carve is a groove on any tone. The pair is also the platform's TWO FRIEZE FAMILIES STACKED — the feed colorway's deep end under the crown strip's own plaque tone — rather than a palette invented for one 9px bar, and the spread is enormous by frieze standards (Material 800 under a 50) because a slim inner bar has no lip and no rolled edge, so the motif is the only thing separating plate from wave. (The plaque walked in one sitting: a `--teal-9`/`--teal-6` pair, added and taken straight back out without deploying, then `--indigo-10`/`--brown-4`, then `--indigo-9`, then here. `$indigo-9` is new and was KEPT — for the marks, not the plate: -10, already in the file, is the media tabs' ink, and a plaque level with the platform's deepest mark leaves the family no step underneath to write in.) **EVERY MARK AND EVERY INNER RULE IN THE BOX IS `--indigo-9`** (user ask): handle glyph and title, seat name, field and button, the slotted lens row, the count, the three band dividers, and the `is-on` plaques as a FILL. It is published as `--fhead-ink`/`--fhead-rule` on `.feed-head` and read through the slot by FeedStream's controls, which keep their previous values (`--indigo-3` rims, `--indigo-8` ink) as `var()` fallbacks for anywhere else they might be slotted. The box's OUTER edge — that line and its four corner sweeps, which follow it by construction (verified: change the dial and the arcs' gradient stops change with it) — walked `--indigo-4` → `--indigo-8` → **`--indigo-7`** across two later asks. So the box reads in THREE CONSECUTIVE STEPS with one job each: **-7 the frame, -8 the two inner posts, -9 everything written between them** — lightest at the outside, deepest at the marks, so the eye lands on what the box says and the structure holding it stays structure. `$indigo-7` and `$indigo-9` were both minted for this. **The edge is also ASYMMETRIC** (user ask): `2px` top, `3px` bottom. Not by thinning the top — the 2px is the thickening asked for two passes earlier — but by weighting the BASE, where the cast falls and where the stream runs up against the box; the post cards wore exactly this shape (a hairline box on a 2px foot) until their bed went lighter than the card. It re-solves the BOTTOM pair of fillets on its own — height `R+T` = 14, offset `-14`, centre `12.5`, ring `9.5 → 12.5` — and leaves the top pair alone, which is the derivation paying for itself. The lip is dialled to the plaque so it draws nothing (both of a slim inner bar's long edges face content). They are ABSOLUTE with `top`/`bottom: 0` — the padding box, so they span handle, body and lane in one run — while `.feed-head__inner` insets all three rows past them by `FLARE + --frieze-h/2`; measured, each row clears each post by exactly 0px, no overlap. At ~9px the motif reads as TEXTURE rather than pattern, which is the same trade `FriezeBar slim` documents for the media viewer's band. **(6) THE LABEL LANE** — a constant-height (19px) scroller across the foot holding the labels being filtered on. The active-filter chip moved here out of the lens row; the funnel stays there and takes the lens buttons' `is-on` plaque instead, so the control still says the stream is filtered without also having to say what by. The lane is ALWAYS drawn: one that appeared with its first chip would change the box's height — and move the well's reserved home slot — on every pick and clear (verified: 71px filtered and unfiltered alike). It scrolls rather than ellipsing, because a filter whose name you cannot read is not one you can trust. **(7) THE SEAT AND THE FIELD:** a **seat** (`EntityAvatar` at 24px + `OrgLogoChip`, NO NAME — three asks trimmed it, the role sub-line first and the name last, leaving the face to carry the seat; the badge stays because it is the only visible thing left saying WHICH org this seat belongs to, which was the point of putting a seat here, and both elements wear the full name/org tooltip) that CURRENTLY references claude in the Dream Operator's Garage, then an **inert filter/chat field** on the same line: a `Filter by…` input and one button, both `disabled` — no store, no service, no events; the surface placed before the wiring, and `disabled` is the honest way to say so. The MESSAGE LOG that sat above them (`No messages yet.`) is GONE (user ask): it was a summary of a conversation that does not exist, the one part of this box making a claim rather than holding a place. The button wears an oversized `filter_alt` rather than a send arrow (user ask) — 17px in a 20px box, so the mark reaches the rim on both axes and the control reads as one solid glyph; it is the same funnel the label lens wears, which is the point, the two are one gesture at two scales. The line's spring is the INPUT (`1 1 auto`, min-width 0): face, badge and button are fixed, the name yields first (it ellipses), and whatever the half has left is the field. The seat is looked up, not hardcoded: `refService.search('entities', 'claude')` once per page load (module-cached PROMISE, so two mounts share one request), taking the MASK for the org badge and the ROOT person for the face and the name — the same recomposition `entityProfileService.getIdentityCards` does for the feed, since a mask's own display name is the `"<person> @ <org>"` string that seam exists to take apart. Entity ids differ per install (claude's garage mask is 23 locally), which is why the reference is a name + an org regex at the top of the script and a static stub behind it. **The SECOND HALF** is the stream's own lenses through the `controls` slot — the box owns none of them. Position rides `v-model`-style `offset`/`update:offset` (emitted on RELEASE, not per frame: it is persisted upstream and a drag would otherwise write a `nav_state` row per frame) and its measured height rides up as `update:height`. **THE WIRING LANDED 2026-08-07** (the Talavero seat, `docs/plans/talavero-seat.md`) and the runtime claude-lookup is RETIRED: the seat is a **prop** now — Talavero's identity card from `GET /feed/lens-context` — and the box's states are: **stub** (`seat: null` — faceless "Talavero", field + button `disabled`, tooltip "the seat isn't seeded here"); **ready** (typing + Enter or the `filter_alt` button emits `ask(text)` and clears the draft); **thinking** (prop — a conic ring spins on the face, field disabled, placeholder "Talavero is thinking…"); **live** (prop — a 7px green dot on the face's corner, session-local: lit by a real lens/♪ round-trip, cleared by the 30s timeout — witnessed, never claimed); and the **reply line** (`line` prop `{kind: say\|song\|fail, text}` — a button borrowing the field's flex slot ~6s: the seat's `say`, the ♪ verse with newlines as `·`, or the one failure string; clicking emits `open-chat` → the stream fronts ChatDock on the pair conversation). FeedStream owns every round trip; the box is a face and a field. **The LANE split 70/30 later the same day** (user ask): a BROOM button at the far left (`cleaning_services`, `sweep` emit), the active chip scroller (~70%, `#labels` slot), and a ruled-off TRASH section (30%, `#trash` slot, faint bin glyph as its name) — the box owns the geometry and the disabled aesthetic's home; the stream owns the semantics: closed label chips land there instead of deleting, stand as VETOES against later verdicts (stripped before every applySpec branch, the say line appending "· trash kept out"), restore by click, and the broom clears both sections in one press with the filter change actually applied. A spoken reset leaves the trash standing; only the broom empties it.  **THE BOTTOM EDGE HAS ITS OWN TONE** (2026-08-07, user ask): `--fhead-rim-b`, split off from `--fhead-rim` the same way `--fhead-rim-w` / `--fhead-rim-w-b` (2px / 3px) already split the WEIGHT. Same reasoning read in colour: the bottom is the heavier edge because the box stands ON the stream, and a heavier edge in the SAME ink reads as more of the same line, where a lighter ink at more weight reads as a base the box rests on. It **walked `--indigo-5` → `-6` → `--indigo-9` across three asks that day** and the walk is the argument: -5 made the base the box's brightest edge, -6 (the pure hue) was the first setting where it read as the DEEPER of the two, and -9 is the tone the box's whole INTERIOR is built from — so the frame is a `--indigo-7` LID and a `--indigo-9` FOOT, and the foot is made of the same ink as everything the box is built out of. ⚠ **The bottom edge is drawn in THREE places** — this border plus BOTH bottom fillet gradients (`--bl`, `--br`), whose colour stops carry the line around the corner — and all three read the one dial, which is the only reason the sweep doesn't change colour halfway (verified by measurement: bottom border + both bottom flares all `rgb(40,53,147)`, top border + both top flares `rgb(57,73,171)`); the TOP pair keeps `--fhead-rim`. ⚠ The feed container's frieze bars took `--indigo-5` on their inner-facing lips in the ask right after this edge FIRST moved, deliberately as one mark — **that pairing is broken by three levels now** and the lips were left where they were; don't restore either to match the other without an ask. |

The body (both halves at once, since the lens half declares no floor of its own) went `--indigo-9`, then `--indigo-7`, and landed back on **`--grey-4`, THE PLATE'S OWN TONE**, with `--fhead-rule` tracking it each time: `--indigo-8` on the -9 floor, still -8 on -7, and **`--indigo-6`** now that the floor is pale — a wall is one step off its floor, and which direction "off" means depends on which side of the floor it sits. So the box is a light plate with ONE plated band across its top: `--indigo-8` handle · `--grey-4` body · `--grey-4` lane, the colorway spent entirely on lines and marks. The three CONTROL FLOORS — chat bubble, lens bundle, label bubble — are **`--grey-3`**, one step above the body, and their rims travelled together as **ONE decision in two files** (`--lens-rim` in FeedStream, `--fhead-chat-rim` in FeedHeadBox) through `--indigo-8` → -7 → `--indigo-6` — until one ask moved the SECTION alone to **`--indigo-9`** — and on 2026-08-08 the FIVE WALLS and the HEADER PLATE joined it there (user ask, the board's inner borders) while the COMPOSER went the other way entirely, to an **`--indigo-1` face under an `--indigo-5` rim** (`--fhead-chat-face`, a new dial, read by the field AND the reply line so the bubble cannot change colour when the seat answers; the face came off `--grey-3` via one pass at -2, the rim walked -8 → -7 → -6 → -9 → -6 → -5). ⚠ Measured 1.01:1 against the `--brown-1` room — the SAME lightness — so the bubble is stated by HUE (cool pane, warm room) and by its rim, never by tone; the face dial's next step DOWN is -2 at 1.36:1 if it ever has to stand up tonally. So the board is one deep structural ink (-9), one warm mark ink (`--brown-1`) and one pale object (the bubble). ⚠ `--fhead-chat-rim` and `--lens-rim` are four levels apart and no longer one decision — check both before moving either. ⚠ At -9 the section's lines equal its own floor and its buttons' own plate, so **the lens dividers stop drawing**: three lenses read as ONE dark bar carrying three glyphs, not three plates with rules between them. That is what "all indigo-9" means here — don't hunt for a missing border. The rim still draws where it meets a `--grey-3` face (the label field's edge, the rule behind the verb). ⚠ The body states `--grey-4` and does NOT read `--fhead-face`, though they are the same tone today: the face lifts to `--grey-3` while the box is HELD, which is exactly the controls' floor, so a body following it would rise to meet its own controls on every drag (verified held: body 224, bubble 238). **AND THE BODY'S PAINT NO LONGER SHOWS** (2026-08-08, user ask): the TALK half took a floor of its own — **`--brown-1`** on `.feed-head__half--talk`, stated for the same held-drag reason. **`--brown-1` also floors the LANE'S TWO CHIP TRAYS and `FeedStream.vue`'s label bar** since a later ask the same day — the trays had been transparent (the box's `--grey-4` showing through), which is why the radius they were briefly given drew nothing; the lane now reads as this room one band lower, warm trays with `--indigo-9` chips standing in them. **BOTH KEYS STAND OUTSIDE THEIR TRAYS, AND AFTER THEM** (two later asks): the BIN left `.feed-head__lane-trash` for the lane itself, then BOTH keys moved from the LEFT of their trays to the RIGHT — each room now reads "the labels, then the thing you do to them", and the lane order is `active · broom · trash · bin`. Knock-ons: the trash tray took symmetric 6px padding (a chip pressed into a rounded corner sits inside the curve); the ROOM DIVIDER is declared once, on `.feed-head__lane-broom`'s `border-right`, the only key edge that falls on a boundary now (the bin's would land at the lane's outer end) and the bin carries none; the tray's basis is `calc(40% - 24px)`, the room being tray + key. **THE SPLIT IS 60/40** (user ask, from 70/30 — measured 40/59 with the broom's rounding), and the percentage is of the lane's CONTENT box, 6px narrower than the lane since the post gutter. ⚠ **The keys state `padding: 0`**: a `<button>` carries the UA's `1px 6px`, which with `border-box` overran the broom's stated basis and sized it by min-content (25px against a stated 22) — the bin, a `q-icon`, never had it, which is how the pair drifted apart. The **BROOM is 22px with an 11px glyph** (user ask: a little smaller, from 24/12px which measured 26 with the wall outside it), so it is the smaller key by 2px; 24 is the number if they should be equal. ⚠ **Their glyphs are 11 and 13** (the bin went up in a later ask): the numbers differ on purpose — `delete_outline` spends its size on a lid and a body drawn in hairlines where `cleaning_services` is a solid shape, so matching the numbers is what made the pair look uneven. ⚠ **THE LANE IS 20px and its top rule is 1px `--indigo-7`** (`--fhead-lane-rule` / `--fhead-lane-rule-w`, 2026-08-08 — two asks, thicker first and then "a thin `--indigo-7` hairline", which is one story: at -9 between a -9 body and a -9 lane no thickness could make a mark appear, so the 4px it spent an ask at bought a wider dark BAND and nothing else. Given an ink that contrasts, a hairline states the same division in a quarter of the space, so the width came back down in the same move). It now matches the header's foot rule and the four container rims exactly — every VISIBLE line on this board is `--indigo-7` at 1px, and the `--indigo-9` walls are the ones that state a division by a plate ending. ⚠ The lane's height and that rule are ONE CHAIN, the one to redo after any change here: a chip measures **16.6px** → a tray needs 17 of inside → with its 1px rims a tray needs 19 → the lane needs 19 + the rule. At 4px that was 23; at the hairline it was 20; and with the 3px of daylight the hairline was then given below it, the lane is **23** again (19 + 1 + 3). Board **95.8** with both halves of that daylight, 89.8 without. At 19 and at 21 the chips clipped by 1.6px SILENTLY. **Box 89.8 desktop / 97.4 phone** after the sitting (91.8 → 84.4 → 85.8 → 87.8 → 89.8). ⚠ With the lane at `--indigo-9`, its top rule and BOTH KEY PLATES stop drawing (all three are that tone) — what is left of the keys is their `--brown-1` glyphs on the band, and the broom's `--indigo-8` hover plate appears only when pointed at, which says "control" better than a plate that was always there. **IT IS RIMMED 1px `--indigo-7`** (`--fhead-room-rim`, 2026-08-08, user ask) — the ink the BOX'S OWN FRAME is drawn in, shared by all four warm containers (this room, the two lane trays, `FeedStream`'s label bar) so they are outlined as one family; a -9 rim would have made each read as another wall. ⚠ **That rim REPLACED this half's `border-right`** — one of the box's five walls, the rule between the two rooms, which had already stopped drawing when the body went -9 under it: **four walls in the box now, not five**. **FOUR GUTTERS, ONE DIAL** (`--fhead-gutter` 3px, four user asks): the BODY is padded `3px` ALL ROUND, so the two rooms sharing that row stand off the header above and off the lane's hairline below (the lane answers with its own `padding-top` — the other half of that pair, so the line sits between two runs of `--indigo-9` and reads as a rule BETWEEN two sections rather than as one section's edge; ⚠ that bottom half only became worth having once the rule stopped being a 4px `--indigo-9` band and became a 1px `--indigo-7` line, daylight beside an invisible band being a gutter beside a gutter) — one declaration because it is one relation, a `padding-top` on either half alone starting them at different heights (measured: both open 3px under the header's rule). Top only, the band's bottom being flush against the lane's 4px rule which already separates there; ⚠ the box pays the 3px (90 → 92.8 desktop / 99.4 phone) since this band has no stated height to absorb it, and on the phone it composes — body-top puts the talk room off the header, the lens half's own `padding-top` puts the filter block off the talk room. Then: the POST GUTTER goes on `__body` and `__lane`, the two bands already painted -9, because `.feed-head__inner`'s own padding is post CLEARANCE whose box spans the full width — painting that would cover the flare and post area over the box's face; and the CHAT/LABEL gutter is `padding-left` on `.feed-head__half--lens`, its one non-zero edge (measured before it: the talk room's right edge and the label bar's left edge were BOTH at x=547 — two 1px rims making a 2px seam), turning 90° to `padding-top` on the phone where the pair stacks. **IT IS ROUNDED** (all four corners — **`--fhead-talk-r` = `calc(--fhead-room-r + 6px)` = 13px** (it was +3/10px for a few asks; 13 is where the CONCENTRIC answer lives — the 12px bubble inside is held off 2px top/bottom and 3px left/right, so a room at 14/15 would be exactly parallel to it, and 13 reads as parallel where 10 cut across the bubble's own corner), against the trays' 7 and the label bar's 10: a modifier on the shared dial rather than a second number, so the four still move together, and the biggest container takes the bigger corner — it is also the only one holding an object with a corner of its own, the composer's 12px bubble) **and the BODY BEHIND IT IS `--indigo-9`** — which is one decision, and the second attempt at it. Round ONE (these same radii over the body's `--grey-4`) was reverted on sight: every corner opened a pale notch and the board read as a tray of loose tiles. The rule that fixed it is the user's own — **whatever lies behind a rounded container is painted `--indigo-9`** — so a corner opens onto the board's structural ink and the curve reads as the container ending. That took `.feed-head__body` off `--grey-4` and `.feed-head__lane` off transparent; the label bar already had the lens section's -9. ⚠ The box's own FACE stays `--grey-4`, and the body's floor-versus-control reasoning below is HISTORY from when this line was the floor you saw — it is what to come back to if the rooms are ever unpainted, radii included. The composer keeps its 12px: rooms take the house corner, the one BUBBLE keeps its own. — so with the lens half already `--indigo-9` and both halves stretching full height, the body's `--grey-4` is the BASE the two rooms lie on rather than the section itself (delete a room's paint and it is what shows through). The pair is not a new one: `--indigo-9` plaque under a `--brown-1` motif is exactly what the box's two inner frieze posts, its lane keys and its lens buttons already wear — the body now states that mapping at ROOM scale, deep plaque beside warm motif, so the two halves separate by MATERIAL and no longer lean on the wall between them. The talk room is a warm bay one step ABOVE the plate (measured `rgb(239,235,233)` against the body and lane's 224: 1.11:1, R−B 6 where the neutrals are 0) — the one warm thing in a box otherwise spent on indigo and greys. ⚠ **What it cost, measured, and what answered it:** the composer and its reply line were floored `--grey-3` (238) against this room's #efebe9 — **1.02:1, a neutral on a warm tone at the same lightness** — so the bubble stopped reading as an ISLAND and hung on its rim alone. The next two asks answered with HUE rather than tone: the bubble took an **`--indigo-1` face** (`--fhead-chat-face`) under an **`--indigo-5`** rim, which measures 1.01:1 against the room — the same lightness AGAIN — but now the two tints lean opposite ways (bubble B−R 14 cool, room R−B 6 warm) where before one of them was neutral and leaned nowhere. A cool pane in a warm room. If it ever has to stand up by TONE, that face dial's next step down is `--indigo-2` at 1.36:1. (The lens half is the opposite bet throughout: pale `--grey-3` bars on the box's deepest floor.) Knock-on: the seat's liveness dot, ringed in `--fhead-face`, is a faint 1.11:1 halo here — better than the nothing it drew on the body's own tone. And the composer's corners went **4px → `--radius-sm`**, the radius the two lens bars already wear, so the three control blocks match. **Measured after:** box 84px desktop = handle 18 + body 42 + lane 19 + 5 of rims (it was 71); with the bigger face the body is 51 and the box 93. 105px at 390px wide, where the two halves stack. |
| `posts/FeedStream.vue` | ⭐ **The feed container's content column** (end of 2026-07-25) — the post stream that had been parked in `FeedPageLegacy.vue`, refitted for the narrow indigo box on `FeedPage` and mounted in `.feed-container__body`. Two changes came with the move, both forced by the column's width: (1) **ONE column, not two** — the legacy page put the stream (`col-md-7`) beside a tall `FeedPostPanel` (`col-md-5`) across the whole page, and Quasar's `col-md-*` breakpoints read the **WINDOW, not the parent**, so inside a 50%-of-track box that split would still fire and leave two cramped strips; (2) the detail panel UNFOLDED IN PLACE — selecting a card rendered the same `FeedPostPanel` directly beneath it. **That unfold is GONE (2026-07-25, fourth pass)** and with it the whole selection machinery (`selected`, `isSelected`, `select`, `keyOf`, the holder's `selectedKey`, `.is-selected` styling, `.feed-stream__detail` and the four `:deep(.feed-post-panel*)` overrides). It was **the one thing on the surface that broke the square rule**: the panel obeyed no ceiling, so a selected card measured **615 card + 570 panel = 1185px against a 615px width — 1.93×** — and being full-bleed and flush underneath it read as the card simply continuing, which is what "the height is way longer than the width" was seeing. It had also become redundant: the card now carries the whole body, the author, the label paths and the tallies, and the panel's remaining extras (node/moment chips, thread provenance) are one click away in the post viewer. The card is no longer a click target at all. **The PANEL came back on 2026-07-26 as a floating box beside the container** (`skeletons/SkeletonFlyout.vue`) — what died was laying it INSIDE the column, not the box itself, and the ceiling argument above is exactly why it may not go back there. The card carried ONE TRIGGER for that box between 2026-08-09 and 2026-08-10: the foot's **`PostMicro` with `:linked="false"`** — a chip that stopped being an anchor so the same press could open the box instead of navigating (a wrapper cannot catch that click; the chip's root carries `@click.stop`, so the listener goes on the chip — see [gotchas](../../specs/gotchas.md)). (The byline's title plate was the second trigger from 2026-07-26 until 08-09 deleted it, together with `.post-square__go`, the byline's open-post link — the title states itself in the CAP and the way into the VIEWER is the flyout's own links.) **THE FOOT CHIP IS A DOOR AGAIN (2026-08-10, user ask)**: a router-link to the POST VIEWER (`/posts/:id`, the kind's own route — the `:linked="false"` and the `@click` both came off), reading **`sym_o_post :: skeleton :: <hash>`** in Nasalization. Three deliberate departures from a stock chip, all borrowed from the CAP one strip up so the card's two ends speak one dialect: the **glyph** is the cap's `sym_o_post` and not the kind's default `edit_note`; the **type word** is `skeleton`, because a post's address is a handle on its skeleton and that is the reading every control around it opens; the **separator** is `::`. Later the same day the type WORD became the cap's `sym_o_orthopedics` DRAWN — the chip's neighbours in this strip are all glyphs and one spelt-out word was the odd thing out. The three ride new `typeLabel` / `typeIcon` / `sep` props on MicroChip (see its row). The card's doors into the skeleton reading are unchanged and both in the cap's lane — `sym_o_orthopedics` routes to `/skeletons/:id`, `open_in_new` opens it in the flyout beside the feed. **Nothing in the feed emits `select` any more**, so the flyout's rich `item` face is reachable only from `FeedPageLegacy`; the emit, the `selectedId` prop, `isOpen` and the `.is-open` card marking all stay wired — one `@click` from a new trigger — but no card presses them today from the chip — the REFERENCES button re-homed that press hours later (below), so `select` has a live caller again and the foot chip's own `.is-open` teal accent is what went for good. When it IS set, the CARD wears an **`--indigo-11` border + halo glow** (2026-08-09: ring + bloom box-shadow layered over the contact shadow, hover at half strength, open/expanded at full — reach ≤ ~8.5px, dying inside the 10px card gap). **THE FOOT IS RULED IN FOUR CELLS (2026-08-10, second ask)** — the CAP's arrangement at the card's other end, so the square opens and closes in one grammar: `[refs] │ [chip copy] │ [comments forks] │ [⌃n over ⌄n]`. The strip itself is `align-items: stretch` with NO padding (each cell carries it), which is what lets `.post-square__foot-rule` meet both edges square without the negative-margin trick `__byline-rule` needs; the old row was chip + `q-space` + three tallies at `padding: 5px 9px 7px`. **REFS** at the far-left edge is the flyout press the chip gave up, re-homed and named — `sym_o_developer_guide` (SYMBOLS-prefixed: bare `developer_guide` resolves against Material Icons, finds no ligature and draws an empty 14px box — it did, for one pass), emitting `select`, `.is-on` when this card's panel is open. The **ADDRESS** cell is the only one that gives (`min-width: 0`, so the hash ellipsizes and the three rigid cells never leave the card); its `content_copy` puts the **pathchain address** — `skeletons/<hash>`, not a browser URL — on the clipboard, with the media viewer's glyph-flips-to-`check` receipt keyed on a per-card `copiedId` rather than a shared boolean (thirty cards, one flag, thirty checkmarks). **VOTES** closes at the far right as a COLUMN: `keyboard_arrow_up` over `keyboard_arrow_down` at 9px (tailless, third ask — the tailed 11px pair read as two instructions), each with its own tally; the single `thumb_up` it replaces showed `votes.up` alone, half of what the feed has always answered with. Tallies, not controls — nothing in this strip has ever been a vote button. `.post-square__stat` letters in **Nasalization** now (it was the card's last Space Mono), as does the strip, and `.post-square__foot-act` is `__cap-act` one register smaller (18px). Foot measured 28px. **THE DIVIDER WALK ENDED 2026-08-10** (three asks). The `RgbHairline` was copied to the byline→labels and labels→content seams; the FRIEZE moved UP from byline→labels to close the CAP, replacing the flipped band that had bracketed the foot's (the `--cap` modifier went with it, and `vflip` has no user on this surface); then an ask **re-made the bracket one seam lower, around the LABELS** — a pair `.post-square__hairline--rail` (1px bread instead of 2, the lower one `--flip`ped to indigo-over-cyan so the two indigo ends faced each other across the chips) — **and the day's LAST ask took that pair straight back out**, with both modifiers, leaving `.post-square__hairline` alone at its base weight over the foot. ⚠ **The card reads `cap │ FRIEZE │ byline │ hairline │ labels │ hairline │ pit │ rgb │ foot`**, ONE rgb band — the label strip bracketed by two plain `--grey-6` `border-bottom`s since the day's closing asks (the byline's, and the rail strip's riding its `v-if`), which is what those seams wore before the frieze ever took them. Plain rules could come back where the rgb pair could not: a flat line beside a rimmed box states a seam, a 4px sandwich pressed against one states it twice. The pair went because the label strip took a RIM of its own (below): a rimmed box pressed between two full-bleed rules is the same seam stated twice, and one line replacing two bands is fewer lines, not more. The FOOT's band never belonged to the bracket — it closes the reading area and is half of nothing, the standing the cap's band had before the frieze took that seam. **THE LABEL RAIL STOPPED BEING A TRAY, THEN BECAME A PANEL AGAIN** (2026-08-10, six asks in a run). It first lost everything that made it a box: `.post-square__rail-strip` lost its `6px 7px` padding on both axes, `.post-square__rail` lost its vertical padding (the chips' own rims are the lane; the 6px side lead stays as the scroller's run-in/run-out), its `7px` radius, and its uneven `1px/2px-top` rim (`border: 0`) — and its floor walked `--grey-4` → -6 → -7 → -6 → -8 → -6 → -4 (a full circle closed the same day) → **`--grey-5`**, the one level the walk had never tried, where it rests. The walk tried every level a full-bleed figure could stand on: -7 is the divider plate (the RgbHairline's bread), -8 the frieze band's plaque, and both read as CHROME — right for a plate the card is BUILT from, wrong for one it CLASSIFIES itself on, where the strip stopped being part of the card and became a fourth divider with chips lying on it. -6 is the card's own LINE ink, so the band was that rule given height and filled. **The round trip is the finding**: every step of it argued DEPTH, and depth was the wrong axis — -6/-7/-8 differ only in how much like chrome the band reads, and this row is the one thing on the card that is neither chrome nor content but CLASSIFICATION. At the shallow end it is the shallowest figure on the square, lighter than the card's own line ink instead of equal to it, so the strip stopped competing with the two rgb rules then bracketing it and read as a shelf the chips stand on (the rules are gone now — the level survived them, and with the rim back the floor is a step under its own edge, which is what a shelf is). ⚠ **-4 was one step too far, and for a reason worth keeping**: that level is the **feed BED's** tone, so card and field met at exactly one place and that place sat between two rgb rules — the band read as a window through the card to the surface under it, a real device but not what a classification strip is. **`--grey-5`** keeps everything the shallow end bought and takes the coincidence back; it belongs to no other figure on this surface. What separates the tiers now is HUE (`--indigo-2` plate, `--deep-purple-2` members), not the floor. ⚠ **THEN IT WENT BACK TO BEING AN INSET PANEL** (the day's last rail ask, and the same ask that deleted the rgb pair above — the two are ONE decision): `.post-square__rail-strip` takes `padding: 4px 7px` again and `.post-square__rail` a **`6px` radius** and an **EVEN `1px` `--grey-6` rim**. Three dials, one reading — the rail is a box set INTO the card, not a band spanning it, and it has to stand off the card's edges or its corners curve against them. The rim is EVEN this time, where the old one was `1px/2px-top` to mirror the card's own uneven border (that weight said "something sits down onto this"; nothing sits on a free-standing panel). The vertical air is on the STRIP, not the band — it is card showing around the panel, and it replaces the lane the two rgb bands used to hold; the band keeps its own thin `2px` inside the rim for the chips. ⚠ The floor was tried at **`--grey-4`** once more and came straight back to `--grey-5` on the next ask. Worth recording because the try was principled: -4's objection (it is the feed BED's tone, so a full-bleed band read as a WINDOW through the card) had genuinely EXPIRED with the inset — a rimmed plate standing on card material all round reads as a plate, not a hole. It came off anyway, so the level is settled by EXPERIMENT and not by that argument: -5 has been chosen over -4 twice now, under two different classifications of the row. Full walk: -4 → -6 → -7 → -6 → -8 → -6 → -4 → -5 → -4 → **-5**. Height 42 → 21 → 34 → **31px** (strip padding, rim, then the padding tightening to 2px against the new hairline's 1px). The rail's own SIDE padding came down `6px` → **`4px`** in the same run — the run-in before the first bundle plate — which may not reach 0: a scroller with no side lead reads as content already cut off at rest. ⚠ **THE THREE RAIL RADII ARE ONE NESTED FAMILY** since that run's last ask: rail **10px** › `.post-square__bundle` **7px** › `.post-square__bundle-item` **5px**, each inner corner roughly its parent minus the lane between them. The rail now OUT-ROUNDS the pit (7px), reversing the rule its original 6px was chosen under ("a hair under the pit's") — move one and move all three, or concentric boxes read pinched at the corners. **A `new_label` BUTTON closes the strip's right end** (`.post-square__rail-add`, wearing `.post-square__cap-act` so the card keeps ONE button language across cap, foot and rail). It sits OUTSIDE the rail, in the strip's flex row, because the rail is a horizontal SCROLLER — a control laid inside it slides off exactly on the cards carrying the most classification; the rail takes the slack (`flex: 1 1 auto` + `min-width: 0`) and the button stays put. ⚠ **It has no handler yet, on purpose**: attaching a label is `POST /skeletons/:id/labels {labelId}` behind `maker/LabelPicker.vue` (compact mode, `picked`, `excludeIds`) and is owner-gated, while a feed is mostly other people's posts — what the control should do on a foreign card is a product decision, and wiring the owner path alone would put a button on every card that 403s on most, which takes back the 8px the deleted bands paid for: the pit's media constant moved 276 → 278 → **276** again, the day's closing asks putting it back where it started by a different route. **THE CHIPS WERE REBUILT, THEN BUNDLED.** The rebuild first: they had carried a heavier foot (1px box, 2px bottom) since the rail was born; that foot became an `--indigo-11` → `--deep-purple-11` **gradient**, then grew `--grey-7` bread above and below it — the RgbHairline's sandwich at chip scale, 4px of edge under an 18px pill — and then **the whole construction came off**. What it was solving ("this chip sits ON something") is a DEPTH problem, and it was answering with colour and weight on a card that already runs three coloured devices down its length. A `0 1px 1px` shadow replaced it for two minutes and came off too. **BUNDLED BY ROOT (2026-08-10, user ask)** — the rail holds one plate per label TREE, not per label PATH. A post classified twice under one root printed that root twice; on a plumbing post the rail read `PATHCHAIN › … PATHCHAIN › …`, the same word at the head of every plate, and the root is the least informative step in a path besides. Now: a bundle reads `[ 🪐 :: ]` then one member plate per label of that tree. `labelBundles(item)` groups `labelPaths(item)` by `names[0]` into `{root, mark, items}`, each member spelling only the TAIL of its chain (`lp.path` rides along unsliced, so the tooltip is still the whole classification). Grouping only, never filtering — every label keeps its id, its link and its funnel; a bare-ROOT label states its own name in the member cell rather than rendering an empty one; order is `labelPaths`' order held by Map insertion, so user classification still sorts ahead of the PATHCHAIN families. Verified on a full feed page: 72 label paths → 42 bundles (30 marked PATHCHAIN, 12 name-only CLAUDE), 30 of them merging two members each. **The chrome did not change, it MOVED**, from `.post-square__label` to `.post-square__bundle`: coat `--grey-3` (a day's walk: `--grey-3` → `--deep-purple-1` → `--indigo-1` → `--light-cream` → back. The cream's argument was that the plate IS the card's own material lying in a band cut through the card; what it cost was legibility of the TIERS, since the members are greys and a warm sheet under cool tokens states hue where the arrangement wants depth. At -3 the plate is one step off its members' -2 and three off the band's -6 — band, sheet, token, three tones of one material), rim **`--indigo-2`** at `1px` (tone taken off `--indigo-1` on 2026-08-10, one step deeper because the weight had already left the highlight register; 0.5 → 0.75 → 1 across two asks, so the plate's edge is twice its members' 0.5px rims — the DIFFERENCE is the point, not the weight; ⚠ a full pixel leaves the sub-pixel register the rest of the rail is drawn in, and indigo-50 was the palest thing that level could do rather than a tone picked for a full pixel — which is what the move to -2 fixed), **plus a SECOND rim outside it since 2026-08-10** (user ask): a `box-shadow: 0 0 0 0.5px var(--grey-7)` ring. The two do different jobs, which is what makes two lines on a small plate legitimate — the indigo is a HIGHLIGHT (a lit lip against the plate's own coat) and never could state the plate against the BAND, since the band is grey and the lip near-white; with the rail floor up at `--grey-4` it has even less to push against. The grey ring is the OUTLINE the highlight cannot be, at the divider-plate tone, well under both band and coat. Read outward: coat → lit lip → drawn edge → band. ⚠ A RING, not a border or an `outline` — it costs no layout (a border would grow the plate and the rail's measured 24px with it), it follows the `4px` radius exactly, and it leaves `outline` free for focus; the plate's own `overflow: hidden` does not clip it, since overflow clips CONTENT, never a shadow outside the border box. `4px` corners, `overflow: hidden`. The chip's old rule was chip-on-tray restating card-on-bed one level down; it broke when the tray became a mid-grey band and the step widened from one to three levels, and what replaces it is stronger — the plate is not a step off its tray, it IS the card's material, and the strip is a hole cut through the card. Warmth is what makes that read: band, lines, pit and foot are all neutral, so these are the rail's one warm object and they are warm *because the card is*. The rim landed on indigo-50 after a walk that ran the greys all the way up (-7 → -6 → -5 → -4 → -3 → -2) and then stepped off them — at -6 it was exactly the band's own tone and did nothing; by -2 it had become a HIGHLIGHT rather than an outline; indigo-50 keeps that register and adds a COOL edge on a warm sheet, so the outline is stated by hue as well as by lightness. Sub-pixel because 1px is not the thinnest register and the platform already writes in that one. **The members are plates of their own** (`.post-square__bundle-item`): `--grey-2` on a `--grey-5` rim after a three-ask walk (-2 on -4 → -4 on -1 → here) that tried the pairing both ways up and settled on the conventional one — a pale coat with a darker rim. The inverted setting (mid coat, lit lip, echoing the plate's own highlight) is a real device and is why the walk happened, but it made every member as heavy as the sheet holding it. **Each member also carries an INNER RING since 2026-08-10** (user ask): `box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 0.5px var(--deep-purple-2)`, a level **minted for this one line** (Material 100, beside the family's two hover tones — deep purple's first RESTING role on the rail). With it the row has **one colour per TIER** — `--indigo-2` states the plate, `--deep-purple-2` states its members — which is what finally tells the two nested boxes apart after every edge here had been a grey separated only by depth, a job six steps at 0.5px could not do. Reading outward from a member: ring → member rim `--grey-5` → plate coat `--grey-3` → plate rim `--indigo-2` → plate ring `--grey-7` → band `--grey-4`. ⚠ **INSET, where the plate's ring is outset** — members sit flush against each other inside the plate, so an outward ring would land on the next token's and read as one doubled line down the row. That two-TIER reading is the whole point of bundling — a warm sheet per tree, cool tokens lying on it — and it is why the members carry no hairline between them the way the CAP's and the FOOT's cells do: a rule and a rim inside the same three pixels would be one seam drawn twice. The label itself is now bare TYPE inside its member; `--indigo-9` ink on hover. **The `::` seam** (`.post-square__bundle-sep`) stands between the tree and its labels — the card's own separator for "this term qualifies the next", as in the CAP's origin clause and the FOOT's `post :: skeleton :: <hash>`. It takes the MARK's tone rather than the labels' ink — both `--grey-9`, and the seam BOLD (four dots of ink have to hold their own between a 26px drawing and a bordered plate; weight costs no space where a size bump would push both neighbours apart) — because mark and seam are one utterance and the members answer it; `margin: 0 -2px` halves the plate's 4px gap around it, since punctuation is a joint between two objects and not a third one. **The plate's HOVER** is two dials, and the ask was precise about what not to do: coat → `--grey-2` (*not* the `--deep-purple-1` it took while this chrome belonged to a single chip — a plate that turns lilac states a SELECTION, and this one is a container, so what it should say is "you are in here", a step of tone rather than a change of hue), rim → `--deep-purple-11`. ⚠ At `--grey-2` the coat meets its members' own tone and the two flatten into one field, the `--grey-5` rims still telling them apart — that is the ask's arithmetic, not an oversight. The root's side answers together: mark and seam both → `--indigo-8` (walked `--indigo-11` → `--indigo-2` → here; pale accents looked like a wash on a drawing whose mass is stroke). **The funnel takes no width while hidden.** It was `opacity: 0` alone, which hides a control without UNPLACING it — 11px of icon plus its gap sat inside every member as dead space that looked like slack padding. It collapses to `width: 0` now and opens on `.post-square__rail:hover`, the same trigger as before, so the strip shifts once as a whole rather than each member twitching. ⚠ Its `display: inline-flex; line-height: 1` is load-bearing, not tidiness: as a default `inline-block` button the glyph sat on a text baseline and measured ~22px for an 11px icon, which cost nothing while it stood outside the chip and drove the rail 21 → 28px the moment bundling moved it inside a member. Hover ink `--indigo-8`, replacing the hard-coded `#00829c` teal — the last of that generic interactive tone left in this rail. Rail geometry after all of it: bundle 20.03px, rail 24px (the band's own vertical padding came back at a thin `2px` — ⚠ on `.post-square__rail`, *not* on `.post-square__rail-strip`, which would float the band in a gap between the two `RgbHairline`s instead of giving the plates room inside it). Card chrome 146px; media budget **276 / expanded twin 310** (280/314 until 2026-08-10: +2 for the frieze band's new grey-6 rules, −6 for the pit's halved margin. An inner pit HEADER lived between those two edits — a bold post name over a grey-6 hairline, which put the pair at 295/329 — and was **removed the same day at the user's word**; nothing of it survives but the sticky lesson in `specs/gotchas.md`). Two things the gradient pass is worth remembering for: a gradient **cannot** be painted on one border edge as a `bottom / 100% Npx` strip — `border-radius` clips the rectangle and the colour dies before the corners, which is exactly how that foot looked wrong; the way that works is a full **border-box** background layer with the coat clipped to **padding-box**, so what shows of the gradient is the border RING and it follows the radius like a real border. And ⚠ `background-origin` must be restated after every `background` shorthand — the shorthand resets it, and the `:hover` rule silently moved the foot 2px up the chip until it was. Both are in [gotchas](../../specs/gotchas.md). The strip ELEMENT survives its padding: a divider on this band has to run lip to lip, which means the full-width strip and never the inset rail. `FeedPostPanel.vue` now has TWO live consumers: `FeedPageLegacy` (the restore point) and the flyout. The **container's box is the frame, so the scroller lives here, not on the window**: a `.feed-stream__head` band on top (padded `calc(var(--frieze-h) + 8px)` so the title clears the crown strip the container runs up over) — **moved INSIDE the well and made `position: sticky; top: 0` at the end of 2026-07-25**, which is the whole reason the cards can travel behind it: as a sibling ABOVE the well it was a separate box, the well clipped its own content, and nothing could ever pass under the band (a translucent coat there would have shown only the container's flat plaque). It **was GLASS** for one day — `rgba(var(--indigo-3-rgb), 0.82)` over `backdrop-filter: blur(5px)` (`-webkit-` prefixed too, Safari still needs it), retired for a flat `--indigo-1` plaque at the end of 2026-07-25, see **(E)** below — with a `2px solid var(--indigo-3)` bottom edge — the band's own tone at full opacity, so the border reads as the solid edge of a translucent band rather than a separate rule laid under it (it was a generic `rgba(var(--ink-rgb), .14)` hairline, then briefly `--indigo-2`). That was the **one deliberate exception to the surface's flat-plaque rule**, which bans sheen and glass everywhere else — the rule is universal here again since (E). The alpha rose from `.72` when the blur arrived: at `.72` with no blur the text travelling under the band still read as *text*, competing with the title; the blur softens it to movement and the extra opacity settles the rest. The band is also **short** — `padding: 8px 12px`, symmetric. It used to carry `calc(var(--frieze-h) + 8px)` on top to clear the crown strip the container runs up over; now that it is its own surface it **stands in for the strip** across the container's width instead of sitting below it, the same move the drawer and the stack widget already make where they meet the top edge (band height 82.6px → 55.6px). `margin: 0 -5px 10px` seats it — side negatives cancel the well's side padding so the band stays full-bleed frieze lip to frieze lip (both were `10px` until 2026-08-06, when the padding halved and this had to halve with it), the bottom margin is the 10px gap to the first card. **The margin-top is `0` and the well's `padding-top` went to `0` with it, NOT cancelled by a negative margin** — sticky constrains the margin box, so `-10px` there is undone by `top: 0` and drops the band 10px below the container's top edge while swallowing the card gap; see [gotchas.md](../../specs/gotchas.md). Verified: band top − well top = `0` at rest AND after scrolling, gap to card₁ = 10px, band width = well width, 56px of card₁ behind the band at `scrollTop: 260`. **THE BAND IS RETIRED — 2026-08-06, user ask**: the head is a draggable BOX now, `posts/FeedHeadBox.vue` (its own row above), placed over the well instead of pinned inside it, and `.feed-stream__head` is gone with all four of the rules this paragraph exists to explain — the sticky pinning, the `margin: 0 -3px 10px`, the `--grey-4` coat and the `2px --indigo-3` edge. Each was an answer to *a plate living at the top of a padded scroller*, and the head does not live there any more: it spans the field by construction, hides the cards by being opaque rather than by being pinned, and draws its own two edges. What the stream KEPT is the band's right-hand end — the sub-line and the lens cluster, handed to the box through its `controls` slot, still styled here, now stacked under a new `.feed-stream__controls` (a centred flex row that SCROLLS sideways rather than wrapping — a wrap would silently make the whole head taller) because the slot is half a box wide instead of a whole band. **THE CLUSTER IS FOUR DROPDOWNS, A FUNNEL AND THE COUNT (2026-08-07, three user asks)** — each dropdown one 21px `.feed-stream__label-open` plate wearing a glyph and, beside it, a `.feed-stream__lens-n` MARK that states what the lens is set to. ⚠ **That shape lasted a day**: later on 2026-08-07 the count moved to the box's header, SORT BY followed it, the label funnel became a row of its own, and the three that were left went into a `QBtnGroup` — so `.feed-stream__label-open` no longer exists and the cluster is TWO BARS, not one row. The per-lens notes below are still current; see the `FeedHeadBox.vue — the 2026-08-07 board pass` row for the arrangement. The lenses were: **TRUST** (`.feed-stream__hops`, `connect_without_contact` + the radius — `∞` for the open feed, else 1/2/3 → `?maxHops`; it was a four-plate segmented control, ~100px spent naming the three radii you are NOT under, and folding it left the row 9px narrower even after two lenses were added beside it), **DATE** (`.feed-stream__when`, **`calendar_month`** + a short word → `?from=&to=` — WHICH DAYS. It was the "time lens" and owned the clock too until **2026-08-08, when the user asked for the pair to be split**: one control answering both questions answered neither fully — you could say "since Tuesday 09:00" but never "mornings". Menu = six presets over a custom-range drawer of two native **`date`** fields, either end optional, plus APPLY. ⚠ The split trades away an instant-precise window FROM THIS DRAWER; `?from=`/`?to=` still take full ISO and the spoken lens still resolves them, so the capability is in the platform, not in this tray. It builds the same `when` object the spoken lens's clause does, so `resolveWhenLocal` stays the one resolver and "today" is the VIEWER's today), **TIME** (`.feed-stream__clock`, `schedule` + `a.m.`/`p.m.`/`eve`/`night`/`9–5` or the hand-typed pair itself → **`?timeFrom=&timeTo=&tzOffset=`**, new with the 2026-08-08 split) — WHAT HOURS, on whatever days the calendar beside it admits. It is the question a window cannot ask: a window is two instants on the line, "mornings" is a pair of clock hands repeating on every day inside it, and the two COMPOSE (measured: open feed 115 → date=7d 7 → +morning 4; clearing the clock returns to 7 with only `from`/`to` on the wire). Menu = five presets over two native `time` fields. `tzOffset` is `getTimezoneOffset()` verbatim so morning means the READER's morning — the same promise `resolveWhenLocal` makes for "today" — and a wrapping pair (22:00 → 06:00) is nights, written server-side as two ranges since one BETWEEN matches nothing. ⚠ NOT part of the spoken `FilterSpec` (the seat has a `when` clause and no clock), so unlike the date and identity buttons this lens has no twin to drop and nothing arrives here from a lens reply — if the seat is ever taught hours, this is the state it would write. State: `TOD_OPTS`/`todWin`/`todKey`/`todMark`/`todTitle`/`setTodWin`/`applyTodCustom`; the date lens's own were renamed `DATE_OPTS`/`dateWin`/… in the same pass. The bundle is FOUR buttons now (measured 42/42/42/40 in its row), calendar to the LEFT of the clock as asked), **IDENTITY** (`.feed-stream__who`, `person_search` + the count of picked seats → `?authors=<csv>`; menu = a debounced `refs/search?kind=entities` field over the picked chips — faces, click to drop — over the results as EntityAvatar + name + @handle. Picks union and the menu stays open for several; it filters the EXACT seats picked, never their masks, per the 2026-08-04 seat-strict doctrine) and **SORT BY**. The time and identity buttons write the same parameters the spoken lens's `when`/`authors` clauses do, so each drops its spoken twin when used and yields to an arriving spoken one — one clause, one place, and the box never states a filter the stream is not under. Menu content is TELEPORTED to `<body>`, so every rule inside these plaques states raw `--indigo-*` tokens: the head box's `--fhead-ink`/`--fhead-rule` dials do not reach outside the box (see [gotchas.md](../../specs/gotchas.md)). `.feed-stream__heading` is deleted; the name is the box's bar title. **The well changed with it in two places:** `.feed-stream-pane` took `position: relative` (the box's containing block — and deliberately NO z-index, or the pane becomes a stacking context and the flyout's 3002 starts ranking against it as a unit, the trap `.feed-page` already documents), and the well's `padding-top` came BACK as `calc(var(--fhead-h, 118px) + 20px)` — the box's measured height (published down from `setup()` as a px custom property, same reasoning as `--post-square-max`) plus its 10px resting offset plus the 10px gap to card₁, so at rest the stream begins exactly where it began under the band (measured: box top = 10px inside the pane, box height 117, well padding-top 137, gap box→card₁ = 10). It does NOT follow the box down: the reserve is the head's HOME SLOT, and a stream that reflowed while you dragged would turn the drag into a resize — drag the head away and the reveal it leaves is where it came from) and one `.feed-stream__well` (`overflow-y: auto`, bottom padding `calc(var(--frieze-h) + 12px)` — sized to clear the frieze footer band the container hovered over until that band's 2026-08-04 retirement; the breathing room at the window floor stays, and — since 2026-07-25 — the SCROLL BED: **`background: var(--indigo-2)`** then **`var(--grey-4)` on 2026-08-05** (user ask), flush with the container and its two frieze edges, and — after an afternoon of walking on 2026-08-06 (`--grey-5`, a sunk tray; `--grey-3`, a lit page) — **back on `var(--grey-4)`, the plaque, in the ask that also removed the well's side padding and took the frieze lips to the same tone**. Those three go together: bars, container, head band and bed are ONE material with no edge anywhere between them, so the box is continuous lip to lip.
