The step did not vanish, it MOVED — the last ask of the day put the CARD at `--grey-3`, one above the bed — and that is the right way up: a lighter bed makes the box a page with darker sheets on it, a lighter card keeps the box reading as one material with its own frieze edges. The bed is the only part of the field a reader ever actually SEES — it covers the container edge to edge — so leaving it indigo while the box went neutral would have made that change true in the stylesheet and invisible on screen. The tonal step it used to hold under the cards is not lost, it changed sign: the `--indigo-1` cards separated by HUE and by their own outline rather than by sitting a shade above their bed — and after 2026-08-06 the step is back and pointing the other way: a pale card on a `--grey-4` bed. Both parts moved again on 2026-08-07 (two user asks): the LINES went `--grey-6` ("from indigo to grey") and then the COAT went warm — `--brown-1` on "just the background", and an hour later the minted **`--light-cream` (#FCF3E0)** — so the card is a cream sheet on a cool plate, edged in neutral. **It became THREE LAYERS in the last ask of that day**: the cream coat + its `--grey-6` border, then a VEIL over them (`.post-square::before` — `--grey-3` **`--grey-2` at 65%** (it walked `--grey-3` 70% → 60% → `--grey-2` 80% → here on 2026-08-07) with `backdrop-filter: blur(2px)` and a **2px `--grey-1` border at full strength** of its own, filling the padding box so the card's line keeps its own tone, turning its OWN 7px corner (= the card's 8px radius − its 1px border — a clip cannot bend a border), `pointer-events: none` so it eats no clicks), then the CONTENT above both (`.post-square > *` at `position: relative; z-index: 1`, with `isolation: isolate` on the card so that pair of z-indexes cannot mean anything outside it). **Measured** off the rendered card, left edge inward: `x=1` `rgb(158,158,158)` the card's `--grey-6` line, `x=2` `rgb(248,247,246)` the veil's own hairline, `x=3+` `rgb(242,239,234)` the veiled field, against a bed of `rgb(245,245,245)` outside. ⚠ So **card and field are only THREE levels apart and the card is the DARKER of the two** — inverted from the arrangement held since 2026-08-06 (darker sheets on a light page, not pale sheets on a grey plate) and the thinnest margin this pair has run at. The card is stated by its outline rather than by tone: **TWO LINES, dark outside and light inside**, a bevel whose hairline is the brightest thing on the surface. Legitimate as a very quiet register; the dial that widens it is the FIELD, not the wash (`--grey-4` held the wash for one setting at 13 levels). The veil's border shows anything at all only because `background-clip` defaults to `border-box`: the wash paints under it, so the border tone composites over its own washed self. It went `6px --light-cream` at 50% (*"thick transparent borders"*) → `1px --grey-2` at 80% (*"waay thinner"*) → **`1px --grey-1` at 80%**, and the first swap is a TONE lesson rather than a width one — a border in the COAT's own tone is capped by how far the wash has moved the coat, since it can only give some of it back, while a border off the card's axis states whatever value it likes. One pixel of a free tone says more than six of a capped one. ⚠ **THE ONE RULE TO KEEP FROM THE WASH'S WALK.** It ran `--grey-3` 30% → 50% → `--grey-2` 50% → `--grey-3` 70% → `--grey-4` → back to -3 → -4 → **-3 again**, and the repeated `--grey-4` settings are not dithering — they were measured against **different beds**. The first time the bed was `--grey-4` too, so the card's coat converged on the field it lies on (`rgb(232,229,224)` against `rgb(224,224,224)` — eight levels of red, NONE of blue), the step vanished and the card's shape fell entirely to its outline. Then the FIELD moved (`--grey-5`, then `--grey-2`), and against a `--grey-2` bed the same wash read 13 levels darker: a clear object again, just inverted. **A wash may not be, or approach, the bed's tone — the rule is the relation, not the token.** Measure after each change, the dials interact. At 70% the coat is doing HUE and the veil is doing VALUE. The veil tints only the card's OWN exposed coat, since every inner panel is content and sits above it (the pit keeps its flat `--grey-1`, the title plate its `--grey-1`, the rail its `--grey-4` tray), so the veil reads in the gutters BETWEEN panels, which is where a card's coat is visible at all. The blur is nearly a no-op by construction and that is understood, not overlooked: a backdrop filter samples what is painted behind it, and behind this one is a flat fill, so it shows only as a faint perimeter haze where the border's tone is pulled a pixel or two inward — raising the radius widens that edge and does nothing to the middle. Both traps (paint order putting an `::after`/`::before` veil over sibling TEXT, and flat-backdrop blur) are in [gotchas](../../specs/gotchas.md) — hue is doing work again, but carried by the OBJECT this time rather than by the field. For the one day between those asks the coat and the line were both neutral and the line alone was load-bearing; do not thin it without moving one of the two tones. It **walked the whole colorway in one day**: -4 (deep, when the cards were full-bleed and the bed alone had to separate them), then -3, then -2 once the card gained its own `--indigo-4` outline — the outline took over the separating, so a dark field underneath was doing that work twice. -2 was also the one step NOT in the frieze bars' tone mapping of the day (1/3/4), so the bed read as its own quiet layer rather than echoing an edge — an argument that has expired several times over since 2026-08-05: the bed is neutral (`--grey-4`, briefly `--grey-5` and `--grey-3`, back to `--grey-4`) and the bars kept no indigo but their WAVES for two days — the lip walked -6 → -5 → -4 → `--grey-4` on 2026-08-06 and draws nothing at all. **2026-08-07 reversed that**: the bars are `--indigo-8` plaques under `-1`/`-3` waves, with the lip following the plaque, so the indigo on this surface is now the FRAME (four frieze edges + the head box) and the bed and cards are what carry no colorway. The head band above wears the container's coat, whatever it is — `--indigo-1` from 2026-07-25, `--grey-4` since 2026-08-05, following by construction, since the band's whole definition is *the container showing through the stream* (its `--indigo-3` bottom edge stays: that line states where the band ends and belongs to the colorway, not to the plate) (it was transparent over `.feed-container` before the glass, and states that same tone explicitly since (E)), so the box reads as chrome over its field, and the cards are light plaques lying ON that field. What SEPARATES one card from the next has moved with the bed, and the cards cast no shadow, so this is worth stating exactly: it was a tonal step while the bed was `--indigo-2` (cards a shade above their well), it became hue plus the outline when the bed went `--grey-4` under `--indigo-1` cards, and since the end of 2026-08-06 it is a tonal step PLUS a line — a `--grey-3` card on the `--grey-4` plaque, edged all round in `--indigo-4`; 2026-08-07 then took the line to **`--grey-6`** and the coat to **`--light-cream`** (`#FCF3E0`, via one hour on `--brown-1`), so it is a tonal step plus a line plus a HUE the object carries. **The well also carries SIDE and TOP padding** (end of 2026-07-25 — it had neither for most of that day, when the cards were full-bleed): the side padding is the gap between the frieze bars and the cards — `10px`, then `5px`, then `0` ("remove completely"), then `3px` (all four on 2026-08-06, the last "just a little little"), and **`8px` since 2026-08-07** (*"a little more padding on the sides from the frieze bars"*). Three pixels was a SLIVER, not a gap: the smallest reveal that keeps the card's own border from LANDING ON the frieze bar, which was the whole job while the bar was `--grey-4` and the card's edge was the only line at the seam. What changed the job is the same day's other ask — the bars went `--indigo-8`, and two objects that far apart in tone need SPACE rather than a hairline of daylight; at 3px the pale card looked stuck to the dark bar. The `0` pass is why the bar's lip draws nothing — at zero the card's edge and the bar's met as one line at the seam — and the lip stayed plaque-toned through every later setting, so the gap does the separating. At `8px` against the `10px` vertical rhythm the reveal is now NEARLY EVEN where it was deliberately uneven; the last 2px are not obviously an improvement, since the sides are read against a hard vertical edge and the top/bottom against another card. Note this is no longer 2026-07-25's full-bleed arrangement either way: that pass zeroed the card's side borders and radius *because* they would collide with the lip, and the card has been a complete four-edged box throughout this one. The top padding is the gap under the head band, which the first card sat flush against until it was added. **The head band's negative side margins are exactly `-1 x` that padding** (`margin: 0 -3px 10px`) — they cancel it so the BAND stays full-bleed lip to lip while the CARDS keep the sliver; at `0` they must be `0` too, or a negative margin with nothing to cancel pushes the band out over the frieze bars. The top padding can only ever affect that first card — every card below it is spaced by the stream's flex `gap` — and `10px` matches that `gap`, so the bed's reveal was one uniform margin on all four sides of every card (measured: head-band→card₁ = 10px, card₁→card₂ = 10px). **The sides broke that symmetry deliberately on 2026-08-06**: 5px at the lips against 10px vertically, which is the one asymmetry on this surface and the one to restate if the padding is ever touched again. The bottom padding is the exception and has a job of its own: it cleared the frieze footer band the container hovered over (band retired 2026-08-04; the clearance stays as the last card's breathing room at the window floor). It narrows `.feed-stream`, which is the element the square ceiling is measured from (615 → 595px at a 1440 viewport) — the `ResizeObserver` picks that up on its own and `--post-square-max` follows, so nothing else needed touching) — so the page itself never scrolls (verified: `documentElement.scrollHeight` stays 900 at 900px tall with 30 cards / 4854px of stream in a 770px well). `useStateHolder` runs with **`trackScroll: false`** + `trackContainer(wellEl, 'feed')` for that reason, and remembers the unfolded card in `selectedKey`; unlike the legacy page it **pre-selects nothing** — with the panel inline there is no empty side column to fill and an auto-unfolded first card would only push the stream down. `min-width: 0` on the pane, the well and `.post-square__name` is **load-bearing geometry, not tidiness** — see the `min-width: auto` note on the `/feed` row. **The cards stopped being the legacy ones on 2026-07-25** (they were verbatim copies until then) — two changes, shape and body: **(A) FULL-BLEED (then GAPPED), SQUARE-CEILINGED squares.** A card's horizontal extension is **100% of the column** — `.feed-stream__well` lost its `12px` side padding (`padding: 0 0 calc(var(--frieze-h) + 12px)`) and `.post-square` its `border-left`/`border-right` and `border-radius`, so the card ran frieze lip to frieze lip and the body got the container's whole width to portray itself in. **The well took side padding back at the end of that day** — `10px`, halved to `5px` and then removed entirely on 2026-08-06 (so the cards are full-bleed once more, but drawn as complete boxes), to open a gap between the frieze bars and the cards (see the well's own note above) — so the cards are no longer literally full-bleed, though they still fill 100% of the column *inside* that padding. **The card was then drawn as a complete box again**: a border on all four edges plus `border-radius: var(--radius-md)` (11.9px as computed; **a flat `4px` since 2026-08-06 — two user asks that day, `--radius-sm` (7px, the step the card's inner boxes turn) and then "even less"; flat px because there is no platform step below -sm and minting `--radius-xs` for one box would state a rhythm that does not exist**), and the side borders run within `3px` of the frieze lips, the well's side padding having gone to `0` and back to a sliver that same day, replacing the `border-left/right: 0` + `border-radius: 0` + `rgba(var(--ink-rgb), 0.16)` of the full-bleed pass. The side border had been zeroed *because* it would have collided with the frieze lip it touched; the well's padding removed that constraint, and with it the reason the card drew only two of its four edges. It landed as `1px solid var(--indigo-4)` — one step DARKER than the bed, so the outline read rather than dissolved — and **settled on `--indigo-3`** at the end of the day. Lighter *and* thicker is one move rather than two opposing ones: a hairline needs contrast to register, a wider band does not, so the outline could drop to the colorway's lip tone and still read — as a drawn EDGE rather than a dark line. That also puts the card's outer border on the same ink as its inner one (the pit's) and as the frieze lips, which was the division of labour on this surface until 2026-08-05/06 — **-1 coats container and card, -2 is the bed, -3 draws EVERY line, -4 is left to the frieze bars' wave layer** — and every one of those four steps has since moved (grey-4 coat, grey-3 bed, -4 lines, -4 also the lip); what survived is the SHAPE of the rule — one coat, one bed, one line ink — including the last of those, which came within one ask of being two (a bevel: colorway on bottom + left + inside, neutral on top + right) before the user put all four edges back on `--indigo-4`. The weight is **EVEN on all four edges since 2026-08-06** and, after two asks on 2026-08-07 ("slightly thicker" → "a little thinner"), **1.5px** — exact at 2×, floored to 1px at 1×, and a real position rather than a compromise: the inner lines are 1px, so the edge has to outweigh them to read as the card's outline, and 2px made it the heaviest line on the card by double once the dividers inside it thinned. Its INK spent an hour of that day out of `--grey-6` — to `--grey-7`, following the dividers, then `--grey-8` (a "one tone down" read as darker) — and came home on the third ask ("clearer"). **So the card's oldest rule is intact on every LINE it draws** (inner and outer alike, `--grey-6`, only the weight telling them apart) and what the day actually added is a second group that is not lines at all: the two DIVIDER PLATES, `--grey-7` for the rgb hairline's rules and `--grey-8` for the frieze band's plaque. An edge a step under its own inner lines read as a box around a box. Move the lines together; the plates move on their own. It was UNEVEN from the end of 2026-07-25 (`border-bottom-width: 2px`, a hairline box with a heavier FOOT; 2px all round before that read heavy, the border being the card's only line). What retired the foot is that the bed stopped being something to sit ON: at `--grey-3` it is LIGHTER than the card, so a weighted base under a box resting on nothing darker read as a shadow with no caster. The old rationale, kept because it is the reason the shape existed: one weighted base was enough to seat the card on a darker bed, written as a 1px box plus a single overridden edge rather than a four-value `border-width`, because that is what the intent says. Verified across the stream: `1px` top/left/right, `2px` bottom, one distinct border color `rgb(176,190,197)`, 0 cards deviating. Its **coat is the CONTAINER's coat** (2026-07-25): `background: var(--indigo-1)`, the same plaque tone the feed box and its frieze lips wear — NOT the generic white `--paper-card` every other square on the platform (`.label-square`, the legacy feed) uses. A post square was a piece of this container, cut from the container's material, so card + bed + edges made three tones of ONE colorway (`--indigo-1` card ⇔ `--indigo-4` well ⇔ the frieze bars' `1/3/4` wave stack). **Since 2026-08-05 the card is the only one of the three left in it** — bed and plate are `--grey-4`, the bars' waves settled at `-4`/`-6` — so the card reads as an object ON the plate rather than as more of it, which is the arrangement that ask was after. Its height is **`min(content, width, 60vh)`**: the square is a **CEILING, not a fixed shape** — a short post stays a short band whose height simply follows its content, a long one grows down until it is exactly as tall as it is wide *or 40% of the window, whichever comes first,* and stops. That is `max-height: min(var(--post-square-max, 100cqw), 60vh)`. **The `60vh` is the absolute cap (2026-07-26 — the number is a DIAL and has been turned three times: `30vh` on 2026-07-25's fifth pass, opened to `40vh` the same day)** and it is there because *a square is a ratio, not a size*: the width ceiling alone says nothing about how much SCREEN a card may take, so on a wide column (or a short window) a square card is simply a tall card — at 615px the cards were 615px tall against a 852px slot, one card per screen, and the stream stopped reading as a stream. `min()` is the operator, not a second `max-height` declaration beneath the first (which would replace it, not narrow it). Nothing else in the card had to change: both limits bear on the same flexible pit, so whichever bites trims the body and spares the foot. Measured at 900px tall: every card `max-height: 360px`, `360 / 900 = 40.0%` exactly, ~2 cards in the well. **`--post-square-max` is the column's width MEASURED in JS** (a `ResizeObserver` on `.feed-stream` in `setup()` writes `clientWidth` onto the element, live across drawer toggles and window resizes) and it comes FIRST on purpose: the pure-CSS statement of the same rule, `100cqw` against `container-type: inline-size` on `.feed-stream`, is correct but **all-or-nothing** — a container-query unit an engine does not understand takes the whole declaration down at parse time, leaving the cards with *no ceiling at all* and letting a 7 000-character post render at its full natural height. A px custom property cannot fail that way; the `cqw` fallback covers the one frame before the observer runs. Either way the width has to come from the ANCESTOR — an element cannot read its own width in its own `max-height`, and the well above is off by its padding/scrollbar. `aspect-ratio: 1` would have been the wrong tool: it *forces* height = width and would leave short posts sitting in a dead white field. The card is a flex **column** so the clip lands where it should — `.post-square__byline`, `.post-square__head` and `.post-square__foot` are `flex: 0 0 auto`, `.post-square__pit` is the one flexible track (`flex: 1 1 auto; min-height: 0; overflow: hidden`), so when the ceiling bites it is the **body** that gives way and the byline's author + the foot's tallies (a post's ORIGIN) are never what gets cut off. Verify by ratio, not by eye, and **measure every card, not the first few** — and check that nothing ELSE in `.feed-stream` is taller than the column is wide, which is how the unfolded panel hid in plain sight: `[...document.querySelectorAll('.feed-stream > *')].filter(el => el.getBoundingClientRect().height > colW)` must come back empty. Measured before the `30vh` cap, 30 cards at three viewports (1440/1920/1280 → columns 615/844/546): worst `height − width` = **0px**, nothing over-tall in the stream, and clicking anywhere in a card changes neither. Since the cap the width limit is the *slack* one at these columns (360px ceiling vs 615px width), so `height > colW` stays empty for free — assert on the window instead: `maxCardHeight / window.innerHeight` must read `0.40`. **(B) The body is RENDERED MARKDOWN, compressed.** Post bodies are markdown, so the pit no longer prints the raw excerpt as text — it mounts `MarkdownBody` (the platform's one pipeline, `ref-display="auto"` since 2026-07-31 — was `micro`: bare node refs to embeddable URLs / media files bloom into the teal `NodeMini` panel right on the card, everything else stays a micro chip; `![[…]]` / `-[[…]]` sigils overrule per ref, see `docs/concepts/references.md`) over `postBody(item)`. **And it is the WHOLE post, not a preview** (2026-07-25, second pass): the stream loads `feedService.getPublic({ limit: 30, body: 'full' })`, so each item carries its full markdown body and a card is an *actual post container*; `excerpt` (the 280-char slice every other feed consumer reads) stays only as the fallback for an item the server resolved no body for, and only that path wears the truncation ellipsis, appended INTO the markdown source (a separate `<span>…</span>` would render as a stray glyph after the last block). Two consequences: the pit **scrolls in place** (`overflow-y: auto`, the well's own thin rail) — which is what settles the two rules the card lives by, *no taller than it is wide* and *holds the whole post*: hidden overflow would make the square a truncation, a scroller makes it a **window** — and the pit carries `@click.stop`, so the body is a reading surface (select text, follow a link) while the head strip and foot stay the card's select-toggle. ⚠ **THE CARD CLOSED IN ON IT on 2026-08-10** (two user asks): the pit's margin — the CARD's gap around the reading area, not the pit's own breathing room — halved from `6px 7px 7px` to **`3px 4px 4px`**, and its radius went **`7px` → `3px`**. Seven was `--radius-sm`, right for a panel FLOATING on a surface and wrong for a frame set INTO one, especially once the pit sat nearly flush with the card's own `4px` corners; three is the tightest step still visibly a curve. Its `padding: 8px 10px` did NOT move — what shrank is the frame, not the sheet the prose sits on, which is why the change reads as a bigger reading area on an unchanged card. **The pit is dressed in two tones** (end of 2026-07-25): `background: var(--grey-1)` floor + `border: 1px solid var(--indigo-3)`. They pull in opposite directions on purpose — the floor is the one tone deliberately OUTSIDE the container's colorway (a tint that made the pit a darker patch OF the card, `rgba(var(--ink-rgb), 0.05)`, became a flat near-white that is a different material set INTO it), while the line is the colorway's own lip/hairline ink, the same one the frieze bars draw the container's side borders with, so the card's inner edge and the box's outer edges are one line. The border landed in the same pass the **card's drop shadow was removed** (`box-shadow: 0 1px 3px` gone from `.post-square`, and the `box-shadow` half of its `transition` with it): the card has nothing to lift off now that the well beneath it is a darker bed, the tonal step alone separates card from field, and a cast edge only muddied the 10px gap between adjacent cards — so with no shadow, the indigo-3 line is what states where the reading area begins. **The surface was then flattened outright** (end of 2026-07-25): the pit's own inset carve — the `.label-square__pit` recipe, an inset dark shadow at the top over an inset white lip at the bottom — is gone as well, so **nothing on a card casts anything**. Depth from two directions at once (a recessed pit inside a flat card on a flat bed) is what left the surface unresolved; tone and line state everything now — grey-1 floor against the indigo-1 card, indigo-3 border around it, indigo-4 around the card. Verified over the whole stream: 0 elements with a computed `box-shadow` other than `none`, across all 30 cards **and every descendant** — the assertion has to walk the subtree, since removing the card's own shadow says nothing about the pit's. **`:breaks="false"` is load-bearing.** Post bodies are hard-wrapped at ~72 chars; under the platform default every source line becomes a `<br>` and the paragraph lays out at the AUTHOR's wrap width — text that looks cut off at half the container (measured: prose filled ~50% of the pit, `br` count in the dozens; after, `br=0` and lines fill **96–99%**). Body type went `0.82em` → **`0.95em`** with `line-height: 1.5` in the same pass — a reading surface, not a preview strip; every size in the compressed tier is relative to it, so the whole markdown scale moved with it. **It settled at `0.88em` / `line-height: 1.6` at the end of 2026-07-25**: a notch smaller fits more of a post inside the card's ceiling AND gives justified prose more words per line to distribute (the narrower the measure, the uglier the gaps justification must open), while smaller type wants proportionally more leading. `.post-square__md :deep(.markdown-body)` is the **compressed tier**: every construct keeps the meaning it has in the full renderer — headings mono-bold on a tight scale (h1 `1.14em` → h4-h6 `0.94em` uppercase), `disc`/`decimal` list markers and heading weights **restated** because Quasar's reset strips them globally, inline `code` as a tinted chip, fences and tables with their **own `overflow-x` scroller** (a long line must not widen the card, which at a percentage-sized container would widen the feed box itself), thin-rule blockquotes, capped images — but the vertical rhythm is tightened and first/last margins zeroed, so a heading reads as a heading without spending a fifth of the square on its margins. **How MUCH tightened was walked back at the end of 2026-07-25**: the step was a single `0.45em`, which bought space at the cost of legibility — at that setting a heading sat almost on the paragraph above it and the blocks ran together into one undivided slab, so the reader had to parse structure the spacing should have been showing. The step is **`0.85em`** now, list items went `0.08em` → `0.3em` apart (at `0.08em` a list read as one wrapped paragraph with bullets in it), `hr` `0.5em` → `1em`, and headings carry a deliberately **ASYMMETRIC** margin — `1.15em` above, `0.35em` below (was an even-ish `0.55em`/`0.2em`). The asymmetry is the part that does the work: a heading binds to the text it introduces and separates from the text it follows, where an even margin leaves it floating between two blocks. **Prose is JUSTIFIED** in the same pass — `text-align: justify` on `p` and `li` ONLY, so the block's left and right edges both line up with the pit's border and the card reads as ordered; headings, tables and fences stay flush-left (justifying a two-word mono heading would stretch it across the whole measure). `hyphens: auto` rides along and is **not optional at this width** — justification works by opening word spaces until the line fills, so on a narrow measure without hyphenation one long word pushes a line into rivers of white. That in turn forced a **`hyphens: none` correction on `code, pre, kbd, samp`**: the property inherits, so the justified `p`/`li` handed it to every inline code span and `entity_type` broke as `entity_-` / `type` — a hyphen a reader cannot distinguish from one the token really contains. Prose may be hyphenated; anything meant to be typed back exactly may not. Verified across the live stream: all 1491 code/pre elements compute `hyphens: none`, and the pit still does not overflow horizontally (`word-break: break-word` breaks unbreakable tokens without adding a glyph). See [gotchas.md](../../specs/gotchas.md). Two rules had to GO for this: `-webkit-line-clamp: 4` (a box clamp counts LINE BOXES inside one `-webkit-box`; rendered markdown is a run of block elements it cannot measure — the square ceiling is the limit now) and `white-space: pre-wrap` (source newlines are the parser's job; preserving them would put a blank gap between every rendered block). **(C) The kind LEGEND is gone; classification is the LABEL PATH** (2026-07-25, third pass). The head strip's right-end tag (`doc` / `post` / `fork` / `comment`, `.post-square__tag` — rule deleted) and the icon's `is_doc` / parent-id branching both **asserted a category the card had derived**; every row here is a POST instance, and what kind of one it is is something the element already CARRIES. The icon is now uniform `article`. **The head strip was then dressed to match the pit** (end of 2026-07-25), so the card reads as two panels rather than a strip over a box: `.post-square__name` sits on its own **PLATE** — a `--grey-1` background rimmed `1px --indigo-3` at `--radius-sm`, i.e. literally the same floor and the same line as the markdown pit below it — with the title ink at `--indigo-8`; `.post-square__icon` is `--indigo-3` (it was `rgba(var(--ink-rgb), 0.55)`); and a **full-bleed vertical rule** `.post-square__head-rule` separates icon from title. That rule runs the strip's whole height, touching the **byline band's hairline above** (the card's top border until the author moved up there — see (F)) and the pit below: `align-self: stretch` alone stops at the strip's *padding* edge, so it also carries `margin: -7px 0` to cancel `.post-square__head`'s vertical padding — the same device as the nav bar's `.nav-divider-v`. **Keep that margin in step with the head's padding** or the line stops short of one edge (verified: rule height 37px = head height 37px, 0 overhang either end, on every card). Meeting the byline's horizontal hairline at a right angle is what ties the two strips together as one head rather than two stacked bands. Below the pit, a rigid `.post-square__labels` rail draws one chip per label the post holds, **root to leaf** (`.post-square__label-step`, steps joined by a drawn `›` inside a single `<router-link>` to the leaf label, so the chip stays one link and one tab stop; `@click.stop` keeps it off the card's select-toggle). A doc says it is a doc by carrying `PATHCHAIN › DOC` — nothing is invented. **This rail deliberately does NOT use `utils/labelChain.js#displayLabels`**: that filter drops the bare `PATHCHAIN > DOC` marker and the whole `PATHCHAIN > POST` family precisely BECAUSE a dedicated tag rendered them, so on the one surface that has no tag it would hide exactly the classification the rail exists to show. The only thing dropped is a chain that is a strict PREFIX of another present (`PATHCHAIN > POST` beside `PATHCHAIN > POST > ORIGINAL` — the longer path already draws every step of it); user classification sorts ahead of the `PATHCHAIN` plumbing families. Measured on the live feed: the doc card carries `CLAUDE › DOCUMENTATION LAYERS › CONCEPTS`, `PATHCHAIN › DOC`, `PATHCHAIN › INSTANTIATION`, `PATHCHAIN › POST › ORIGINAL`, an ordinary post the last two **(D) The author is a CONDENSED IDENTITY BLOCK** (2026-07-25; it left the foot for a band of its own the same day — see **(F)**). The foot's origin row used to run `<strong>username</strong>` beside an `EntityMicro` hash chip — three items in the same row as the post's chip and the three tallies. It is now ONE unit: `EntityAvatar` (34px face) on the left, and beside it the author's **name over their `@handle`**, the whole block a single `<router-link>` to `/entities/:id` (`@click.stop`, `.post-square__identity`). The entity hash chip went with the old run — the avatar routes to the same profile and the row already carries one address (the post's). The name is `display_name || username` at `0.78em/700` in the author purple, the handle `@username` at `0.66em` mono in dim ink, stacked at `line-height: 1.15` (any more and the two lines read as two facts rather than one block); each caps at `18ch` with an ellipsis. The block is **`flex: 0 0 auto`**, NOT shrinkable — left to the flex floor the identity collapses to `cla…` / `@cla…` on a 593px row with 300px to spare (see [gotchas.md](../../specs/gotchas.md)). It also carried **`max-width: 55%`** while it sat in the foot, because the post's adaptive `MicroChip` shared that row and a long display name would have taken the whole run; **that cap went with the move to the byline band** — the only thing beside the block there is the org badge, which the two `18ch` line caps already leave room for. The photo arrives **inline** on `item.author.photo` (the feed resolves it once per distinct author per page), so the stream costs no extra request — and so does the **ORG BADGE** (`item.author.org`, second pass the same day): when a post was published through an organization MASK, an `OrgLogoChip` follows the identity block, drawn OUTSIDE its `router-link` (a link inside a link is invalid, and the two point at different things — the person and the organization). The API now answers a mask with the PERSON's name, handle and face plus an `org` card, so the mask's flat `<person> @ <org>` display name becomes a mark you recognise instead of a string you read; `org.self` (an org publishing as itself) suppresses the chip, and `.post-square__byline .org-logo-chip { margin-left: -3px }` (`.post-square__foot …` before the move) pulls it in against the row's 8px gap so identity + badge read as one unit. **(E) The head band went FLAT** (end of 2026-07-25): `background: var(--indigo-1)` — the container's own coat, the same tone the feed box, its frieze bars' base and the post cards wear — and the heading ink is `--indigo-8` (`.feed-stream__heading`, replacing the platform-wide `text-accent` teal, which read as a foreign colour dropped onto the container; the sub-line stays the generic dim ink, since a caption sharing the heading's ink flattens the two into one block). The **glass is gone with it** — `rgba(var(--indigo-3-rgb), 0.82)` + `backdrop-filter: blur(5px)` (and its `-webkit-` twin) are deleted, and so is the now-unused `--indigo-3-rgb` token, whose only consumer this was. An opaque coat shows nothing through itself, so the blur was dead weight the compositor still paid for; the surface's ONE deliberate exception to the flat-plaque rule is retired and the rule is now universal here. The band stays `position: sticky; top: 0` — the pinning was never the glass's doing, and being opaque is what now lets it HIDE rather than soften the cards passing under it — and the `2px solid var(--indigo-3)` bottom edge is what states where it ends, doing more work now that band and cards share a tone. Verified: `backgroundColor rgb(236,239,241)`, heading `rgb(69,90,100)`, `backdropFilter: none`, band top − well top = `0` at rest. **(F) The author moved ABOVE the title — the BYLINE BAND** (end of 2026-07-25; **A SPLIT STAMP since 2026-08-09's last byline ask** — the day walked FOUR arrangements (ruled columns → two stacked dense lines → a one-line ≥1024px fold that lived hours → here, ONE layout at every width). The title plate and the open-post link are DELETED (the CAP names the card; the flyout's links are the viewer door); what stands is: `.post-square__face` (avatar 24px, its own link to the profile) beside `.post-square__byline-lines` (`flex: 0 1 auto` — not a grower) holding the `__identity` link (name + @handle INLINE, 0.72em/0.6em, both to `/entities/:id`; the NAME letters in **`--cyan-9`** and name+handle light **`--cyan-14`** together on hover — 2026-08-09 user ask, both levels minted for it: the byline is the card's one coloured voice, and one link lights as one object) + `OrgLogoChip` 14px (its own link to the org) + the heat plate, with `.post-square__byline-ago` tucked under; then the full-band SEAM RULE (`.post-square__byline-rule`, stretch + `-4px` margins, carrying the right-packing `auto` margin); then `.post-square__byline-when`, **the moment as TWO STACKED MICRO CHIPS** — the detailed datetime chip (`event` icon) over the city chip (`place` icon, only when shared), real `MicroChip`s routing to `/moments/:id` (the tiny world map's page; a moment-less legacy item degrades to the span face), compressed by `.post-square__moment-chip` (0.58em / 30ch / deep `min-width: 0`). **The whole band letters in Nasalization** (`--font-display` + 0.02em on the band; `mono` came off the handle and the plates so inheritance reaches them; the chips take the cap's named-chip deep-override). **2026-08-10 reworked the LEFT side in two asks.** (1) The HOP CHIP left the who-line and now LEADS the ago line — `[1 hop] · 3h ago`, a flex row with a `.post-square__ago-dot` separator drawn only when there is a chip to separate. It was the widest of the three qualifiers, a bordered plate standing between the name and the card's edge on the one line the name has to fit on; down here it costs the name nothing, and how FAR the author is pairs honestly with how LONG ago they published. Its `-3px` pull-in went with the move (nothing to its left to hug), and the 0.62em stayed OFF the row and on its two text children — the plate is dialled in `em` against the BAND, and a size on the row would have compounded it to 0.36em. (2) **The NAME NO LONGER TRIMS; the HANDLE does.** Both spans carried `max-width: 16ch` + default shrink, so a narrow card took them down together (`Dream Ope… @dreamo…`). Now the name is `flex-shrink: 0` with `max-width: 100%` (the line itself, so it ellipsizes only after the handle is gone) and the handle is `flex: 0 1 auto; min-width: 0` — `overflow: hidden` on `.post-square__identity` is what makes the order enforceable, since a non-shrinking name would otherwise push the badge off the card. On MOBILE (≤600px) three more rules finish it: name 0.66em / handle 0.55em / trust 0.54em, and `.post-square__byline-when` becomes `flex: 0 20 auto; min-width: 0` so the moment chips (rigid by argument on desktop — 2026-07-25's "the place string landed at a different length on every card") give first. Measured at 390px with a 28-char name: before, handle 0 and name clipped 114/160; after, handle ellipsized 81/109 and name whole at 160. Band measured 41px; the media budget constants follow, now 277 / expanded twin 311 (see the card-chrome note below). The paragraphs below describe the 2026-07-25 columns it grew out of.) The identity block of **(D)** opened the FOOT until this pass, which put the person *after* the whole post; a card reads **who → what → about → how it's doing**, so the block (avatar, name over handle, and the org badge that belongs to it) is now the card's FIRST strip, `.post-square__byline`, and the foot is left holding what the post has DONE. Card child order is `cap | frieze | byline | rgb | labels | rgb | pit | rgb | foot` since 2026-08-10 (see the divider note and the FOOT note below). The band is `flex: 0 0 auto` like the head and foot — who published a post is not something the square ceiling may trim, so only the pit gives way — with `padding: 6px 9px` and the row's `gap: 8px`. Its divider from the title strip is a **full edge-to-edge hairline**, `border-bottom: 1px solid var(--indigo-3)`, and it needs **no negative-margin trick** to reach both edges the way `.feed-stream__head` and `.post-square__head-rule` do: `.post-square` has no padding of its own, so the band already spans the card's whole content box and its border runs from one side border to the other. `--indigo-3` because that is the ink EVERY line on this surface is drawn in (the card's outer border, the pit's rim, the frieze lips, the head's vertical rule) — a divider inside the card is the same line as the ones around it, at hairline weight against the card's 2px foot and the head band's 2px edge. Verify edge-to-edge by geometry, not by eye: at a 1440 viewport the band measured `160.89 → 753.89` inside a card of `159.89 → 754.89` — inset by exactly the card's own 1px side border on each side, i.e. flush against both. **(G) The band was then DENSIFIED and given the moment** (2026-07-25, second byline pass). Three changes, one idea — the band is a single statement of provenance (*who*, then *when and where*), so it is drawn tight and ruled rather than spaced: **(i) the face shrank** `34px` → **`28px`** and the block's inner gap `7px` → `5px`, its two lines each dropping a step (`0.78em` → `0.74em` name, `0.66em` → `0.62em` handle) with their cap `18ch` → `16ch` — at 16ch the lines are near enough the same length to read as one stamp, and a name cut there was already being cut at 18. The band's own padding went `6px` → `5px` and its gap `8px` → `6px`; band height `47px` → **`41px`**, which the pit (the card's one flexible track) takes back. **(ii) A full-bleed VERTICAL hairline closes the author section** — `.post-square__byline-rule`, the same device and ink as `.post-square__head-rule` one strip below: `align-self: stretch` + `margin: -5px 0` to cancel the band's vertical padding, 1px `--indigo-3`. **Keep that margin in step with the band's padding.** It stops exactly 1px short of the band's bottom *by construction* — the band's border-box includes its own hairline, so the vertical rule meets the horizontal one instead of crossing it (measured: rule top = band top exactly, rule bottom = band bottom − 1px). **(iii) `.post-square__moment` — the TIME/SPACE CHIP** follows the rule, and **replaces the `.post-square__time` "1h ago"** that used to close the head strip (that rule is deleted; `absoluteTime` is no longer exposed to the template, `momentTitle` calls it). A post is minted at a moment and a moment is a *when* and a *where*, so it is one chip of two stacked lines, on the identity block's own `line-height: 1.15` rhythm — the band then reads as two parallel two-line facts either side of the rule. Top line is the relative time (`0.72em/700` — what you scan a feed by, carrying the weight the author's NAME carries opposite); under it, a `0.6em` mono line showing **`moment.place` when the post has one** ("Mexico City, Mexico") and **`moment.datetime` when it does not** ("Sat, 25 Jul 2026 · 11:26 PM"), with a `place` / `event` icon saying which you are looking at and the `title` carrying BOTH (a located post would otherwise have no way to tell you its date). Space wins because it is the more particular fact — but the date is the ordinary branch, since coordinates are opt-in. Both strings arrive RESOLVED from `GET /feed` (see api-reference: `moment.place` / `moment.datetime`), never formatted here — the date especially, which is built from `time_utc` in UTC server-side so every chip and viewer on the platform states one moment identically; only an item with no moment at all falls back to a local `toLocaleString`. The chip is `flex: 0 1 auto` + `min-width: 0` — **the one part of this band designed to give way** (the identity opposite must not; see (D)), so a long place ellipsizes rather than pushing the author out. Verified across the live feed: 30 cards, both branches present (`place` icon → "Mexico City, Mexico", `event` icon → the formatted date), every band `41px`, every avatar `28px`, no `.post-square__time` left. **(H) The band ABSORBED the head strip; the label rail took its place** (2026-07-25, third and fourth byline passes). The card's children are now **`byline | rail | pit | foot`** — `.post-square__head`, `.post-square__head-rule` and `.post-square__icon` are all DELETED. Everything that IDENTIFIES a post shares one ruled run, read left to right: **`[open] │ [author+badge] │ [when/where] │ [title]`**, the three `.post-square__byline-rule` hairlines dividing it. Two strips of metadata stacked over the body were saying the same kind of thing twice. `.post-square__go` LEADS the run because it is the one CONTROL among facts (at the head strip's right end it read as one more chip); `.post-square__name` CLOSES it, keeping its `--grey-1` plate (which is what still marks it as the card's subject rather than a fourth fact) at `0.74em` and **`flex: 1 1 auto; min-width: 0`** — it takes every pixel the three facts leave and ellipsizes into it, so the band is exactly as wide as the card whatever the title's length (12 of 30 ellipsize; `bandOverflow` = 0). The uniform `article` icon did not survive the move: in a band this dense it stated nothing the card does not. **THE MOMENT CHIP STOPPED SHRINKING** in the same pass — `flex: 0 1 auto` → **`flex: 0 0 auto`**, capped instead at `max-width: 32ch` on its detail line. With two shrinkable items sharing one row, flex split the overflow between them and the place string landed at a different length on every card ("Mexico City," / "Mexico City, Mexico" / "Mexico City, Mexi" down the column); exactly one item per row should absorb, and the title is the one that loses nothing by being cut. Size that `ch` cap against the WIDER branch — the 27-char date, not the 19-char place — plus the icon and gap, which sit inside the capped box: at `24ch` the date clipped on 19 of 30 cards while `innerText` still read whole, so assert on `scrollWidth > clientWidth`, never on text. See [gotchas.md](../../specs/gotchas.md) for both. **The LABEL RAIL then filled the strip the title vacated** — `.post-square__labels` (a bare wrapping run BELOW the pit) became **`.post-square__rail`**, directly under the byline: a post's classification belongs with what identifies it, not appended after the reading. It is a **rounded rectangle that scrolls HORIZONTALLY**, and the two properties are one decision: the BOX is the card's third panel on the same recipe as the pit and the title plate (`--grey-1` floor, `1px --indigo-3` rim, `7px` radius — **the floor moved to `--indigo-2` one pass later, see (I)**), so classification, subject and body are three cuts of one material; and `flex-wrap: nowrap` + `overflow-x: auto` (chips `flex: 0 0 auto`, their old `max-width: 100%` dropped so they slide past the edge instead of compressing to stubs) is what keeps it ONE line — wrapping made the rail's height a function of how many labels a post carries, four paths becoming three rows and pushing the body down on a height-capped card. Same thin scrollbar language as the pit and the well, `height: 4px` since it is horizontal. Rigid (`flex: 0 0 auto`) like the foot: the ceiling trims the body, never a card's classification. Spacing is stated ONCE per panel — rail `margin: 6px 7px 0`, pit `margin: 6px 7px 7px` (it gained the top margin the head strip's padding used to supply) — so the pit sits correctly under the band on a post with no labels, where the rail is `v-if`'d away entirely. **(I) Final band order, a two-line title, and the panels' lines** (2026-07-25, fifth pass). **The open button moved to the RIGHT END** — the band is `[author] │ [when/where] │ [title] │ [open]`: the three FACTS in the order a post is read, then the one CONTROL, standing apart instead of leading. **The title plate became a fixed TWO-LINE BOX**, denser (`0.74em` → `0.7em`, tracking `0.03` → `0.02em`, padding `2px 7px` → `2px 6px`) and built from three rules that only work together: the plate is a flex box centring its content on BOTH axes; an inner `.post-square__name-text` span does the clamping (`display: -webkit-box` + `-webkit-line-clamp: 2` + `overflow-wrap: anywhere`, the guard for a title that is one unbroken token; a titleless post reads `(untitled)` since 2026-07-27 — it no longer borrows its CONTENT node's source path); and `min-height: calc(2.6em + 6px)` (2 × line-height + padding + border — **keep all three in step**) sizes the box to two lines ALWAYS. Each does one job: a title too long for one line now WRAPS rather than being cut at the first line's end (two lines of mono uppercase is most of a sentence, one is a fragment), a short title sits CENTRED in the middle of the identical box, and because the box never changes size the band stays uniform down the column — the same card-to-card raggedness fixed for the moment chip in (H), and more visible here since the plate is a drawn box. `-webkit-line-clamp` is legitimate HERE and wrong in the pit (see gotchas.md): it counts line boxes inside one `-webkit-box`, which is exactly what a single text node is, where the pit holds a run of block elements it cannot measure. Verified: all 30 plates `31px`, 19 titles at one line / 11 at two / **0 over two**, `textAlign: center` + `justifyContent: center` + `alignItems: center`, band `41px` → `42px` and uniform. **The panels then took their own lines and tones**: the rail's floor went `--grey-1` → **`--indigo-2`** (a tray you SCAN takes the bed tone; the pit's near-white is a surface you READ, and it keeps `--grey-1`), and **the rail gained a HEAVIER TOP edge** — `border-top-width: 2px` on an otherwise 1px box, a shallow tray with a lip. The pit took the same weight in that pass and **gave it back the next**, returning to an EVEN 1px frame: it is the READING area, and a weighted edge above the text reads as a rule the prose hangs from rather than as the top of a box. The heavy-top device is the rail's alone; the pit's own uneven-border relative is the card's 2px foot, which is a different idea (seating the whole card on the bed). Finally the rail moved inside a full-width **`.post-square__rail-strip`** (`padding: 6px 7px`) carrying a `border-bottom: 1px --indigo-3` — the **edge-to-edge hairline dividing classification from content**. It must live on the strip: the rail is inset by that padding, so a border on the rail would stop 7px short of both sides. That settles the card's grammar — **DIVIDED by full-bleed lines (the byline's, the strip's), PANELLED by inset boxes (rail, pit, title plate)**, two devices that are never mixed. The pit keeps `margin: 6px 7px 7px`, so it sits correctly under whichever line precedes it — the strip's, or the byline's on a post with no labels, where the strip is `v-if`'d away entirely. The **chips inside the rail** took the other half of that step: `background: var(--indigo-1)` (they were transparent, so they wore the tray's tone and the rail read as one field with outlines drawn on it) and a heavier 2px BOTTOM — the chip-scale reading of the card's own uneven border, and deliberately the opposite edge to the rail's and pit's heavier TOP: a panel is seated by the edge where it meets what came before, a chip RESTS on the tray beneath it. Rail height `27px` → `29px` with the thicker chip foot, still uniform. Verified: chip background `rgb(236,239,241)` at "1px top / 2px bottom" across all 82 chips, rail background `rgb(207,216,220)`, rail "2px top / 1px rest" and pit an even "1px top / 1px rest", strip hairline `1px rgb(176,190,197)` spanning `161–754` inside a card of `160–755` (inset by exactly the card's own side borders). Verified after (H): card order `byline | rail-strip | pit | foot`, every rail `27px` tall with 11 of 30 actually scrolling (up to 86px of overflow), 0 stale `.post-square__labels`/`.post-square__head` elements, and the container still measuring exactly `50%` of the track. **Block embeds in a card** (2026-07-26): a `![[pathos:…]]` reference renders the full Mini panel whatever the surface's inline chip tier is, so the compressed tier restates `.element-mini { max-width: 100% }` — the Mini's own 480px cap is a prose MEASURE, and a figure is not prose — `ElementMini` drops it itself now for any media node (`.element-mini--media`), on every surface (an embedded video otherwise sits in a 480px box with dead card beside it). This is how a video LINK node shows as a PLAYER in the feed: post body → `MarkdownBody` → `ElementMini` → `NodeMini` → `EmbedFrame`, with the frame **bounded by the card's media budget** — `.post-square__pit` publishes `--media-max-h` (`max(120px, calc(min(var(--post-square-max, 100cqw), 60vh) - 264px))`, derived from the card's own ceiling; it was a flat `30vh` here, `20vh` under the 40vh card, until 2026-07-26) and `EmbedFrame` turns it into the width cap via `--embed-ratio` so the ratio survives exactly — measured 1.778 at 1440×900, 1600×1080 and 1280×720). The cap is about REACH, not looks: at the pit's full width a 16:9 frame is taller than the pit itself (579px wide wants 326px in a 229px window), so the card opened on the player's top third and its PLAY BUTTON, dead centre, fell below the fold — the one control an embed exists for could not be clicked without scrolling first. A video cannot be made to fit a card whose whole height is capped, so the frame gives way instead; the pit still scrolls for the rest. It only works because `GET /feed?body=full` answers with RAW markdown (corrected the same day — it used to hand over the excerpt's stripped string, in which no chip and no block embed survives). **THE CARD'S TWO INNER HAIRLINES BECAME TWO DIFFERENT DIVIDERS on 2026-08-07** (a run of user asks). It began as a PAIR of `FriezeBar slim` bands, the second `vflip`ped so the two reflected about the label lane; hours later the mirrored half was replaced by an **`RgbHairline`** (see its row above), so the card now reads byline │ **frieze band** │ rail strip │ **rgb hairline** │ pit │ foot, and the hairline is drawn only WITH the rail (a labelless card has the band alone and the pit follows it). The swap is the more interesting statement: the two full-bleed dividers stopped being the same device twice — the band is the platform's MOTIF, saying a new kind of thing starts here, and the flat rule closest to the reading area can be a third of its height. (`FriezeBar`'s `vflip` prop and the `-mirror-vmirror` masks were minted for that mirrored half and have **no consumer** now; both are kept — they are the fourth corner of a four-way asset square that was already three-quarters built.) Both `border-bottom`s went at that point — the band IS that line, given height and a motif — ⚠ **and the BYLINE's came back on 2026-08-10's last ask** (`border-bottom: 1px solid var(--grey-6)`, "a classic grey-6 hairline between the author/moment section and the label section"): the frieze had long since moved UP to the cap, and the three devices that held this seam after it (an `RgbHairline`, then half of a label bracket, then nothing at all for one pass) ended with it bare — so the plain rule is back where it started, at the card's inner 1px against its 1.5px edges. It goes on the BAND and not on the rail strip below it, because the strip is inset `7px` now and a divider has to run lip to lip; the strip's own `border-bottom` stays gone. Meanwhile the frieze's plaque walked DOWN THE GREYS one user ask at a time — `--grey-6` (the card's line ink, which is what let the hairline it replaced simply go: the band was that line with height and a carve), then `--grey-7`, then **`--grey-8`**. Every step buys the same thing, and it is the carve rather than the tone: the waves are two very light accents and a groove only reads when the plate is well under what is cut into it (1.7:1 on -6, 3.5:1 on -8). What it costs is the "the band IS the card's line, thickened" reading — two steps under that ink the band is its own object, a dark plate laid across the card, which is the relation the feed container already has with its `--indigo-8` edges one family over. The MOTIF is the one thing here not made of the card's material — an `--indigo-11` → `--teal-11` gradient down the wave through the new `--frieze-bar-wave-two-paint` dial (born `--teal-11` → `--indigo-11`, and REVERSED on the mirrored band so indigo faced the lane from both sides and mint faced out; **the ramp itself was flipped to indigo-down-to-cyan on 2026-08-10**, a user ask, once that twin was long gone). The flip runs the band AGAINST the three rgb hairlines below it, which are cyan-down — deliberately: they are different devices (a motif with height vs three flat lines), and opposing them makes the card read as a cyan low point at the reading area closed by cold indigo top and bottom. The flat `--frieze-bar-wave-two` fallback follows the ramp's TOP end and moved with it. The band stands just above `slim` (`--frieze-bar-h: calc(var(--frieze-h) * 0.55 + 2px)`, ~10.4px of plate at a 900px viewport) after **four settings in one day** — `slim` (0.5) → `0.4` ("slightly thinner") → `0.65` ("too thin, I cannot see the friezes well") → here ("thinner, but not so thin the friezes get deformed"). What the walk settled is that the number to think in is ONE MOTIF ROW of the masks' 13-row grid: ~0.54px at `0.4`, ~0.67px at slim, ~0.87px at `0.65`, ~0.80px here — **under ~0.7px a meander stops being a pattern and becomes a texture, which is what "deformed" means in numbers**. `slim` stays ON at that height (it is what drops layer one — two interlocking waves in 10px go straight back to texture). It is `flex: 0 0 auto` — the bar states a height, but in this flex column anything that MAY shrink will, the pit being the only part meant to give. **IT WEARS TWO RULES since 2026-08-10** (user ask): `border-top`/`border-bottom`, `1px solid var(--grey-6)` — the CARD'S line ink, not the plate's, at the card's lightest weight (its outer edges run 1.5px). That is the point of them: two greys down, the band had become its own dark object laid across the card, and an object wants an edge in the same ink as every other line on the square, which BINDS the plate to the card instead of leaving it floating. ⚠ **The height dial pays for them** — everything here is `border-box`, so 2px of border eats the plate and drops a motif row to ~0.65px, under the ~0.7px floor above; the `+ 2px` in `--frieze-bar-h` gives the rules their own space and leaves the motif exactly where the four asks put it (verified: outer box 12.39px, content 10.39px = the old band). Keep that term through any future move of the `0.55`. The pit's `--media-max-h` budget subtracts the band as `0.55 × --frieze-h` **plus 2px folded into its px constant (280 → 282)** and counts the hairline in that constant at a flat 6px — once, since 2026-08-10's last rail ask deleted the label bracket's thin pair; move either and move that with it. The constant stands at **276** — the rail strip's padding and the band's 1px rim against the pair's −8, plus the label strip's TWO classic hairlines (+1 each) and the padding tightening 4px → 2px (−4) that came with them. **Its expanded twin is `310`** (`276 + 34` of the well's own padding, on `.post-square.is-expanded .post-square__pit`): ONE number written twice, and the copy that lags is always the expanded one — see [specs/gotchas.md](../../specs/gotchas.md) for the grep that catches it. The card's OUTER BORDER went 1px → 2px → **1.5px** across two asks the same day (even on all four edges, same `--grey-6` ink, inner lines left at 1px). The half step is a real position, not a compromise — the outline has to outweigh the card's 1px inner lines to read as its edge, and at 2px it was the heaviest line on the card by double once the dividers inside it thinned. Measured: exact at 2× (3 device pixels), floored to 1px at 1× (the headless driver reports `1px`), so it degrades to the full step down rather than to something wrong. **THE CAP — the card's own header strip (2026-08-07, user ask).** A thin band ABOVE the byline, now the card's first child, answering what the byline never did: what this post IS and what it CAME OUT OF. Split by the byline's own vertical-rule device (`.post-square__cap-rule`). It was **70/30** — both cells `flex: 1 1 <pct>` + `min-width: 0`, measured 391/168 in a 559px card — and is now **FACTS / CONTROLS** (2026-08-07, user ask): the control lane is `flex: 0 0 auto` (it measures its own buttons and nothing else) and the fact cell `flex: 1 1 auto` + `min-width: 0` (it takes the remainder and is the one that gives). 30% of a feed card is far more than three 20px glyphs need, and the lane held its share whatever was in it — measured 473/82 in the same 556px cap, which is ~85 px of title handed back per card. The rule now stands where the pressing starts rather than at a fixed fraction, which is the honest place for a line dividing what you READ from what you PRESS. **RULED AT EVERY SEAM since 2026-08-09** (user ask): an EXPAND LEAD cell (`.post-square__cap-lead`, one `expand` button at the card's far LEFT edge) opens the strip behind its own cap-rule, and the same rule runs between the lane's three controls (stretch + `-2px` margin against the lane's 2px padding) — `control │ facts │ pin │ skeleton │ flyout`. The lead is the **hash lens**: press → the feed filters to this post's own chain address (`?hash=`, the same clause Talavero issues for a spoken address), the single card draws FULL-HEIGHT (`.is-expanded` — board home slot to container floor, `--feed-well-h` minus the well's own paddings, ResizeObserver-published; the fsck `--static` `expanded-card` witness holds the subtraction to the well's padding line), the head box auto-slides to its top berth, and the lane wears a `post <hash>…` chip; a second press (or the chip) releases. `is-on` keys off the ACTIVE hash — local or Talavero-spoken — prefix-matched against the card's address. The WIDE side reads `[icons] Comment on «chip» :: <name>`: the ICONS state the kind off the feed's new `origin.relation` — `sym_o_post` for an original, `sym_o_comment` for a comment, and for a fork **both** `sym_o_post` and `sym_o_alt_route`, a fork being a post that came out of a post and wearing the two marks together; the ORIGIN CLAUSES are one per pointer (comment before fork, so a post that is somehow both names both parents), each carrying its parent as a **`MicroChip`** rather than a `#41` — the parent is an element with an address, the chip is how this platform states one, and a comment's parent is as often a NODE as a post, which is why the generic chip is used and not `PostMicro`; the NAME closes the run — `item.title` or **`post #<id>`**, a fallback deliberately different from `cardName`'s (the title plate falls back THROUGH the parent because it has no room to state a relation; the cap has already stated it, so what is missing there is only the name). The other side is the CONTROL lane — three glyphs: a **pin** (`pinService`, target type `skeleton`: the same PINS skeleton the pins widget reads, with `pins-changed` bubbling FeedStream → FeedPage → MainLayout's `pinsRefreshKey` through a listener now bound on the ROUTER-VIEW); an **`sym_o_orthopedics`** link into `/skeletons/:id`, the post read as its skeleton; and **`open_in_new`** (2026-08-07, user ask), that SAME destination without the navigation — it points the right-hand flyout at this post's skeleton, so the slots open BESIDE the feed and the reader keeps their place in the column. It is a button, not a link, for the reason the title plate is one: it opens a panel on this page rather than going anywhere, and it TOGGLES (a second press closes it). FeedPage owns that state — `@open-skeleton` sets `flyoutRef` to the bare id, which is one of the two forms `SkeletonFlyout` already accepted from `?flyout=`, so the flyout learned nothing new — and hands it back as the `flyoutId` prop purely so the button can wear `is-on`. Opening it clears `selected`: the box shows one thing, so the skeleton face and the post face close each other. Pinned state is ONE `pinService.list()` per stream load into a `pinnedIds` Set — never a `pins/check` per card, which would be thirty requests to draw thirty tacks — and the tack flips optimistically with a reload-the-truth catch. All three controls are chromeless until hovered (this strip is read far more often than pressed) and the pin adds `is-on` in `--accent`. The whole band is set in **`--font-display` (Nasalization)**, the first thing on this card to wear it, at `0.62em` / `0.02em` tracking — the `.nasalization` utility's own `0.05em` is meant for headings with room — over `--grey-9`, the card's deepest ink, because this strip is the post's NAME. ⚠ **THE NAME ITSELF STEPS UP ON DESKTOP** (2026-08-10, user ask): `.post-square__cap-title` takes `font-size: 1.16em` of the band (≈`0.72em` of the card) inside `@media (min-width: 1024px)` — **FeedPage's own desktop gate**, the same number the byline reads, so the card never invents a second breakpoint. The rest of the strip stays at `0.62em`: the lift is for the one cell a reader is looking for, not for the chrome around it. Below the gate the name sits flush with the lane, where the strip is too tight for it to buy anything but an earlier ellipsis (the cap's other two cells are rigid, so every point comes out of the title's own `flex: 1 1 auto` slack). It changes TYPE, not the band — `CAP 24` in the media budget is unchanged at both widths (verified) — and ⚠ the media query is written **below** the base rule, since a query adds no specificity and would otherwise lose to source order (`specs/gotchas.md`). **THE ORIGIN CHIP NAMES ITS TARGET** (2026-08-07, second ask). It printed a hash — `mono`, capped `max-width: 13ch` because `MicroChip` is container-adaptive by design and given the room took 64 characters, leaving the post's own name as `post #…` — which made the one line that says WHICH post is being answered the one line a reader had to parse an address to follow, while the card's own name sat one span to its right in plain words. A chip whose target is a POST now carries `:display="originName(target)"` — the feed's new `origin.*.title` under exactly `capTitle`'s rule, `title || post #<id>`, so both ends of the clause are named the same way — and `:icon="'sym_o_post'"`, the glyph the cap states a post with two spans to the left (`MicroChip` defaults to `KINDS.posts.icon` = `edit_note`, the post-as-document drawing, which is a different mark). It gains `.is-named`, which lifts the character cap (`max-width: 100%`) and letters it in `--font-display` down through a `:deep(.micro-chip__hash)` — two classes + the scope attribute against the component's own single class, so no `!important` is needed — with `min-width: 0` so a long title ellipsizes instead of holding MicroChip's 6-character hash floor. The ceiling moved UP a level instead: `.post-square__cap-origin` is `max-width: 50%`, so the parent may take half the strip and the card's own name keeps the rest whatever the card's width. A chip pointing at anything that is NOT a post gets no name and keeps the hash face it always had — a NODE has no title, and a CLAIM parent is named by its STATEMENT, which the feed does not read. **The `RgbHairline` moved up with it** (third ask the same day) — it closed the CAP, dividing the header from everything under it, unconditional where it used to be `v-if`'d on the rail — went **DOWN on 2026-08-09** to stand between the PIT and the FOOT (a plain cap `border-bottom` holding the seam for hours) — **and became a PAIR on that day's last ask**: one band back under the cap with its filling FLIPPED (`--cap` modifier, `--rgb-hairline-fill` restated reversed — indigo up, cyan down), one over the foot, the two cyan ends facing the content so the pair reads as a BRACKET rather than a repeat (children: `cap | rgb-hairline--cap | byline | frieze | rail-strip | pit | rgb-hairline | foot`); the rail is closed by nothing, its strip padding and the pit's 6px top margin being the lane. The pit's media budget absorbed the cap: the px constant went 271 → **295** (the cap measures 24 and is a one-line strip by construction) and the hairline's 6px inside it is no longer conditional. **THE CARD CASTS AGAIN** (2026-08-07, user ask, "very very subtle") — flat since 2026-07-25, now a two-layer CONTACT shadow: `0 1px 2px / .045` (the dark line where sheet meets plate) over `0 2px 5px -2px / .05` (ambient). The negative spread is what keeps it a contact shadow rather than a float — reach is `blur/2 − spread + y` = 2.5px down and 0.5px sideways, so it dies inside the 10px card gap and the 8px bed reveal, which is exactly what the July shadow failed to do (the constraint was never "no shadow", it was "nothing that reaches the neighbour"). What makes it right now is the ask before it: the bed went back to `--grey-4`, so the card is the LIGHTER object again, and a pale sheet on a grey plate is the thing that HAS a contact shadow. Measured: darkest rendered pixel 212 on a 224 bed, clean bed by 3px. ⚠ **The alphas are chosen against the FIELD** — on a near-white bed they vanish, and the honest answer at `--grey-2` was no shadow at all. Same ask: rounder corners and the outline **`1.5px` → `1px`** — one reasoning for both, that the card stopped being a flat plaque held by line weight and became a sheet stated by cast and silhouette. The radius went **`4px` → `6px` → `8px`** across two asks. **`7px` is the one value to SKIP**, which is why the second step was 6 → 8: the pit, title plate and label rail all turn at exactly `--radius-sm` (7px), and a container turning at the same radius as the boxes inside it reads as a tray moulded around them rather than a sheet they are laid on — at 6 the outer corner was a pixel tighter than its contents, at 8 a pixel looser, and looser is the better reading. The **veil (`::before`) follows at `border-radius: 7px`** — card radius minus card border, the padding box it is pinned to. It had none before and got away with it because `overflow: hidden` clipped its square corners for free; a clip cannot bend the veil's own 2px light border, which thickened into the bend rather than following it. Invisible at 4px, not past it — keep the three numbers in step. **The VEIL walked four settings** the same day, and the walk matters because SWAPPING ITS TONE CHANGED WHAT THE ALPHA DIAL DOES. Measured card each time: `--grey-3` @70% rgb(242,239,234) luma 240 R−B 8 → @60% rgb(243,239,232) luma 239 R−B 11 ("like 15% less strong") → `--grey-2` @80% rgb(245,244,240) luma 244 R−B 5 → **@65% rgb(246,243,236) luma 243 R−B 10**. Read the last two rows: fifteen points of alpha moved luma by ONE level and DOUBLED the warmth — arithmetic rather than coincidence, since `--grey-2` (luma 245) is `--light-cream`'s own brightness (243) within two levels, so mixing them can only change HUE, where `--grey-3` (238) sat five levels below the cream and moved both at once. **Three dials, none a substitute for another: the TONE decides what the ALPHA is a dial FOR** (at this pairing it is very nearly pure WARMTH — go back to `--grey-3` or lower if the card should get DARKER rather than cooler)**, and the FIELD sets how far the card stands off its bed** (~19 levels here). ⚠ When re-measuring: `backdrop-filter: blur(2px)` pulls bed pixels into the composite at the card's rim (sample the middle), and a sample just ABOVE a card now lands inside the contact shadow, ~7 levels dark. | |
| `skeletons/SkeletonFlyout.vue` | ⭐ **The skeleton flyout — the feed's floating detail box** (2026-07-26; GENERIC + renamed/moved from `posts/FeedPostFlyout.vue` on 2026-07-31, closing the deferred G8 item: props are now `item` (a feed item → mounts `FeedPostPanel`, the rich post face) OR `skeleton-ref` (ANY 'skeletons/<hash>' address or numeric id → mounts `SkeletonTable` since 2026-08-10 (dashboards phase 1) — the dense Field \| Type \| Data face at max detail — in a `.skeleton-flyout__generic` scroll wrapper; `SkeletonMini`, which this branch mounted 2026-07-31→2026-08-10, stays the compact face post bodies wear); the header titles itself after what it resolved — "Post's Skeleton" / "<NAME> Skeleton" — via the face's `resolved` emit; deep link `/#/feed?flyout=<ref>` opens it on any skeleton; CSS class `.post-flyout` → `.skeleton-flyout`) — the feed's detail panel, back after the in-place unfold was deleted on 2026-07-25. The CONTENTS are not new: `FeedPostPanel.vue` unchanged (full excerpt, every bound element as a Micro/Info chip, thread provenance, labels, tallies, the two "open the post" links). Only the FRAME is: the panel was built as a `position: sticky` side column, so the flyout undoes with `:deep()` the three things that assumed one — `sticky` (offsets it 12px in a non-scrolling parent and nothing else), the `calc(100vh - 90px)` ceiling + `340px` floor (stated against the WINDOW, where the well is a fraction of it), and its `pathos-card` white coat and 12px padding (the shell is the card now). Its own `__scroll` track stays the scroller, so the panel's head stays put. Drawn as one of the platform's FLOATING WIDGETS rather than as part of the feed container — it hovers over the page canvas BESIDE that box, the way the stack and pins widgets hover over the right edge — so it is BUILT like them — but **not COLOURED like them** (2026-07-26, second pass). The coat is the neutral **`--grey-3`** (`--brown-1` before) and **every line is `--grey-4`** (`--brown-4` before — a new token, added with this box): a warm edge round a neutral box reads as trim borrowed from another surface, so the coat and the lines moved together. `--grey-3` against `--brown-1` is within a hair in lightness (`#eeeeee` vs `#efebe9`), so what actually changed is the HUE — the box keeps the side widgets' weight and drops their warmth. The INSET CONTAINER was **ONE FLAT TONE** with it for a day — the well sharing the plaque's exact coat, its `--grey-4` rim rather than a tonal step stating where the container began (tone-stepped → line-drawn, the same move the feed's post cards make on the other side of the window). Since **2026-07-27 the well is `--grey-4`**, SUNK one step into the plaque: a floor rather than a region drawn on the box, which is what gives the quoted panel something to be raised out of, and what stops every boundary in the box from being carried by the same 1px hairline. Its rim became a **two-tone BEVEL** in the same pass — **`--grey-5` on the top and left, `--brown-1` on the bottom and right** — one step darker than the fill on the two walls a top-left light cannot reach, one step lighter on the two it strikes as the floor climbs back to the plaque. It is the only depth device available here (the box's single drop shadow is the whole plaque's, onto the page), and it is what the rim was freed to do: four even `--grey-4` lines had gone invisible the moment the floor took that same tone, so an edge no longer needed to state WHERE the container is could state what KIND of edge it is. The lit pair is WARM deliberately — `--brown-1` is the side widgets' plaque coat, the tone this box gave up when coat and lines went neutral together, and at #efebe9 against `--grey-3`'s #eeeeee it is the same value one hue over, so the lip changes HUE and nothing else: warmth catching the light, where the neutral `--grey-3` it wore for one pass drew the same geometry and said nothing about the material. Third warm thing in the box after the frieze waves and the `--brown-8` header ink, and like them it sits on an EDGE, never on a surface. The header bar restates the plaque's coat and must stay in step with it, or the head reads as a separate strip laid on. The **`FriezeBar` band followed on the third pass** (2026-07-26): the flyout sets `--frieze-bar-base: var(--grey-9)` on `.post-flyout__frieze`, the bar's own base dial, so its plaque is neutral too. `--grey-9` is DARK where the bar's default `--brown-4` is a light plaque, which INVERTS the motif — the `--brown-2`/`--brown-1` waves are untouched and now read as the light on a dark strip — and that leaves the band the one carved and the one dark thing in a flat, pale box. What stays BROWN after it is only the wave motif itself and the `--brown-8` header ink that agrees with it, neither having a neutral colorway to move to. So: `--grey-3` plaque, 1px `--grey-4` outer border, `--radius-lg` on all four corners (it floats free of every edge, where the side widgets are anchored to one and round only their inner corners), `overflow: hidden` so the frieze band cannot square them off, and a real all-round `0 12px 38px rgba(0,0,0,.5)` drop rather than the widgets' lateral `--shadow-side-edge` (high alpha for the same reason the feed container's is — it falls on the near-black canvas). **Reframed as a SKELETON VIEWER on the fifth pass** (2026-07-26): the box is a viewer for a skeleton and the post is the INSTANTIATION in front of it, so it is named and glyphed for what it views rather than for what it is currently pointed at. The file, the class and the folder still say `SkeletonFlyout` / `.post-flyout` / `components/posts/` DELIBERATELY — it takes a `GET /feed` item and mounts `FeedPostPanel`, so a post's skeleton is all it can show today; the rename and the move belong with the work that makes it accept any skeleton, since names promising a genericity the props do not keep are worse than the mismatch. Head = the shared `.dock-bar` info box with its white coat and bottom hairline overridden to one uniform plaque coat (as on `StackPanel`), brown-8 glyph + title, brown-4 meta (`#<skeleton_id>`). The glyph is **`kindFor('skeletons').icon`** (`schema`), read from `utils/kinds.js` the way `SkeletonMini` reads it rather than written into the template, so re-glyphing skeletons platform-wide carries this header along. It was `edit_note`, which is not a generic document icon but literally `KINDS.posts.icon` — the platform's POST glyph on a box that shows a skeleton. The title reads **"Post's Skeleton"** (2026-07-26) — "Post information" before, and the `aria-label` on the `role="dialog"` moved with it so the accessible name still matches the visible one. The COMPONENT keeps its own name (`SkeletonFlyout`, `.post-flyout`), and so does the prose here and in `specs/codemap.md`, which describe the box rather than quote its head, and a `.dock-bar__action` close button; then a `FriezeBar` band — **`slim`** since the fourth pass (2026-07-26): half height and the brown-1 wave alone, on the `--grey-9` base, the widest band in the smallest box having been the one thing on the plaque shouting; the mask refit and halved carve that keep it legible at 13.5px are the FriezeBar row's business — then the information container holding the panel — the stack/pins wells' GEOMETRY (8px reveal where it meets the band, since a carved wave needs more air than a flat plaque edge; 6px elsewhere) without their recess: `--grey-3` floor, `--grey-4` rim, and a **`--grey-5`** scrollbar thumb on the panel's own `__scroll` track (it was `--grey-4`, quiet at 1.14:1 on the pale well; the well taking that same tone left the thumb drawn in its own track's colour, i.e. drawn nowhere, so it went a step darker to 1.34:1). GEOMETRY IS NOT HERE — `.feed-flyout` on `FeedPage` places it; this component fills what it is given (`height: 100%`). Dismissal: the header's ×, Escape (FeedPage's `keydown` listener), or re-clicking the trigger that opened it. **2026-07-27 — the panel's CONTENT section is now the node itself.** It used to print `item.excerpt` (280 chars, markdown punctuation stripped) in a `panel-excerpt` div under a `panel-title` line. Both are gone: the section mounts the same **`NodeMini`** a `![[pathos:nodes/…]]` block embed drops into a post body, in its new **`raw`** mode, so the post's CONTENT node arrives as the element it is — round pill and address chip in its header — with the markdown AS WRITTEN in its body (the foot, and the votes and tallies it carried, came off NodeMini on 2026-07-27). Three things follow. (1) It costs **one request** where the box used to cost none: `/feed` hands over `content_node` as `{ id, path, type_id }` only, so `FeedPostPanel` fetches the enriched row from `GET /nodes/by-path` (`file.text`, `embed`, the votes NodeMini's caption thumbs state) — deliberate, the price of quoting the real document rather than the excerpt. It clears first on every change and drops late responses by the path they were requested for. (2) The **post's TITLE rides the Mini's header as a TOOLTIP** (2026-07-27 — it moved INTO the header as text first, replacing the `panel-title` line that had named the same document twice; then the header's title zone became NodeMini's address chip, and `effectiveTitle` retreated to the zone's `title` attribute); a post with no TITLE bound leaves the `Node #332` placeholder standing there, which is the honest label. (3) The flyout **publishes two dials** to the Mini it holds: `--node-source-max-h: 33cqh` caps the whole QUOTED PANEL at a FIFTH of this box — `calc(20cqh - 34px)`, since the dial caps the PANE and the ceiling wanted is on the panel, so it is stated as 20% minus that panel's MEASURED chrome (header band 22px — the 18px round pill plus this box's 2px zone padding — the body's 9px inset, two outer borders; the foot band's 25px came out of the constant when NodeMini lost its foot, 2026-07-27). It was `33cqh` on the pane alone, which put the panel at 42.5% — 327px in a 770px box — and pushed identity, labels and the CTA below the fold.
