The 34px is the one number here that can go stale: it fails loudly (the panel overshoots 20%) but nothing checks it, so restate it if the band padding, the border weights or the round pill's 18px box move. The alternative — a `max-height` on the panel with the pane flexing inside it — needs `min-height: 0` plumbing through MiniPanel's body that every other Mini in the family would then carry for this box. **DENSITY** came with the ceiling (2026-07-27), as `:deep()` rules on `.post-flyout__well`: the quoted panel's paddings roughly halve (header zones `5px 8px` → `2px 6px`, MiniPanel's body inset `7px 10px` → `4px 7px`, the source pane `8px 10px` → `5px 7px`) and its type comes down with them (source `0.78em` → `0.72em`; the 1.62 line-height is untouched, being what makes aligned source scannable). NodeMini's own paddings are drawn for a panel QUOTED INTO a post, where the bands can breathe — under a hard ceiling every pixel of air in a band is a pixel of document nobody reads. Stated as host `:deep()` and NOT as more dials, unlike the colour work: tone is the component's business, density follows from the ceiling THIS surface publishes, and specificity carries it (two classes + the scope attribute against NodeMini's one) with no `!important` — which would not hold for anything reaching a MicroChip internal. (A FOOT rework lived in this block for part of 2026-07-27 — sections dividing the row, `q-space`s display-noned, a 70% chip allowance, per-section `--teal-2` seams turning literal-10px corners; all of it died the same day when NodeMini lost its foot outright. The two Quasar traps it recorded stay true and stay in [../../specs/gotchas.md](../../specs/gotchas.md): `--radius-md` is an `em` token, and `.q-space { flex-grow: 1 !important }` can only be beaten by removal from layout. Header and borders sit outside the scroller) (`container-type: size` on `.post-flyout` makes it the container — safe there, since its height comes from a top/bottom-pinned parent and its width from that parent's box; `vh` math would be a second copy of FeedPage's geometry to keep in step), and all four of NodeMini's dials — `--node-mini-coat: var(--grey-3)` + `--node-mini-rule: var(--teal-2)` + `--node-mini-rule-hover: var(--teal-12)` + `--node-mini-head-ink: var(--brown-10)` (`--node-mini-foot-glyph` was the fifth until NodeMini's foot went, 2026-07-27) (plus two local overrides: **a 2px `--teal-13` BASE on the bottom border** — NodeMini's own uneven-border device restored as the panel's ONE mark of its own, and the chip's device one level up: a light object seated on a mint line. The flyout spent 2026-07-27 arguing itself out of that weight and back into it — the base was flattened to 1px while a ring was doing the seating, a 2px cap on TOP was tried and reverted (the uneven device turned over, on the argument that a document read top-down wants a head rather than a base), and with the ring gone there is no second line outside this one, so the weight is the whole of what makes a base read as a base. **Base and hover are two values of one mint** — `--teal-13` resting, `--node-mini-rule-hover: --teal-12` — which is the CHIP's own rule (an object's own mark and the pointer's are never the same colour at once). They were BOTH `--teal-12` for a day, and that crossing is what moving the base up the accent scale un-crossed; the other way out, hover at `--teal-11`, is drawn for a `--teal-1` coat and reads weakly on a neutral one. Keep them apart if either moves — and **FLAT vote pills**, `background: none; border: 0; padding: 0`. Those pills were the last literal teal on the panel, a `--teal-1` fill and `--teal-3` rim written before coat and lines were dialled, so on a grey panel they read as two coloured patches left behind. The viewer's own vote keeps its semantic green/red in the INK alone) take the quoted node's COAT out of the teal colorway. A 1px **`--teal-1`** `outline` RANG it for part of that day — NodeMini's own default coat, i.e. the one the panel gave up when it took the box's tones, so the node was drawn in the HOST's material and ringed in its OWN, the outermost line restating once everything the flyout had dialled away. It went out when the two reading panes went `--teal-1` themselves: the colorway was back INSIDE the panel, and a ring in the same tone was then a third statement of it round the outside. It is a shade darker than both surfaces it separates — **1.00:1 on the `--grey-3` well** (identical in lightness: HUE is doing all of it) and **1.06:1 on the `--grey-2` coat** — which is precisely why it survived the coat moving under it on 2026-07-27 (on the old `--grey-4` coat it sat BETWEEN the two at 1.14:1) and why no grey managed the job: `--grey-6` shouted beside teal lines, `--grey-3` was the well's own tone and vanished, and `--grey-2` (a thread of light, 1.06:1 / 1.21:1, worn for part of the same day) read correctly but said nothing, a neutral being the one thing that cannot state an edge on a box where hue does all the work. That ring is why `.feed-post-panel__scroll` still carries `padding-left: 2px` beside its old `padding-right: 2px` — vestigial now that the extra edge is a bottom border INSIDE the box, and kept with its lesson because it is the trap any future outline in this track will hit: an `outline` draws outside the border box, the panel's left edge sits exactly on that `overflow-y: auto` track's, and in LTR a scroll container clips everything before its scroll origin — so the ring drew on three sides until the track was given room on the fourth. The LINES are `--node-mini-rule: --teal-2` as of that day's last passes — the outer border AND every inner hairline in one value, at the component's own 1px, a step lighter than the `--teal-3` default because -3 is drawn for a `--teal-1` coat and this coat is neutral. They spent the middle of the day OUT of the colorway with the coat (`--grey-4`), on the argument that ONE line system from the box's outermost edge to the hairlines three levels inside it makes the node part of the box; that lost once the panel's two reading panes went `--teal-1` and its ink `--brown-10`, since with the node's own material back INSIDE it, lines belonging to the BOX read as the box drawing on the node. Seven passes in one day: `--teal-3` → `--grey-5` → `--teal-2` → `--grey-3` → `--grey-5` at 2px → `--grey-4` → `--teal-2`. The `--grey-4` reasoning is kept below — it is the argument this one had to beat, and it is still what the BOX's own lines are drawn in. The tone is chosen for **whose it is**, not for its 1.21:1 on the `--grey-2` coat: the plaque's border, the well's rim and the scrollbar thumb are all `--grey-4` already, so the box has ONE line system from its outermost edge to the hairline between two zones of a panel three levels inside it, and the node reads as part of the box rather than a visitor with its own drawing conventions. **Six passes in one day** (2026-07-27): `--teal-3` (the component default, drawn for a `--teal-1` coat) was the loudest mark in the box → `--grey-5` → `--teal-2`, quiet and read by hue, and inaudible once the ring and the ink were already carrying the colorway → `--grey-3` → `--grey-5` at 2px → `--grey-4`. Two of those are the SAME tone read against a coat that moved under it — `--grey-3` measured **1.14:1 and LIGHTER** than the old `--grey-4` coat, then **1.06:1 and darker** than the `--grey-2` one, and left the panel no structure to read either way — and the 2px pass is where the panel found its ceiling (see the `--grey-5` row). HOVER goes to `--teal-12` (2026-07-27), the accent mint, taking the panel's outer border AND every inner hairline at once. Against neutral resting lines it is the panel's ONE moment of hue, and it answers by **CHROMA, not weight or value** — the `--grey-4` lines measure 1.21:1 on the coat and the mint 1.14:1, both DARKER than it and within a hair of each other, so the hairline holds its weight and close to its value and simply arrives in colour. One line weight throughout, with hue carrying the whole answer, on a box that has spent its entire lightness range on being quiet — chroma being what is left when every tone sits within a few percent in lightness, the same property that makes -12 work as the base under NodeMini's round pill. It was `--teal-10`, the panel's own dark ink, for one pass the same day — emphatic, and wrong in the other direction: lines written to state their divisions QUIETLY became the heaviest marks in the box on the way past them. The component default is `--teal-11`, the paler mint, drawn to lift off a `--teal-1` coat. Dialling hover is mandatory once the resting lines move at all, since the whole system lights from that one value — doubly so now the resting lines are grey, where the `--teal-11` default would flare a mint on a panel with no teal in its lines at all. **THE INK AND THE PANES TRADED PLACES on that day's last passes**, and what they landed on is the arrangement to know: **every WORD on this panel is the BOX's ink, and every reading surface under them is the NODE's material.** The ink is `--brown-10` — the header row through `--node-mini-head-ink` (one value for the row's zone ink, landing mostly on the open glyph now that the round pill and the address chip are their own neutral objects), and the SOURCE PANE's text set on the pane directly (the foot's four glyphs rode `--node-mini-foot-glyph` until the foot went, 2026-07-27). It went `--teal-10` → `--grey-10` → here inside one day: the family's 900 every time, so the ink only ever changed HUE — and brown because the box is brown wherever it is anything at all (its own header is `--brown-8`, the frieze band's waves are brown), so a quoted node lettered in the HOST's family is part of the box where a neutral could only say "not teal". It is two steps darker than that header ink, which is the right order: the box names itself once at the top, and the document it is holding is the darkest thing in it. The SURFACES went the other way — the raw TITLE BOX and the SOURCE PANE are **`--teal-1`**, NodeMini's own coat, where the component defaults to a `--grey-1` near-white and where this panel's coat is the host's `--grey-3`: the node is drawn in the BOX's material and the DOCUMENT inside it in the NODE's, so the colorway lands where the reader's eye actually is rather than on a rim. Both neutrals were worn the same day and neither states a document. `--grey-1` measured 1.04:1 on the coat — a step of lightness too small to read as a step, so the pane looked like a mistake rather than a decision; that is what started the sequence. `--grey-4` INSET it a real 1.14:1: correct as depth and wrong as material, since the document then belongs to the box — drawn in the tone the plaque's border and the well's floor are drawn in — and the panel stops being the one thing in the box with a material of its own. `--teal-1` is 1.08:1 AND tinted, an inset stated by MATERIAL, which is this box's own device: the well is sunk by tone, the panel raised by tone, and hue is what says whose a surface is. Keep the title box in step with the pane — `raw` sets the header FROM the body it heads, and two floors there read as two documents. NodeMini exposes the ink PER ZONE for exactly this reason: the source pane still reads `--teal-10` directly, so what keeps saying NODE after the coat, the lines and the head ink stop is that ink, the `--teal-2` lines, the mint base and that hover. (The foot's glyph/count two-tone read — `--brown-10` glyph, `--teal-10` count, the viewer's own vote excluded — is a 2026-07-27 record now; the foot went the same day, and the raw-mode quote has no caption line for the thumbs that replaced the pills.) The COAT is the `--grey-3` plaque's own tone, so the panel is FLUSH with the box — one continuous neutral surface from the box's edge through the well to the panel, its tinted edges stating the boundary by HUE where a tonal step would have. Both alternatives were worn the same day: `--grey-4` a step UNDER (inset, "quoted FROM somewhere") and `--grey-2` a step ABOVE (raised, the lightest thing in the box). The cost is stated where it lands: the `--grey-1` source pane inside it drops from **1.26:1** to **1.04:1**, drawn by its rim now rather than by its tone. Net arrangement: a `--grey-3` panel RAISED a step out of the `--grey-4` well it lies in and flush with the plaque around that well (all three coat steps were worn on 2026-07-27 — `--grey-4` inset, `--grey-2` raised, `--grey-3` flush, and -3 is what stands; the RELATION changed under it when the well sank to -4 the same day), its own lines `--teal-2` and the BOX's `--grey-4`, a 2px `--teal-13` BASE on its bottom border as its one mark of its own (`--teal-12`, the pale mint, for the day before — A400 is that hue with the chroma up and a step of value behind it, since a 2px edge on one side is a statement and its tone should carry as far as its geometry does; it replaced a 1px `--teal-1` ring that ran all four sides), its two reading panes `--teal-1` at 1.08:1 on that coat (they spent two passes neutral in between — flush `--grey-3`, then `--grey-4` inset — both on 2026-07-27), and everything written on it `--brown-10` bar the viewer's own vote — every surface within ~1.2:1 of every other, holding because nothing here separates by lightness except the document itself, with the two inks the only marks in the box past 10:1 and the only ones carrying words. **2026-08-10 — TRAFFIC LIGHTS, AND THE SHELL BECAME A FAMILY** (user ask, with the dashboard flyout). The `.dock-bar__action` close × at the head's right end is GONE, replaced by a classic light cluster pinned to the LEFT edge with the title centred — the `.dock-window--creation` header layout exactly, promoted from the docks because a flyout is a window like they are and the platform should state a window's controls in one place. **THREE dots** (it opened the day with two — "there is no yellow because a flyout has nowhere to park" — and the same day's minimize ask gave it one): red closes, **yellow retreats the box into the thin band at the TOP of the screen, `MediaTabsBar`, where the floating media viewers park** (user ask: "for the skeleton flyout, make it retreat into the thin header on the top of the screen, where the media viewers retreat too"), green maximizes. That split says something true about the two families — a dock rises from the bar and folds back down into it; a flyout hovers over the page and retreats UPWARD, out of the way of the thing it was describing. Parking is `v-show`, not `v-if`: the box keeps its DOM, so the scroll position survives and restoring costs no request (`FeedPostPanel` quotes the content node through `GET /nodes/by-path`), the same reasoning `MediaViewerHost` uses to keep a parked video playing. The minimized/maximized flags live in `stores/flyouts.js` because the tab bar is on the far side of the layout and props cannot reach it; the box mirrors its own resolved TITLE into that store (`describeSkeleton`, watched `immediate`) so the tab and the window it restores are never two different names. `minimized: true` implies open — a parked flyout has no reachable red light, so the only exits are restore, select another card (which un-parks rather than toggling: clicking a card means "show me this", never "close the thing I cannot see"), or leave `/feed`, which resets the store `onBeforeUnmount` so no tab outlives the page that owns its box. The green light EMITS `toggle-max` and reads back a `maximized` prop — the size belongs to the host by this box's oldest rule, so `FeedPage` holds `flyoutMax` and puts `.is-max` on `.feed-flyout` (`left: 2.5% !important; right: 5% !important` — the container's own left gap to the flyout's right one, i.e. the whole free track width; the vertical band NEVER changes, the box staying between the two frieze strips, and the scroll-follow inline offsets stop being written while maximized). ⚠ the mobile block names `.is-max` too (`.feed-page .feed-flyout.is-max`) — it stands ABOVE these rules in the file, so two equally specific `!important`s would otherwise be settled by source order and maximizing on a phone would SHRINK the box by the desktop 5% inset. Everything the box is DRAWN with — plaque, head, frieze band, well — moved to the shared **`.flyout-window`** block in `css/_components.scss` the same day, unchanged in value; what stays scoped here is what this box CONTAINS (the `--node-mini-*` dials and the `:deep()` densities). The well carries BOTH class names (`skeleton-flyout__well flyout-window__well`) so those `:deep()` rules still hang off the name they were written against, and the tone prose above stays in this component's style block as the family's record. |

The tree grammar + THE MIN PANE SIZE (240×160) live in **`utils/splitTree.js`** — stated ONCE; a restated copy owes an fsck --static witness. Dividers ride the BottomSplitter pointer-drag pattern (pointer capture, clamp, dblclick → half): the clamp holds both panes ≥ the min (verified: a hard-left drag lands at exactly 240px), a split too small for both minimums pins its divider mid-way, and a pane under the min swaps content for its NAME PLATE (ResizeObserver). A bound cell renders the referenced element as its full Mini panel (`shared/ElementMini`), with a pencil button (textable fields — reopens the inline editor) and a clear button beside it. Click an empty cell to edit on the fly: unconstrained/`nodes` fields open an inline text box (`PUT /:id/slot/:name` `{ text }` — first bind mints a real NOTE node; re-edits **version that node** via the ancestor chain, so its viewer shows prior texts); **`moments`-constrained fields open a native DATE input instead** (Thread H, 2026-07-31 — `event` icon, "click to pick a date"): committing calls `POST /moments/ensure` (mint-or-reuse at UTC midnight, unlocated) and binds the returned `moments/<hash>` ref, so BIRTHDAY-style fields fill without hunting for a moment element; any field also accepts a dragged/picked ref (`{ ref }`). Right panel = `SlotRefPicker` (kind-locked search, `entities` included) + **Upload** / **Build post** buttons (open the uploader/maker) + a **fresh tray**: elements minted while populating are delivered by `stores/skeletonBuilder.deliverFresh` (no navigation) and drag/pick straight into a field. `onMounted`→`beginPopulate`, `onBeforeUnmount`→`endPopulate`. |
| `maker/SlotRefPicker.vue` | The builder's search column — kind pills for every referenceable kind incl. moments + links (locked to the selected slot's declared kind), debounced `GET /api/refs/search` (matches by title/name AND hash prefix), results clickable (fill selected slot) and draggable (`application/x-pathos-ref` payload) onto slot rows |
| `maker/LabelMakerDock.vue` | ⭐ Docked **label maker** (`stores/labelMaker.js`; nav-bar create bundle, `label_important` icon; wears `.dock-window--creation` — see MakerDock). Left: `labels/LabelTreeMini` in **appendable** mode (2026-07-19) — every unraveled layer ends with an add row: inline create on owned layers, suggest/fork hand-off on foreign ones; root layer ends with an inline new-root input. Right: action panel on the selection — breadcrumb chain, sys/yours badges, then **child** / **brother** (both `LabelMiniMaker` with `can-suggest`; brother targets the parent), **rename** + **move** (owner-only, `PUT /labels/:id` — move picks the new ancestor via a second mini tree or detaches to root), **suggest** (foreign trees only: name + optional message → `POST /labels/:id/suggest` mints the owner's poll; "Fork instead" escape in the panel), **fork tree** (`POST /labels/:id/fork`, selects the owned copy so it opens in the maker ready to grow), **delete** (owner-only, non-system: danger panel → `DELETE /labels/:id` deletes the whole branch; flash reports the count). Flashes render regardless of selection (a delete clears it). No selection → plant a new root. |
| `posts/PostCommentItem.vue` | ⭐ Recursive comment card: inline reply embeds `PostMakerSurface` (draft parented to the comment), vote panel, and a "N comments / forks" dropdown (`reply_count + fork_count`) that merges the child's own `listChildren` + `listForks` — comment AND fork trees unravel recursively. Honors the `unravelPath` prop (`?unravel=` chains): expands along the chain and halos the target (`.is-unravel-target`, luminous-yellow like the nav return halo). |
| `nodes/NodeForkItem.vue` | Recursive fork-NODE row for the node viewer's Forks pane (`GET /nodes/:id/forks`): owner, excerpt, `node #id` link, and a per-fork "N forks" toggle that recurses into the fork's own forks. Same card idiom as `PostCommentItem`. |
| `posts/AttachedSection.vue`, `VersionsSection.vue` | **Retired, unimported** — attachments were removed 2026-07-14 and `PostViewerPage` renders its versions section inline; kept on disk only |
| `shared/ElementSurround.vue` | ⭐ The shared **surround band** (2026-07-14): author / moment / forkedFrom chips, label rail, versions / forks / comments bands of InfoChips, and scores — all from one `GET /api/refs/surround` via `composables/useElementSurround.js`. Adopted by `LinkViewerPage`, `PathViewerPage`, `LabelDetailPage` sidebars (Node/Post pages keep their bespoke section UIs over the same walk-backed data). Since 2026-07-30 takes an optional **`provenance-ref`** (`'<kind>/<hash>'` of the element itself) and mounts `ProvenanceBadge` in the header row — link/path viewers pass their element's address. Since 2026-07-31 (Thread K) also an optional **`access-ref`** — when set, `AccessContractPanel` closes the surround (path/link viewers pass the same address) |
| `shared/AccessContractPanel.vue` | **The element viewer's access contract** (Thread K, 2026-07-31): the owner's who-can-read band over `GET /access/status` (decorated entries) — one row per recorded grant (world/person icon · name · by whom · moment), a `history` chip to the ACCESS path viewer (`access_path_id`), and per-row **revoke** (`POST /access/revoke` — mirror rows removed, chain history stays; revoking the pioneer = unpublish). Self-hides unless `gated && canManage`, so it mounts safely anywhere an address is known: NodeDetailPage side column, SkeletonPage (owned-instance editor + read/grid branch side column, schemas excluded), and via `ElementSurround`'s `access-ref` on path/link viewers. |
| `shared/ProvenanceBadge.vue` | **The truth spine's face** (Thread B, 2026-07-30) — `FileContractBadge`'s general sibling: where that one asks "are the bytes behind this FILE node intact?", this asks "does this element's CHAIN story hold?" via `GET /api/verify/<kind>/<hash>` (`services/verify.service.js`). Renders **chain verified** (mint carved chip — click downloads the portable bundle for the offline verifier), **unsigned** (FLAT muted chip — legacy is a fact, not an alarm; covers `unsigned-legacy` + `unchained`), or **chain broken** (red carved chip, `gpp_bad`) with the failing check in the tooltip. Drafts, non-verifiable kinds and transport failures hide it (no verdict ≠ a verdict). Mounted via `ElementSurround`'s `provenance-ref` and in `NodeDetailPage`'s meta row |
| `shared/MarkdownBody.vue` | ⭐ The **only** markdown renderer (marked + DOMPurify + `utils/pathosRefs.js`). `[[pathos:<kind>/<hash>\|label]]` renders as an inline chip; `![[pathos:<kind>/<hash>]]` as a **block embed** — the full `ElementMini` panel in the text flow; `-[[pathos:…]]` (2026-07-31) as a **forced micro chip** — the author's sigil beats the surface everywhere. Chip tier for bare refs per surface via the `refDisplay` prop (`mini` / `micro` / `info` / `auto`). The **`auto` tier** (feed cards + post viewer) mounts `nodes/NodeRefAuto.vue` for bare NODE refs: the micro chip renders immediately, one `GET /nodes/by-path` probe runs behind it, and a node carrying an `embed` descriptor or a media `file.kind` (image/video/audio) swaps to the teal `NodeMini` panel — its `upgrade` event tells MarkdownBody to widen the placeholder to `pathos-ref-embed` figure treatment. Non-node bare refs on `auto` stay `RefMicro` chips. **`breaks` prop** (2026-07-25, default `true`): the platform default treats a single newline as a hard `<br>` — the chat/comment convention the app was written around — and it is now overridable **per render** (`marked.parse(text, { breaks })`, not the global `marked.setOptions`, so one surface opting out cannot move another). Pass `:breaks="false"` for **hard-wrapped prose**: the docs and every post mirroring one are wrapped at ~72 chars in source, and under the default each of those lines becomes its own `<br>`, laying the paragraph out at the AUTHOR's wrap width rather than the container's — the symptom is body text that looks cut off at half the box (`FeedStream`'s post squares, where it measured ~50% fill before and 96–99% after). **`plainRefs` prop** (2026-08-04, default `false`): `true` short-circuits the whole ref pipeline at the `parsed` computed — no placeholder spans, no chips, no probes; `[[pathos:…]]` renders as literal text. For surfaces showing a markdown FILE as a *document* (the floating media viewer): the file's refs are quoted content there, not the surface's links. |
| `shared/EmbedFrame.vue` | ⭐ The platform's **one `<iframe>`** (2026-07-26). Everything it needs arrives as DATA on the `embed` descriptor an `EMBED_RULE` produced API-side (`node.embed`, or `POST /api/embeds/resolve`): `src`, `title`, `allow`, `aspect`, `icon` (the caption's q-icon, 2026-07-27 — `play_circle` under a video, `menu_book` under a Wikipedia article, defaulting to `play_circle` when a rule states none), `mode` + `zoom` (2026-07-27 — `mode: 'page'` swaps the ratio-locked geometry for a DOCUMENT's: `.embed-frame__box--page` takes the surface's full width and `--media-max-h` directly as height (fallback 60vh), and the iframe is laid out at `100% / zoom` then `transform: scale(zoom)` from the top-left corner — the only way to shrink a cross-origin page's type; zoom is re-clamped to 0.4–1 in `boxStyle` before it lands on the transform). No surface builds an embed URL, and no rule ever ships markup — that separation is what keeps a forkable rule from becoming stored XSS. A ratio-locked box (`aspect-ratio` from the rule, `loading="lazy"`, `referrerpolicy="strict-origin-when-cross-origin"`) over an optional provider/source caption, wrapped in `@click.stop.prevent` because Minis are router-links and a click on the play button must not navigate the panel away. The box publishes the rule's ratio TWICE — as `aspect-ratio` and as a plain number on **`--embed-ratio`** — because a surface can only bound such a box by its WIDTH (`aspect-ratio` derives height from width, so a `max-height` clamps the derived value and letterboxes the player inside its own frame), and the width matching a height limit is `limit × ratio`, which the surface cannot compute from the `16 / 9` string. Since 2026-07-26 the box does that conversion ITSELF — `max-width: min(100%, calc(var(--media-max-h, 100vh) * var(--embed-ratio)))` — so a surface publishes only a HEIGHT budget and never has to know the rule's aspect (a 4:3 rule must cap at a 4:3 width). It replaced a `30vh` rule written into the feed card's stylesheet; see **the media budget** under `shared/MiniPanel.vue`. The caption grew a **`#cap-end` slot** (2026-07-27): an optional `margin-left: auto` span at the row's right end, for whatever small marks the surface wants on the provider/url line — `NodeMini` parks its flat vote thumbs there; the url beside it keeps its ellipsis (its `overflow: hidden` zeroes the flex minimum, so it gives first). Mounted by `NodeContentViewer` (full mode, frame **above** the address — the node's content IS the address), `NodeMini` (whose header chip tooltip carries the provider when the node is titleless; this is the branch a feed card's `![[pathos:nodes/…]]` block embed lands on) and `UploaderDock`'s link mode. Concept: `docs/concepts/embeds.md` |
| `nodes/NodeCard.vue` | Card surface for a node |
| `nodes/NoteEditor.vue` | Markdown editor used by MakerDock and the UploaderDock's Write mode. **Dense chrome (maker retouch, 2026-08-02)**: 5px mode bar, 10/14px pane padding, textarea 0.85em/1.6; the split **preview is a working proof, not a reading surface** — `.md-preview` steps headings down hard (`h1` 1.45em … `h6` 1em, first-heading top margin zeroed) and tightens the block rhythm (0.6em paragraph/list margins) via `:deep` overrides on the shared `.md-rendered` pipeline |
| `nodes/CommentItem.vue` | Legacy node-level comments (kept for migration). `CommentMaker.vue` was **deleted 2026-07-17** — the embedded `PostMakerSurface` replaced it everywhere |
| `nodes/ForkMaker.vue`, `ForkCard.vue` | Fork creation + card |
| `nodes/OriginPanel.vue` | Provenance panel — entity, secret, moment chain |
| `labels/LabelChip.vue` | Single chip |
| `labels/LabelViewer.vue` | ⭐ Ancestry-aware chip; detects MOMENT chain for clock styling. |
| `labels/LabelScroll.vue` | Horizontal scrollable row of LabelChips |
| `labels/LabelTree.vue` | Tree view of a label + children |
| `labels/LabelTreeMini.vue` (+ `LabelTreeMiniNode.vue`) | ⭐ The **mini recursive label viewer/picker**: search across all labels OR walk the forest from its roots with lazy branch expansion (`GET /labels/:id/children` on first caret click); sys/yours badges; select-only (emits `select`) by default. The `appendable` prop (label maker dock's browse tree only) closes every unraveled layer with an **add row** — owned layers inline-create a sibling/son right there (leaves become expandable so a son can be planted anywhere; emits `created`), foreign layers show "suggest / fork…" (emits `append-foreign` with the parent) — and appends a root-layer inline input |
| `labels/LabelMiniMaker.vue` | Inline create form: new root or child under a parent; when the parent tree isn't owned it pivots to the **suggest/fork flow** — `can-suggest` (label maker dock) adds a "Suggest to owner" button (emits `suggest` with the parent), fork (`POST /labels/:id/fork`) keeps working inside the owned copy. Emits `created` / `forked` / `suggest` |
| `labels/LabelFieldPicker.vue` | Form control whose value IS a label — button + `q-menu` hosting `LabelTreeMini` (pick) and `LabelMiniMaker` (create, with parent picking + fork pivot). The schema builder's field rows |
| `labels/LabelSlider.vue` | Multi-label horizontal slider on cards |
| `maker/MakerHeader.vue` | Author picker + label picker shared by every maker |
| `maker/AuthorPicker.vue`, `LabelPicker.vue`, `LabelBox.vue` | Building blocks. AuthorPicker renders the caller's identity tree from `GET /api/identity/authorables` — options indented by `depth`, defaulting to the `acting` identity (the one the JWT acts as, possibly an alter-ego). Org rows (2026-07): ORGANIZATION + ORG_ALTER_EGO (mask) entries list with teal icons; rows with `switchable:false` (a MEMBER seat's org entity) are dropped — publishing as the org is an admin power. A restored value (reopened draft) is kept only if it still exists in the tree; a stale id (e.g. a pre-reseed draft's author) resets to `acting` so the field never shows an unmappable raw id |
| `entities/AlterEgoPanel.vue`, `AlterEgoTreeNode.vue` | Sub-entity tree + creation module + identity switching on the entity viewer; the recursive tree node highlights the acting identity and offers per-node switch buttons |
| `paths/PathChainView.vue`, `PathChainStep.vue` | Per-step ancestry display |
| `skeletons/SkeletonSquares.vue` | Origin-skeleton square on `/skeletons` (LabelSquares' carve-the-pit language, skeleton-slate tint). Head click = select + dig; the pit mounts `SkeletonInstanceList` instead of recursing squares — instances all inherit the template's name, so nested name-squares would just repeat the same word. |
| `skeletons/SkeletonInstanceList.vue` | Paginated instantiation list (`GET /skeletons/:id/instances`, 30/page + "+ N more…"): each row shows title (or compacted `ELEMENT:` name), author, age, 2-line excerpt, `SkeletonRefLinks` (post link on POST rows only), per-schema icon; rows select into the Structure panel and carry their own dig toggle (nested list) for instances-of-instances |
| `skeletons/SkeletonUsages.vue` | Right-hand Structure panel of the explorer — identity (MicroChip / owner / moment / lineage), slot inventory as InfoChips, label chips for whichever skeleton is selected |
| `skeletons/SlotRenderer.vue` | Read-only field row on `SkeletonPage` (non-owned instances / plumbing views; prop `entry`). TITLE renders inline text, a node CONTENT renders through `NodeContentViewer`, plumbing slots with a hint (LABELS_PATH, the navigation PATH_VIEW template's INTERACTION_TREE) stay compact InfoChips — every other bound reference blooms into the kind's full Mini via `shared/ElementMini` |
| `skeletons/SkeletonMini.vue` | The skeleton member of the Mini family (`MiniPanel` chrome, NodeMini-sized). Summarizes a schema or populated instance as a KEY → value table: bound NOTE text inline (2-line clamp), other bound refs as dense InfoChips, unbound fields as their ACCEPTS hint / `(empty)`; populated rows sort first, footer counts `bound/total fields populated`. Self-resolving via `id` or `skeletons/<hash>` address (one `GET /skeletons/:id/walk`); header shows the SCHEMA badge or `instance of <schema>` chip. Dispatched by `shared/ElementMini` for every non-POST skeleton ref — so `[[pathos:skeletons/…]]` chips on post bodies bloom into it. |
| `dashboard/SessionActivityCard.vue` | **The session projection card** (dashboards phase 3, 2026-08-10) — the default USER_HOME dashboard's one VIRTUAL cell (`{"virtual":"session-activity"}` in LAYOUT): renders `GET /api/identity/sessions` rows (Session \| Device \| IP — login/last-seen, one-word device off the user-agent w/ the full string on the tooltip, ip) in **SkeletonTable's chrome** — the same `--skel-table-*` dials consumed, so a host that re-tones one re-tones both (chrome restated, not the component: a session row is not Field\|Type\|Data). Owner-only + sid-less by construction (the endpoint answers for the caller's login only); a PROJECTION deliberately not a skeleton — the receipts-later path (lazy SESSION_EVENT) is written in the plan. The `current` session marks with the teal inset bar + "this device" pill. |
| `skeletons/ResourceSkeletonMini.vue` | ⭐ **The EMBEDDABLE MINI TABLE** (dashboards phase 2, 2026-08-10) — one skeleton readable inside another: `MiniPanel` chrome (`bodyFit` — a data table's size is its meaning) around a `SkeletonTable` body, in exchange for its OWN scroll contract (`--resource-mini-max-h`, published by the surface; silence = uncapped). Dispatched by `ElementMini` for every non-POST skeleton ref, so `![[pathos:skeletons/…]]` blooms into it (GITHUB_PR instances resolve through the same walk and render their native card BARE — the chrome would double-frame a card). **RECURSION** (ClaimTopologyNode precedent): a cell whose ref is a skeleton nests another ResourceSkeletonMini; `visited` (ancestor REF STRINGS, checked before any fetch — an A↔B cycle renders an inert dense chip and costs zero requests past the first table) + `depth` (auto-nest budget 1: the top table expands its skeleton cells; deeper cells render an InfoChip leaf + `unfold_more` click-to-expand, so depth is user-driven past the first level). The panel is deliberately NOT a router-link (nested panels inside a linked panel would nest anchors — the OrgLogoChip lesson); the head carries an explicit `open_in_full` glyph-link instead. Own-head row: kind icon + name + SCHEMA badge / instance-of + hash + open. Re-toning: NodeMini's dial pattern — `--resource-mini-coat/-rule/-rule-hover/-head-ink` repaint the chrome; the table's own `--skel-table-*` dials pass through untouched. Non-skeleton cells restate SkeletonTable's default cell (text / dense chip / unbound) — a scoped slot replaces the whole default, keep the two in step. **PHASE 6**: computes `editable` (walk's `is_resource` && `!locked` && owner === `auth.entityId` — the server re-checks all three on write: 40300/40303/40010), passes it down, owns the `edit` commit (`setSlot` → refresh, or the `changed` emit when pre-walked so the grid reloads its batch), flashes constraint refusals inline, and the head grows the **lock toggle** (owner-only on resources; `lock`/`lock_open` glyph → `POST /skeletons/:id/lock\|unlock` — coral when locked). |
| `skeletons/SkeletonTable.vue` | ⭐ **The DENSE TABLE — one skeleton as a real data table** (dashboards phase 1, 2026-08-10): a `<table>` (`table-layout: fixed`) with columns Field \| Type \| Data at SkeletonInstanceEditor's 26/16/58 (the editor is its read-write sibling), drawn in the FLYOUT GREY FAMILY — plaque `--grey-3`, rules `--grey-4`/`--grey-5`, ink `--brown-8`/`--brown-4`, teal ONLY on hover (a 2px `--teal-12` inset bar on the first cell — the NodeMini-in-flyout precedent). Tones are SIX DIALS (`--skel-table-coat/-rule/-rule-strong/-ink/-ink-mute/-hover`, NodeMini's dial pattern at the dock's six-dial naming) so hosts re-tone without a repainting modifier. Type column = `expectedKind` pill in `kindFor(kind).color` (mute-ink 'any' when unconstrained), shown for BOTH bound and unbound rows — a table states its column types where the compact Mini only hints them on empties. Data column = `textValue` inline (pre-wrap, no clamp — the walk's 2000-char cap bounds it; the HOST owns the ceiling), other refs as dense InfoChips, unbound in italic mute. Declaration order preserved (a table reads as the schema's shape; the Mini re-sorts bound-first). Two mounting modes: pre-walked (`skeleton` + `slots` — the dashboard grid batch-walks once and deals results out) or self-resolving `refOrId` (id or address → one walk, `resolved` emitted so hosts title themselves). GITHUB_PR instances bloom into GithubPrCard exactly as in SkeletonMini. A `#data` scoped slot lets phase-2+ hosts replace the default cell rendering per row. Mounted at three scales: SkeletonFlyout's generic face (max detail), ResourceSkeletonMini's body (phase 2), dashboard grid cells (phase 4). **PHASE 6 — the mutation surface** (`editable` prop; the HOST decides — RESOURCE + unlocked + owner — and owns the `setSlot` on the `edit` emit): textable cells go tap-to-edit (inline input in the grey family; unit cells get a numeric input + STEP steppers with float-dust trimming), the Type column wears the **unit symbol chip** beside the kind pill (`slot.unit {label, symbol, step}` off the walk), and every row grows the **history glyph** — a popover loading `GET /skeletons/:id/history?slot=` on open (actor · value · moment, superseded rows dimmed). ⚠ The `#data` slot replaces the whole default cell, so the slot SCOPE carries `canEdit` + `beginEdit` — slot content compiles in the HOST's scope (RSM restates the tap affordance on its own spans; the table's editor row itself renders outside the slot). |
| `shared/HashLink.vue` | Inline routed hash link — icon + truncated hash + open-in-new arrow; routes to the kind's viewer page via `utils/kinds.js` when an id is known. Fallback chip in the file tree. |
| `shared/SkeletonRefLinks.vue` | Mono nav links for a skeleton id: `sk #N` (structure view) and, **only when `post` is true**, `post/N` (content view). Posts ARE skeletons — the two links are two views of the same id — but no post exists for other schemas' instances, so callers listing arbitrary skeletons pass `:post="row.name === 'POST'"` (default `true` fits the post-only surfaces: comments, forks, feed). |

### Micro + Mini component family

The canonical reusable preview surface for pathchain elements. One `XMicro` (inline chip) and one `XMini` (mini card) per kind, all built on shared primitives. Adding a new viewer kind = one Micro + one Mini wrapper, not new chrome.

| Component | Role |
|-----------|------|
| `utils/kinds.js` | ⭐ Single source of truth: on-disk prefix → `{ kind, icon, color, route(id) }`. Also `parseAddress`, `hashOf`, `shortHash`, `isHash`, and `parseRef` (decoded-field → navigable reference). `HashLink`, `AddressChain`, `MicroChip`, `RefChip` all import from here. |
| `shared/MicroChip.vue` | ⭐ Generic `icon / type / hash` chip. Container-adaptive width; routes to the kind's page when given an id. A linked chip IS an anchor, so **`.is-link:hover` forces `text-decoration: none !important`** (2026-07-26): inside rendered prose the surface's own `a:hover { text-decoration: underline }` outscored the `none` on `.micro-chip` and struck a line through `node / a1b2c3…`. The box is the affordance; see `MiniPanel` for why this is `!important` and not a specificity fight. **`linked` prop** (2026-07-26, default `true`): pass `false` and the chip renders as a `<span>` even when the kind has a viewer route — for a chip that TRIGGERS rather than navigates. It keeps its icon, hash and address tooltip; restate `cursor`/`:hover` at the call site, since `.is-link` no longer applies. (The feed card's foot chip was that pattern's live user until 2026-08-10 sent it back to being a link.) **`typeLabel` + `typeIcon` + `sep` props** (2026-08-10, all three defaulting to the previous rendering): `typeLabel` replaces the WORD between the separators — the kind's own slug says what the element IS, and a chip standing for a different READING of it says so instead (the feed foot's chip stands for `skeleton` over a `posts` kind, because a post's address is a handle on its skeleton); `typeIcon` DRAWS that word instead of spelling it and wins over `typeLabel` when both are given, for a chip sitting in a strip whose every other mark is a glyph (the feed foot passes the cap's `sym_o_orthopedics`, so it reads `[post] :: [skeleton] :: <hash>`); `sep` replaces the `/` on both sides, for a chip inside a strip with its own punctuation (the feed foot passes the cap's `::`). None of them touches the hash, the route or the tooltip — the chip still addresses exactly what it addressed, and the reading the glyph names stays on the tooltip. |
| `shared/MiniPanel.vue` | ⭐ Slot-based mini-card chrome (`#title`, `#chips`, `#labels`, `#hash`, `#body`, `#foot`). Every Mini uses it. **A panel is never underlined** (2026-07-26): `.mini-panel-link:hover { text-decoration: none !important }`. A Mini quoted into a post body IS an anchor inside rendered prose, and every prose surface underlines its links on hover (`.md-rendered a:hover` in `css/_components.scss`, `.post-square__md :deep(.markdown-body) a:hover` in FeedStream, `.post-md-rendered` in PostViewerPage) — the decoration is drawn by the anchor and propagates down its whole box tree, so hovering a panel struck a line under every run of text in it at once: title, excerpt, chip, counts. `!important` rather than a stronger selector because those rules are SCOPED (four classes + an element) — out-selecting them means beating the highest-scoring surface that exists today and losing again to the next one written, and the rule is not a preference to weigh against theirs: prose styles inline links, and this is a BLOCK quoted into prose. Panels state hover with the border, the shadow and the 1px lift. Two escape hatches (2026-07-26), both `NodeMini`'s: **`#head`** replaces the default zone STACK outright for a Mini that composes its own header ROW, and **`bodyFit`** lifts the body's `max-height: 110px` scroller. That cap is an EXCERPT rule — "a Mini quotes a few lines and you open it for the rest" — and it is wrong when the preview IS the content: it was cropping a 480×270 player to its top 110px, play button and all. **The media budget** rides on that: a medium quoted into a post must show WHOLE, and the size that means is the largest one fitting the window the post is read in, which the Mini cannot know (same panel in a 60vh-capped feed card and in a full-height post viewer). So the SURFACE publishes **`--media-max-h`** and the components size themselves from it — `NodeMini` as a `max-height` on the `img`/`video`, `EmbedFrame` converted into the width cap its ratio-locked box needs. Publishers: `.post-square__pit` (`max(120px, calc(min(var(--post-square-max, 100cqw), 60vh) - 264px))`, off the CARD's ceiling — a flat `30vh` knew nothing about the width half of `min(width, 60vh)`) and `.post-md-rendered` (`max(200px, calc(100cqh - 136px))`, off a `container-type: size` on `.markdown-scroll`). Unset, the fallbacks are a 180px thumbnail and an uncapped frame. |
| `{entities,nodes,posts,paths,labels,moments,links,secrets}/XMicro.vue` | Thin per-kind wrappers over `MicroChip`. |
| `{entities,nodes,posts,paths,labels,moments,links,secrets}/XMini.vue` | Per-kind mini cards over `MiniPanel`. `LabelMini` is recursive (inlines the parent label, depth-capped at 8). **`NodeMini` is the odd one out** (2026-07-26): it is the panel that gets quoted INTO posts by a `![[pathos:nodes/…]]` block embed, so it has a colorway of its own to read as another element on both the feed's indigos and the viewer's white paper — a uniform `--teal-1` coat (no chrome/body tone split), `--teal-3` drawing EVERY line (1px box on a 2px base, no shadow) and `--teal-10` inking what the panel writes in its own zones. **Since 2026-07-27 the panel is HEADER + BODY — the FOOT is GONE.** Its header is ONE ROW split by full-height vertical hairlines (padding on the zones so the rules touch both edges), and the row now reads **`round pill │ address chip │ open`**: (1) the **ROUND PILL** (`.node-mini__round-pill`) replaced the flat kind icon — the old foot pill's material closed into a fixed 18px CIRCLE around the kind glyph alone: `--grey-3` fill, a 1px `--grey-5` ring OUTSIDE the box + a 1px `--grey-5` seam inside its bottom edge (both box-shadows — the one border is spent on the base, and a shadow follows `border-radius` where an outline may not), a **2px `--teal-12` base** that curves along the bottom arc (the pill's own device — its 999px ends did the same), `--grey-8` ink with the hairline `-webkit-text-stroke: 0.35px`; (2) the **ADDRESS CHIP** stands where the title text stood — a plain `NodeMicro` in MicroChip's own STOCK skin (the exact chip the feed card's foot wears: `rgba(ink)` fill, 4px radius, mono 0.72em), `icon / node / hash` with the hash cut to 10 digits + `…` (**`chipHash`**, renamed from `footHash`; full path on the chip's tooltip) — the heavily restyled Nasalization PILL and all its `!important` MicroChip overrides died with the foot; the human title survives on the title ZONE's tooltip (`effectiveTitle` — post title > embed provider > `Node #id`), and the `raw` mode mono-on-grey-1 title treatment is gone with the text (raw still governs the BODY: source pane, `body-fit`, `--node-source-max-h`); (3) the open glyph, unchanged (`--coral-deep` on hover). **The VOTES moved onto the embed caption line** — `EmbedFrame`'s new `#cap-end` slot parks them at the right end of the provider/url row as `.vote-flat`: no fill, no rim, no box, a bare glyph + count in caption-scale `--grey-8`, a size down from the pills they were; the viewer's OWN vote keeps its semantic green/red in the INK alone. A node with no embed (raw docs, images, text excerpts) simply shows no votes — the place to read a node's activity is its viewer, which is also why the comment/fork tallies came off with the band. `GET /nodes/by-path` still feeds `votes`; nothing reads `comment_count`/`fork_count` here anymore. **HOVER in the colorway** (2026-07-26): `:deep(.mini-panel.mini-panel--hover):hover` lights the whole LINE SYSTEM — outer border, MiniPanel's head/body divider, the header's zone splits — to `--teal-11` via two custom properties (`--panel-rule` MiniPanel's, `--node-rule` NodeMini's own; `border-color` restated because MiniPanel's hover sets the property directly; both class names on the selector because a one-class `:deep()` only ties). The round pill's ring and base stay put — they bound an object, they do not divide the panel. Styling the panel takes BARE `:deep()` (`MiniPanel` sets `inheritAttrs: false`, so a class on the component is dropped while the scope id still lands). **FOUR dials** (down from five — `--node-mini-foot-glyph` died with the foot), the `--frieze-bar-base` seam pattern, all read by `SkeletonFlyout` alone: `--node-mini-coat` (both faces of the coat), `--node-mini-rule` (the whole line system in one value), `--node-mini-rule-hover` (its mandatory partner — a half-dialled panel flashes MINT), `--node-mini-head-ink` (the header row's zone ink, which since the chip and pill are their own neutral objects lands mostly on the open glyph). What is NOT dialled: the source pane's `--teal-10`, which is what keeps saying NODE after coat, lines and head ink have gone host-toned. **No WEIGHT dial**, deliberately — `--node-mini-rule-w` existed for part of 2026-07-27 and came out: every line is 1px because at Mini scale that is a hairline; a host that finds them faint has a TONE problem. (Two notes if wanted back: MiniPanel hard-codes 1px on border + dividers, so a width dial must be restated over both, and the heavy 2px base must become `calc(w * 2)`.) `raw` reads the TEXT body as SOURCE instead of excerpt — whole `bodyOf(node)`, every marker intact, `pre-wrap`, monospace at 1.62 on a `--grey-1` floor — opting out of MiniPanel's 110px cap (`body-fit`) and scrolling ITSELF at `--node-source-max-h` (published by the surface, the `--media-max-h` contract). |
| `moments/MomentWorldMap.vue` | The **cute tiny world** (2026-07-19): dotted equirectangular land grid (generated `moments/worldDots.js` — 3° cells rasterized from public-domain country polygons) as an inline SVG, a gold pulsing pin projected at the moment's lat/lon, and a caption reading **city, Country** (bold city) from `human.city`/`human.country`, falling back to `place`. Rendered by `MomentViewerPage` beside the facts block whenever the moment has coordinates. |
| `shared/ElementMini.vue` | ⭐ The "any element as a Mini" dispatcher — self-resolves a `<kind>/<hash>` address (or takes a pre-resolved link target) into the matching Mini: Node/Path/Post/Label/Entity/Moment/Link/Skeleton. Skeleton refs split on the summary route: POST instances render `PostMini`, every other skeleton (schema or populated instance) renders `skeletons/ResourceSkeletonMini` — the embeddable mini TABLE (2026-08-10, dashboards phase 2; `SkeletonMini` before) — with the recursion guards `depth`/`visited` threaded through as props (default 0/[]). Secrets and unresolvable refs degrade to an `InfoChip`; refs the viewer can't read (403 + `{locked}` summary — access doctrine) render `LockedChip`. **The skeletons case checks `summary.locked` FIRST** (2026-07-31): the locked stub CARRIES `id`/`primary` by doctrine (the hash stays visible), so the `id == null` guard passes and a private skeleton used to mount a `SkeletonMini` that 403'd on every field. `RefMicro` gained the same branch — a locked inline ref renders `LockedChip` instead of an inert micro chip. Used by post content rails, MarkdownBody's `mini` tier, pins, and both skeleton-instance field surfaces. Its 480px cap is a prose MEASURE and a figure is not prose, so a media node (embed / image / video, read off the shape it already resolved) gets `.element-mini--media` and the surface's full width instead. |
| `shared/LockedChip.vue` | Access doctrine's locked tier: the element's hashname in a micro-style chip with a **lock bubble** on its edge; clicking opens `RequestAccessPanel` in a q-menu. Rendered by `ElementMini` for unreadable refs; `InfoChip` has a matching inline `.is-locked` state (dashed border, lock bubble, no route, same panel). |
| `shared/RequestAccessPanel.vue` | The "Request permission" surface behind every locked chip AND the access-denied banner's prompt: optional message + request button → `accessService.request` (lands as a chat message + access poll on the owner's side). | `claims/ClaimBand.vue` | **The claim reader band** (Thread D reader surface, 2026-07-31), mounted by `SkeletonPage` above a CLAIM instance's structure view: STATUS pill + live endorse/dispute tally + `ProvenanceBadge` on the claim's spine (`paths/<hash>`) in the head; statement through `MarkdownBody` (`info` tier); confidence line; the evidence tree; stance controls — endorse one-click, dispute opens the mandatory-ref form (`:dark="false"` on its inputs — light panel under global Quasar dark), retract author-only behind a `$q.dialog` confirm. Votes rebind STATUS server-side, so `@changed` → SkeletonPage `reloadSlots`. |
| `claims/ClaimEvidenceNode.vue` | One recursive row of the evidence tree: an `InfoChip` per evidence ref; a ref whose summary carries `claim` fetches that claim and nests its evidence beneath (depth-capped at 3 — a self-citing claim or long dispute chain can't run away). Private evidence = locked chip via the summary endpoint. |
| `claims/ClaimTopology.vue` + `claims/ClaimTopologyNode.vue` | **The Disagreement band** (Thread D stage 2, 2026-07-31), mounted by `ClaimBand` under the evidence tree and re-keyed on every vote/retract (`topoKey`). ONE read — `GET /api/claims/:id/topology` — feeds the whole band; recursion never refetches (the graph rides down as a prop). Renders nothing when the claim has no edges. Three directions: *disputed by* rows recurse label-tree-style (`ClaimTopologyNode` — an attacker expands with its own disputers/supporters, `MAX_DEPTH` 3, visited-set against cycles); *this claim stands against* and *cited as support by* are flat reverse-navigation rows. Every row wears the edge's provenance: voter `InfoChip`, a dotted `comment` link to the carrying comment, relative time. Locked nodes render as `InfoChip` locked stubs and never expand; a dispute standing on a non-claim element keeps its row with the evidence's chip. Status pills reuse the shared claim palette. |
| Claim STATUS on chips | `refs/summary` answers CLAIM instances with `claim: { id, status }`; **`InfoChip`** renders a status **pill** (`.info-chip__status`), **`MicroChip`** a status **dot** (`claim-status` prop, fed by `RefMicro`'s resolved summary; word rides the tooltip). One palette everywhere: open `#5b6c82` / supported `#2e6a3a` / disputed `#a03d3d` / retracted `#8995a8` struck-through. |
| `shared/AccessDeniedBanner.vue` | The main viewers' full-page access-denied surface: when a direct read 403s (code 40310), the node/path/post/link/skeleton pages render it instead of the element grid (and instead of the spinner hanging). A subject-panel-family **frame** (white body, chrome tokens) sized to the viewport (`calc(100vh - 16px - --nav-footer-h)`; SkeletonPage overrides `-32px` for its `q-pa-md`) so it never scrolls: a very thin chrome header with the item's kind icon (`kindFor`) + full hashname + lock, then the picture banner inset on an 8px chrome pad. Desktop picture: the porcelain-octopus landscape (`assets/access-octopus-gray.png`) full-bleed, message + the cloth-textured **Request Access** button on its slate field (`#2e3c45`; SVG `feTurbulence` grain over the taupe felt the octopus holds). Under 768px the portrait shot (`access-octopus-mobile.png`, same slate) takes over — text above him, button below him. The button opens the shared `RequestAccessPanel` in a q-menu below it — the requester is prompted for a message to the owner before sending (owner chat + access poll) — then flips to a "request sent" confirmation. On mount the banner probes `accessService.status(address)`: `requested: true` (the viewer's poll is still pending server-side) replaces the button with an "access already requested" line, so reloads can't spam the owner. `address` comes from the 403 envelope's `error.element` locked stub, parsed by `utils/access.js#lockedInfoFromError` in each viewer's load catch. |
| `maker/AccessTreeDialog.vue` | The pre-publish **access tree** (persistent q-dialog): root row = the post itself, one row per `[[pathos:]]` ref from `POST /api/access/tree` (recursed through referenced posts). World-toggles on owned gated branches; flipping the base flips every branch. Confirm emits `{ public, publish[] }` → `PostMakerSurface.doPost` sends it as the post's `access` payload. Comments skip the dialog (they inherit the parent's audience server-side). `mode="share"` (the chat composer): the audience is the conversation's members — no root toggle, branch picks become `shareRefs`. |
| `chat/ChatDock.vue` | The **7th docked window** (windows.js key `chat`; teal `forum` button on the nav bar's right cluster; minimizes to a `chats` minitab). **THE CREATION FOOTPRINT since 2026-08-03** (user ask): it carries `.dock-window--creation` + `.dock-window--chat`, so its geometry, maximize (`is-max`, renamed from `is-maximized`), shadow and mobile chrome are the maker's to the pixel — see the MakerDock row. Three things had to GO from the scoped block rather than be overridden: `width: 74vw`, `height: 72vh` and the `.is-maximized` / mobile width pairs — a scoped `.chat-dock[data-v-…]` selector outranks the global `.dock-window--creation`, so a leftover width would have quietly kept the old footprint. It anchors straight to the bar's top edge (`bottom: --nav-footer-h`) since 2026-08-03 — the user's words: "we do not want to have the chat float on top of the footer frieze bar"; it had stood ON the frieze footer band via `--dock-bottom` before, and that band (with the token) was retired the next day. **The LIME colorway** (hue-swapped from green 2026-08-05, "even clearer tones") is its own identity: `.dock-window--chat` points the creation chrome's six dials at the `--lime-*` family (`_tokens.scss` § LIME — the brown roles one hue over: -1 coat, -2 wells, -3 lines, -4 heavy line, -10 ink — the ink sits at Material 900, not the sibling colorways' 700, since lime-8 only clears ~2.2:1 on white), and the coat is OPAQUE `--lime-1` where the shell used to be `--surface-glass-strong` over a 12px `backdrop-filter` — the platform's last translucent dock. A **FriezeBar** sits between the header and the body (`.chat-dock__frieze`, `flex: 0 0 auto` or the flex column squashes it) — since 2026-08-05 it runs **`slim`** (one SVG layer, half height, the media viewer's recipe), dials base `--lime-10` and wave two `--brown-1` (no wave-one dial; slim never renders that layer). Interior: the conversations column is a `--lime-2` well in the `--lime-1` coat, rows are `--lime-1` cards on it with the ACTIVE one going to PAPER + a `--lime-4` rim (the dock tab strip's "current = the lightest thing here" device), the thread lies on the coat itself and `MessageBubble` carries the tone difference (theirs paper on `--lime-3`, MINE `--lime-2` on `--lime-4` — the teal `rgba(#00829c,.09)` my side wore was the last translucency in here). **Vertical cap (2026-08-05)**: open (not maximized) the window pins to the bottom of the creation docks' shared frieze-to-navbar span and caps its own height at `min(60vh, <that span>)`; the green traffic light's maximize reclaims the full span (same height every other creation dock always has) and squares off the top-right corner the cap left rounded (`.dock-window--chat.is-max`) — the nav bar's footer stays visible under the window either way, since `bottom: --nav-footer-h` never moves. Left: conversations (latest activity, last-message preview) + new-conversation entity search (`refs/search` kind=entities). **A row is the feed card's IDENTITY BLOCK** (2026-08-03, user ask: "just like the author bubbles on the feed post cards … the image and org badges in a dense style"): `EntityAvatar` at 26px, name over `@handle`, `OrgLogoChip` at 15px pulled in −2px, a `+n` chip for a group's remaining seats, on the byline's own 5px gap / 1.15 leading — fed by the member cards' new `photo` + `org` (see api-reference § chats). The chip is mounted **`:link="false"`**: the row is a `<button>` and the chip's default router-link would nest interactive content inside it (invalid, and the anchor swallows the row's tap). An agent seat has no handle, so that line is dropped rather than left empty. **On a phone the column has two presentations of ONE element** (the stack/pins device, same argument — separate elements drift apart): `is-faces` (mobile ∧ not expanded) narrows the grid track to **46px** and hides everything in a row but its face, the org badge surviving as a corner dot; the head's one control flips `is-list-open`, which lifts the same column to `position: absolute; inset: 0` over the conversation at full width so the search field and the names are usable. Picking a conversation closes the overlay; leaving the phone drops it. Keep the 46px in step with `.chat-row`'s padding and the avatar's `:size`. Main: classic chat + composer (Enter sends; refs open the share tree). **LIVE since 2026-07-29 (Thread A)**: the dock watches `stores/events.js#lastEvent` — a pushed `chat.message`/`poll.*`/`org.invite` for the OPEN conversation reloads its feed within the second (measured 80 ms end to end) and acks that event targeted (seen where it lives); events for other conversations refresh the preview list. The old 15 s poll became a **60 s dropped-stream fallback**. Consent gate (2026-07): pending seats get an accept/decline banner (replying also accepts), conversation rows carry `invitation`/`declined` badges, and the composer locks with a hint while the other side is pending (after your one opener) or declined — server codes 40311/40312/40313 surface under the composer. Feed renders `org_invite` items as `organizations/OrgInviteCard.vue` (PollCard pattern: Accept — put on the mask / Decline for the invitee while PENDING, status chip otherwise). **Agent availability band (2026-07-31)**: chat members that are rostered agents carry an `agent` card (quota bundle from the AGENT_PROFILE skeleton — `free_tier`, `available`, `resets_at`); when the open conversation's agent is a free-tier one with an empty pool, a coral `.chat-dock__agent-away` band under the header shows "*scout is not available — free-tier quota resets in 2h 59m*", with the countdown re-derived from `resets_at` every 30 s client-side. Availability is read from stored state — the UI never spends a provider request to ask. **Delivery surfaces (Thread G, 2026-07-31)**: a quiet `.chat-dock__seen` line ("seen" / group-aware "seen by …", hover = when) sits under the newest of YOUR messages the other members' watermarks (`members[].last_read_link_id` vs `items[].link_id`, both from the feed) cover; a green `.chat-dock__presence` dot beside the conversation header's names = the other side holds a live SSE stream right now (`members[].online`). Silent `chat.read` events (born read — no ack, no badge) reload the open conversation's feed so the seen line moves live. **The channel contract (Thread K, 2026-07-31)**: the conversation header's `policy` button toggles `ChatContractBand` under it. |
| `chat/ChatContractBand.vue` | **The channel contract** (Thread K, 2026-07-31): the live who-sees-what panel over `GET /api/chats/:id/contract` — seats with consent pills (accepted/pending/declined tones), then one row per element the conversation put on the table (each `[[pathos:]]` ref in a message + each poll TARGET): kind icon + routed title, globe when public, first-shared moment, a `history` chip to the on-chain ACCESS path viewer, and the audience as eye / crossed-eye per member (tooltip: granted by whom · when — from the decorated ledger). Derived from recorded state only, so it cannot claim an audience the chain contradicts; reloads on the dock's `feedTick` (every successful feed read), which covers fresh shares and poll grants without its own event plumbing. **Closes with the Attention receipts section (Thread G stage 2, 2026-07-31)**: your consent `q-toggle` (GET/POST `/chats/:id/attention`), per-seat consent pills, and — ONLY while every seat consents — one row per table ref with attended/not-attended eyes per member (tooltip: visit count + last time; quiet grey for not-yet-attended, not the audience red — "hasn't looked" is not a denial); otherwise the consent hint (symmetric, per-conversation, revocable, never retroactive). |
| `stores/events.js` + `services/event.service.js` | **The push spine's client end** (Thread A, 2026-07-29, `docs/concepts/events.md`): ONE `EventSource` for the whole app, connected from MainLayout onMounted after login. Stream URL = `api.defaults.baseURL + /events/stream?t=<1h token from GET /events/token>`; reconnection is the STORE's (close → wait 4 s → fresh token → reconnect with `lastEventId` replay cursor) because the browser's automatic retry would replay an expired `?t=` forever. Signals: `unreadCount` — worn by the **NavigationBar chat button** as a tiny amber floating badge (`.chat-unread-badge`, the pin tack counter's exact treatment); opening the dock acks the whole queue (coarse v1 — every current event kind lands in a chat surface or the polls band one click away) — and `lastEvent`, the most recent pushed event (a signal, not a log). |
| Feed **trust surfaces** (Thread J, 2026-07-29, `docs/concepts/trust.md`) | On `FeedStream.vue`: the byline's **`.post-square__trust`** chip after the identity block/org badge — invite-chain distance as a tiny `--grey-1`/`--indigo-3` plate ("2 hops"; own posts read "you"), tooltip walking the whole vouch path ("Invite chain: you › allegue › Dream Operator's Garage › …") from `item.author.trust` — and the head's own **hop lens** (in the band until 2026-08-06, in the head BOX's second half through its `controls` slot since; a `.feed-stream__lens` segmented control — all / ≤1 / ≤2 / ≤3 — until **2026-08-07**, when it folded into the single `.feed-stream__hops` DROPDOWN: `connect_without_contact` + the radius as its `.feed-stream__lens-n` mark, `∞` for the open feed, the four options now words in a `q-menu`). Either face is a `--grey-1` plate in `--indigo-3` lines whose ON state inverts to a `--indigo-8` floor; it reloads the stream with `?maxHops=` and is session-local on purpose (a lens is looked through, not saved). |
| `chat/MessageBubble.vue` | One message: others left, yours right; body via `MarkdownBody` `mini` tier so refs render as Minis and locked refs as lock-bubbled hash chips. THE message viewer — messages read here first. **In the chat window's lime colorway (green until 2026-08-05)**: theirs is paper on a `--lime-3` rim (author name in `--lime-10`), MINE is the well tone `--lime-2` on the heavier `--lime-4` — so "who said it" is stated by how lime the bubble is, on a bed that is the window's own `--lime-1` coat. Both fills are OPAQUE; the `rgba(#00829c, .09)` teal tint my side used to wear was the last translucency in the window, and a tint over a coloured coat is not the colour it was drawn as. |
| `chat/PollCard.vue` | A poll in the conversation, **kind-aware** (2026-07-19, `poll.kind` from the feed item with a `target_ref` fallback): access polls ("Access poll", `how_to_vote`, "Yes — grant access"), label suggestions ("Label suggestion", `new_label`, "Yes — add the label") and approval polls (2026-08-02, resident dev flow: "Approval", `fact_check`, "Yes — approve"; statuses approved/declined wear the green/red tints; never reversible). Markdown question with chips, status pill (pending/granted/denied + accepted/declined tints), Yes/No buttons only for the DECIDER (`viewerIds` vs `poll.decider_id`) while pending → `pollService.vote` → emits `voted`. `allow-reverse` prop (the profile's My-polls band sets it; chats don't): once a poll is decided the decider gets a "Reverse — revoke/grant access" button → `pollService.reverse` (granted ⇄ denied, access flips with it) — **access polls only**; label suggestions are one-shot (pruning the branch is the undo). |
| `entities/EntityAvatar.vue` | ⭐ **An entity's face**, as a ROUNDED-SQUARE tile (2026-07-25 — a disc for the first day, until the corners of the generated square compositions started disappearing into the circular crop; the radius is a PERCENTAGE, `26%`, not an em-based token, so a 34px feed chip and a 56px profile hero are exactly the same shape, and `EntityProfilePage`'s faceless `q-avatar` fallback restates it to outrank Quasar's own `border-radius: 50%`). The photo is not a column on the entity — it is the `nodes/<hash>` reference bound into that entity's `USER_PROFILE > PROFILE_PHOTO`, resolved server-side into `{ url, … }` — so the component takes whatever the caller already has (`:entity="item.author"` on a feed card carries the photo inline) and only falls back to the cached `entitySummary()` resolver when handed a bare id. Three states in order: **the photo** (`<img>`, `object-fit: cover`, hairline rim, `@error` falls through), **the pioneer's carved-gold star**, and a **monogram tile** whose hue is a deterministic hash of the entity id (golden-angle stepped, so neighbouring ids never collide) — a real fallback, not a placeholder: a faceless author still reads as a distinct person down a column of cards. A PRIVATE photo resolves with `url: null` and lands on the monogram too — there is a face, we are simply not allowed to see it. Mounted by `FeedStream`'s identity block (34px) and `EntityProfilePage`'s hero (56px, replacing the generic person/star `q-avatar` when a photo is bound). |
| `organizations/OrgLogoChip.vue` | ⭐ **The badge an author wears** (2026-07-25). Takes the `org` card the API hands out (`{ id, entity_id, name, handle, logo, role_title, self? }` — on `feed` items, `refs/summary` entity summaries and `GET /organizations`) and draws the organization's `ORG_PROFILE > LOGO` image at `:size` px (18 on a feed card) as a rounded square, linking to `/organizations/:id` with `@click.stop`. No logo, or a private one (`logo.url === null`)? A **monogram plaque** stands in, tinted by the same golden-angle hue walk `EntityAvatar` uses, so two orgs never wear the same fallback. `show-name` adds the org's name beside the mark for list surfaces; the feed card leaves it in the `title` tooltip (with the member's `role_title`), since the row is already carrying a name. The mark has a `box-shadow` hairline rather than a border so a pale badge still has an edge on a pale card. |
| `entities/EntityPolls.vue` | The profile's **"My polls" band** (own profile only — `EntityProfilePage` renders it `v-if="isSelf"`, between the grid and EntityContributions): `GET /api/polls/mine` → responsive grid of `PollCard`s, each cell headed by a role chip (**you decide** purple / **your request** gray) + the poll's moment. Decider cells pass `allow-reverse`, so pending polls get Yes/No and decided ones the reverse button; any change reloads the list. |

### File tree viewer

`/files` renders the on-disk pathchain (`files/<owner>/…`) as a recursive tree. Backed by `services/file.service.js` → `/api/files`.

| Component | Role |
|-----------|------|
| `pages/FileTreePage.vue` | ⭐ Page shell: loads the tree, lineage breadcrumb, and builds the intra-tree **nav controller** (see below). |
| `components/files/FileTreeNode.vue` | Recursive row. Folders expand inline; each leaf carries a Mini-preview dropdown + a decoded/`.proto` dropdown. Consumes the nav controller to force-open + flash a revealed element. |
| `components/files/DecodedContentInline.vue` | The decoded panel: a **draggable vertical `q-splitter`** — decoded JSON leads at 70%, the `.proto` schema trails at 30% (stacks on phones). |
| `components/files/JsonView.vue`, `JsonNode.vue` | Collapsible pretty-printed JSON. `JsonNode` renders any reference-address string as a `RefChip`. |
| `components/files/RefChip.vue` | ⭐ A `MicroChip` whose click **reveals the target inside the tree** (`treeNav.reveal`) instead of routing away. |
| `components/files/FileContractBadge.vue` | Contract verdict chip for FILE nodes (mounted in `NodeDetailPage`'s body-kind row). Lazily calls `GET /api/uploads/verify/…` via `services/upload.service.js` and renders **contract verified** (teal carved chip, `.pioneer-gold` relief pattern) or **contract violated** (red) — the on-disk sealed container's word on whether the bytes still match the node. Hidden on transport errors. |
| `utils/treeNav.js` | The nav controller factory. Indexes every leaf by canonical `<prefix>/<hash>`, force-opens ancestor folders, scrolls + flashes the target, and auto-expands its decoded panel. |

**Intra-tree navigation flow:** open a leaf's decoded dropdown → every `kind/hash` reference field (author, register, tag, head, prev/next/target, …) is a `RefChip` → click it → `FileTreePage`'s provided `treeNav` resolves the address in its index, force-opens the ancestor folders, scrolls the target row into view, flashes it, and auto-expands *its* decoded panel. Targets outside the loaded subtree (e.g. a cross-owner reference on `/files/:ownerHash`) fall back to `/files?reveal=<address>`, which resolves on the whole-platform view. Plain-content fields (`text`) and empty fields stay inert text — only `parseRef`-matching addresses become chips.

## CSS design system

Three layered files under `src/css/` plus generated `quasar.variables.scss`:

```
_tokens.scss      → CSS custom properties (--ink, --paper, --aqua-*, --coral, --radius-*, --shadow-*)
_mixins.scss      → composable SCSS mixins (@glass, @carved, @inset-font, @neon-bg-surface, @chip, @scrollbar-ink)
_utilities.scss   → drop-in utility classes (.bg-surface, .nasalization, .inset-font, .rounded-md, .glass-light, …)
_components.scss  → shared component classes (.pathos-card, .maker-overlay, .maker-dialog, .interaction-bar, .nav-btn, .md-rendered)
app.scss          → thin: imports + the starfield page canvas + body + display-face rules
quasar.variables.scss → exposes $primary / $secondary / $accent for Quasar's own components
```

Re-theming is a single-file edit to `_tokens.scss`. All other styles consume `var(--…)` references.

Page canvas (2026-07): the body paints `assets/backgrounds/starfield-noise.svg`
(a black 700×700 specular-noise tile) repeated + fixed, dimmed by a subtle
opaque grey-3 (`rgba(238,238,238,0.16)`) glass wash so the stars stay quiet;
the rule targets `body, body.body--dark` because Quasar's dark mode otherwise
buries it under `--q-dark-page`. The Nasalization display face ships LOCALLY
at `src/css/fonts/nasalization.otf` (it is **not** a Google font — the old
remote `@import` silently fell back) and is applied globally to `.q-btn` and
`.text-h1…h6` on top of the existing `.nasalization` utility.

The two waves sit **two levels apart** on purpose: stepping them together only darkened the block, and the spread is what the carve reads as. So the roles no longer line up one-to-one with the browns at all — only the MAPPING does (plaque / wave one / wave two / lip), which is what a colorway actually is here. Only the three steps in use are defined — trying another hue means swapping these hexes and the var names in the files that read them. The **lime and teal tokens are gone** — they were this surface's colorway for earlier passes the same day. The frieze bars still use only **1/3/4**. Inside the feed proper the colorway took on a **second, finer-grained set of jobs** at the end of 2026-07-25, which is why **`--indigo-2` was added** — it exists for the feed, not for the bars. One step, one job — **and every one of those three steps left the family across 2026-08-05/06, the SHAPE of the rule surviving all of it** (coat, bed, one line ink): **-1 COATED the container AND the post cards** (a post square is a piece of the container, so it is cut from the container's material rather than the platform's white `--paper-card` — the rule; the container went `--grey-4` on 2026-08-05 and the card followed it there and then one step further to `--grey-3` on 2026-08-06, so this level now has no role); **-2 WAS the scroll bed** the cards lie on (`.feed-stream__well` — `--grey-4` from 2026-08-05, `--grey-3` since 2026-08-06) — and, one level down inside a card, the **label rail's floor** (`.post-square__rail`), with the label chips on it wearing **-1** again: a `-1` chip on a `-2` tray is the same figure/ground step as a `-1` card on the `-2` well, which is why the rail did not need a tone of its own; **-3 DREW EVERY line** — the frieze lips on the container's side borders, the post card's then-`2px` outer border and its markdown pit's `1px` inner one, so only weight told them apart (the card's edges are an even 1px since 2026-08-06); it keeps just the head band's chrome now, the lips having left on 2026-08-05 and the card's whole line set on 2026-08-06. **-4 was left to the frieze bars' wave layer** and took on two more jobs across 2026-08-06: the media tabs strip's edges, and — after a walk out to `--grey-5`/`--grey-6` and a pass as a two-tone bevel — **every line on the post card again**, all four outer edges plus the seven inner ones. (The bars' lip passed through -4 the same day and ended at `--grey-4`, drawing nothing.) The card's lines share an ink BY RULE and move as a set, so grep `.post-square` before touching one. Two of those settled by walking: the **bed** went -4 → -3 → -2 (it started deep, when the full-bleed cards had nothing but the bed to separate them, and stepped back as the card gained its own outline), and the **card's outer border** went `1px` of -4 → `2px` of -3 — lighter and thicker together, because a hairline needs contrast to register and a wider band does not — **and back to -4 on 2026-08-06** (user ask) when the surfaces under it went neutral: a `--grey-4` card on a `--grey-3` bed separates by barely a step, so the line is now the only thing stating the card's shape, and it was the card's CORNERS that gave instead (`--radius-md` → `--radius-sm`). **`--indigo-8` is the colorway's INK** (added end of 2026-07-25): the post card's title text on its `--grey-1` plate.
