| `--grey-6` | **EVERY LINE ON THE PUBLIC FEED'S POST CARDS** since 2026-08-07 (user ask, "from indigo to grey") — the card's four outer edges, its byline hairline and vertical rule, the title plate's rim, the label rail and its strip hairline, the trust chip and the markdown pit's inner frame: **eight lines, ONE ink, differing only in WEIGHT**, which is the card's oldest rule. Grep `.post-square` and move them together or not at all. Not a fresh pick — it already held all eight for part of 2026-08-06 (and then just their top/right pair for the few minutes the box was a two-tone bevel) before the hue came back for a day, which is why the second trip out could land immediately: `--grey-5` had read as a soft suggestion of a card at the frieze seam and this level held. **THE CARD'S LINE SYSTEM SPLIT IN TWO on 2026-08-07** (a run of user asks): the byline's and the label strip's `border-bottom`s became a frieze band and an rgb hairline, and the OUTER edge followed them to `--grey-7`. What is left on this level is everything drawn INSIDE the card — pit rim, rail tray, title plate, byline vertical rule — all at 1px. **AND, since 2026-08-10 (user ask), THE FRIEZE BAND'S OWN TOP AND BOTTOM RULES** — the one place this ink is drawn on something that is NOT the card's material: the band is a `--grey-8` plate, two steps under this level, and framing it in the card's line ink is what stops it reading as a stripe floating on the square. Same 1px inner weight; see `.post-square__frieze`, and note the band's height dial carries a `+ 2px` so the rules do not eat the motif. |
| `--grey-7` | **The POST CARD's RGB HAIRLINE, its two rules** (2026-08-07, user ask, `layout/RgbHairline.vue`) — one of the card's two DIVIDER PLATES, the other being the frieze band's `--grey-8` plaque; every LINE the card draws stays `--grey-6`. It also held that plaque for part of the day (between -6 and -8) and the card's outer border for an hour of it — the only place on the feed drawn in this level, and a level this file had been missing (the media tabs bar once referenced a `$grey-7` that was never declared). The bands stand where the byline's and the label rail's hairlines were, so they began ON the card's line ink (`--grey-6`) and stepped down one tone on the user's second ask. Both readings hold at once: the eye takes -7 as the -6 line thickened rather than as a fourth material on a card that already carries three (coat, tray, pit floor), and the carve finally has ground under it — the motif is painted in two A100 accents (`--teal-11` → `--indigo-11`) and a groove reads as a groove only when the plate is well under what is cut into it (2.2:1 here, against -6's 1.7:1). **ALSO THE RGB HAIRLINE'S TWO RULES** since later the same day (user ask), which is the same argument one level out: a compound rule is read as one object, so its own lines sit a step off the host's ordinary hairlines instead of being mistaken for two of them — and on the feed it puts the card's two very different dividers on one plate tone. **AND THE CARD'S OUTER BORDER** since minutes later (user ask): edge + dividers on this level, the card's inner lines left on `--grey-6`, which is the card's one-ink rule re-stated as two groups rather than abandoned. |
| `--grey-9` | **The public feed post card's DEEPEST INK** since 2026-08-07 — the title plate's lettering and the trust chip's, both on their `--grey-1` floors, taking over from `--indigo-8` at the same weight against that floor. Also **the post information flyout's frieze plaque** (2026-07-26, third pass) — the base of the `FriezeBar` band inside `skeletons/SkeletonFlyout.vue` (was posts/SkeletonFlyout pre-2026-07-31), fed in through that component's **`--frieze-bar-base`** dial; every other placement of the bar leaves the dial unset and keeps `--brown-4`. The band was the last warm surface on a box whose coat (`--grey-3`) and lines (`--grey-4`) had both already gone neutral. It is NOT brown-4's counterpart in lightness the way `--grey-4` is: `#424242` is DARK where brown-4 is a light plaque, so the motif inverts — the `--brown-2` / `--brown-1` waves are untouched and now read as the light ON a dark strip instead of as figures on a pale one. That inversion is the point: it leaves the band the one carved and the one dark thing in an otherwise flat, pale box, with the `--brown-8` header ink and the waves themselves as the only warmth left on it |
| `--grey-10` | **No current role.** It was the INK of the NodeMini quoted inside the post information flyout for part of 2026-07-27 — first the header row (`--node-mini-head-ink`), then the foot's four glyphs beside it (`--node-mini-foot-glyph`, itself deleted with NodeMini's foot later that day) — and lost both to `--brown-10` within the day. Kept for the lesson, which is not about contrast: at the 900 index every candidate clears 12:1 on the panes it letters, so the question is WHOSE ink it is, and a neutral on a panel whose host is brown wherever it is anything could only say "not teal" where the brown says whose box this is |
| `--brown-8` | The left drawer's item ink — nav icons, labels and section headers on the brown-1 plaque (2026-07-24) — **and the stack/pins info-box ink** (2026-07-24, 8th pass): the kind icon, the title, the `.dock-bar__action` history button, and the parked `.dock-side-head` glyph that stands in for them (it was brown-4 before), so a widget's own mark keeps one color in both presentations — plus the **inverted brown chips** that fill with it over a `--brown-1` glyph: the drawer's pinned-top Back button and the nav bar's **unpinned pin tack** (2026-07-24; both hover to Quasar brown-7 `#5d4037`) — plus, on the flipside, the **pinned** tack's rim + pushpin over its brown-1 face (2026-07-24: they were brown-4 and went invisible the moment the slot underneath was painted brown-4, so the chip carries the same ink either way round) — Quasar palette brown-8 | `#4e342e` |
| `--brown-10` | **The INK of the NodeMini quoted inside the post information flyout** (2026-07-27) — its header row through that component's `--node-mini-head-ink` dial (landing mostly on the open glyph, the round pill and address chip being their own neutral objects), and the SOURCE PANE's text set on the pane by the flyout directly. So every WORD on that panel is the box's ink, while every surface under them is the node's (`--teal-1` panes on a `--grey-4` coat). (It also inked the foot's four GLYPHS through `--node-mini-foot-glyph` — never their counts, never the viewer's own vote — until NodeMini's foot and that dial went, later the same day.) The family's 900, standing at `--teal-10`'s own index, so the ink changed HUE and nothing else; it is the third tone at that index in one day (`--teal-10` → `--grey-10` → here). BROWN because the box is brown wherever it is anything at all — `--brown-8` inks the flyout's own header and the frieze band's waves are brown, the only warm marks on a plaque, a well and a line system otherwise drawn in neutrals — so a quoted node lettered in the HOST's family reads as part of the box where a neutral could only say "not teal". Two steps darker than that header ink (#3e2723 vs #4e342e): the box names itself once at the top, and the document it is holding is the darkest thing in it |
| `--teal-13` | **The 2px BASE under the node panel quoted into the post information flyout** (2026-07-27) — its bottom border, the panel's one mark of its own. Quasar teal-13 = accent A400 `#1de9b6`; a NEW token, added with that pass, which on this file's hand-declared Quasar mirrors means two lines in `_tokens.scss` (`$teal-13` + `--teal-13`). It took the job from `--teal-12` (A200 `#64ffda`), the pale mint that reads as a LINE at 1.08:1 on a light coat and says so quietly: -13 is the same hue with the chroma up and a step of value behind it (0.79 → 0.66 luminance), because a 2px edge on ONE side is a statement and its tone should carry as far as its geometry does. It is the one line in that box allowed to be a COLOUR rather than a tone — every other division there is a hairline in a tone. It also un-crosses a cost recorded on -12: the panel's HOVER is `--teal-12`, so base and pointer spoke one colour for a day, where the CHIP's rule (which this base copies one level up) is that an object's own mark and the pointer's are never the same at once. Keep the two apart if either moves | mint, accent A400 |
| `--neon-mid-blue` / `--neon-mid-pink` | Mid neon triad exposure — both now free: `--neon-mid-blue` was the removed stack button's lit state, `--neon-mid-pink` was the tack's pinned state (now a flat brown-1/brown-4 chip) | `#94daff` / `#ff94da` |
| `--surface-1..4` | Carved card surfaces | translucent paper tints |
| `--aqua-1..4` | Accent — cool/cyan scale | mint → deep teal |
| `--coral` / `--coral-deep` | Accent — warm | for emphasis chips, active state |
| `--light-cream` (`#FCF3E0`) | **THE PUBLIC FEED'S POST CARD COAT** (2026-08-07, user ask) — and the first member of **CREAM, the only family in `_tokens.scss` that is NOT a Quasar ramp and the only one named by WORD instead of index**. Both are deliberate: this tone is hand-picked, has no ramp behind it, and members arrive one at a time as they are needed, so a new one is **`--<modifier>-cream`** and the modifier says where it sits — never `--cream-N`, because an index promises a ramp and inventing the missing steps to justify one is how a palette grows tones nothing wears. Its job: a WARM sheet on the `--grey-4` plate, one step above its bed and separating by HUE as well as by lightness, which is the arrangement the cards have had since 2026-08-06 with the tone under it moving three times — `--grey-3` → `--brown-1` → here. The hour on `--brown-1` is what earned the new token: brown-1 is the CROWN STRIP's plaque and already stands on the feed as the head box's inner posts' motif, so a card wearing it read as that plaque BORROWED rather than as a card with a coat of its own. This is yellower and lighter — a paper cream where brown-1 is a greyed one. Everything else on the card stays grey (eight `--grey-6` lines, `--grey-9` inks, the pit's `--grey-1` floor, `--grey-3` chips in a `--grey-4` tray), so the feed reads as four materials, one job each: grey plate, indigo plaques, near-white pit, cream sheet. |
| `--halo-yellow` / `--halo-yellow-rgb` | Return halo | luminous yellow "you came back from here" glow (`.nav-return-halo`) |
| `--dock-bar-h` | Height of the dock header bar (`.dock-bar`) **and** of the parked stack/pins head glyph (`.dock-side-head`) that stands in for it — equal by construction, so the list box keeps its offset across presentations | 30 px |
| `--side-item-h` / `--side-item-gap` | The stack/pins **item rhythm**, shared by both faces of `SidePanelItem` (parked icon key + expanded informative row) and by both lists' `gap` (2026-07-24) — the row's text is compacted and the chip grown to meet this height, so a list unravels **in place** instead of re-flowing | 36 / 4 px |
| `--radius-sm/md/lg` | Corner radii | 4 / 8 / 14 px |
| `--shadow-carved` | Inner shadow style | for `@carved` |
| `--shadow-side-edge` | **Left-edge cast of the right-edge column** (2026-07-24) — worn by `StackPanel` + `PinsDrawer` in **both** presentations; no vertical offset, so the right-edge column reads as one raised strip lying on top of the page. **The nav bar's `.tack-slot` and `.burger-slot` dropped it on 2026-08-02** — an inward cast is ink painted onto the bar's own plaque right where the block meets it, which both darkened the bar (it is meant to read as the same flat material as the left drawer) and made each 41px block look ~5px wider than the 42px column above it | `-5px 0 12px rgba(var(--ink-rgb-deep), 0.16)` |

Legacy purple-on-dark tokens (`#6C63FF`, `#00BCD4`, `#0D0D1A`) have been removed. Any remaining references to them are dead code awaiting cleanup.

### Common compound styles

- `.pathos-card` — translucent paper surface with `@glass` + soft border
- `.maker-overlay` + `.maker-dialog` — full-page overlay for any maker
- `.interaction-bar` — bottom bar on cards (vote / comment / fork buttons)
- `.nav-btn` — bottom-bar button, a Quasar `<q-btn push>` wearing a **light-green-1** (`#f1f8e9`) face; the push preset provides the 3D lip + press animation natively (no fake `--surface-4` ledge); Nasalization face. (Footer `q-btn-group` bundles use **light-blue-1** `#e1f5fe` instead.)
- `.dock-window` + `.dock-bar` (height `--dock-bar-h`) + `.traffic` + `.dock-tabs`/`.dock-tab` — shared chrome for the six bottom-docked windows (thin header — title centred and traffic lights pinned at the LEFT edge desktop+mobile since 2026-08-04, the dots running red-yellow-green close/min/max, the true Mac order — folder-tab strip; the glass shell + 12px backdrop blur it still declares is now only the STACK/PINS widgets' base — the five creation docks, chat included since 2026-08-03, replace it with an opaque `--dock-coat` plaque) + the `dock-slide` transition + the `.is-split-left`/`.is-split-right` 50/50 tiling modifiers used during skeleton-builder element capture. `.dock-side-head` (the parked stack/pins head glyph) is `--dock-bar-h` tall too, standing in for the header — brown-8 ink, zero padding, 20px glyph since 2026-07-24 so it fills that band instead of floating in air (the box height is load-bearing for item alignment; trim the glyph, never the box); `.dock-bar--park` makes the whole header bar the park control on those two widgets, which carry **no traffic light** (2026-07-24 — the maker/uploader/builder/label/chat docks keep theirs) and paint their info box in one ink: `--brown-8` for the kind icon + title, `--brown-4` for the count meta. `.dock-bar__action` — a trailing control at the info box's very right end (20px, flat `--brown-8` glyph, no pebble or border, hover tint): today the stack/pins **history** clock button
- `.flyout-window` (2026-08-10) — the FLYOUT FAMILY's shell, extracted from `SkeletonFlyout.vue` the day the dashboard panel arrived so the two boxes would be one recipe rather than two copies of an argument that took a week to settle. (The panel became a DOCK hours later, which leaves the shell itself with one consumer — but the extraction is what let the dock keep the family's COAT anyway, through `.dock-window--dashboard`'s dials and the two hoisted pieces below.) It carries the `--grey-3` plaque with its `--grey-4` border, `--radius-lg` and full drop; the head (`.dock-bar` restated in the plaque's own tone with no rule under it, `.traffic` pinned `left: 8px`, title centred, symmetric 56px side padding that clears the cluster at BOTH dot sizes, brown-8 glyph/title + brown-4 meta ink); `.flyout-window__frieze` (the `slim` FriezeBar on a `--grey-9` base, which inverts the motif so the brown waves read as light on a dark strip); and `.flyout-window__well` (the sunk `--grey-4` floor, bevelled `--grey-5` top/left and `--brown-1` bottom/right). ⚠ **those last two are TOP-LEVEL rules, not children of `.flyout-window`** — they were nested for the length of one pass and came out the moment the dashboard panel stopped being a flyout and became a dock, since `.dock-window--dashboard` wears the creation shell with these two pieces INSIDE it and a band that only existed as a child of the flyout plaque could not follow. Neither rule ever depended on the parent, which is what made the nesting a convenience and the hoist free. **GEOMETRY IS NOT IN IT** — every flyout is placed by its host (`.feed-flyout` on FeedPage, `.dashboard-flyout` on MainLayout), which is also who owns maximize; the shell is `height: 100%` on a flex column and fills what it is handed. The TONE prose stays in SkeletonFlyout's style block, which is the family's record. **Under 600px its traffic dots grow 13px → 17px on an 8px gap — ONE rule shared with `.dock-window--creation`** (2026-08-10, user ask "make sure the traffic lights look bigger on mobile"), so the two families can never disagree about what a light is worth under a thumb; a flyout is if anything the more pressing case, overlaying the full width down there with its two dots the only way out besides an Escape key a phone does not have.
- `.nav-return-halo` — luminous yellow double-pulse applied by `useStateHolder` to the `[data-nav-focus]` element matching the route the user just returned from (`MicroChip`, `InfoChip`, `MiniPanel`, `HashLink` all stamp `data-nav-focus` with their route)
- `.md-rendered` — carved-paper markdown body used by `PostViewerPage`, `NodeDetailPage`, `SkeletonPage` (docs viewer)

## Routing + auth

`router/routes.js` plus `router/index.js` (the guard). The guard:
- Allows unauthenticated visits to `/auth/*`.
- All other routes require `auth.store.token`. On entry it triggers `/api/identity/verify` (single check on app boot via `App.vue`). On 401 the api.js interceptor clears localStorage and routes to `/#/auth`.

## API connection

`src/services/api.js` reads `process.env.API_URL` (default: `http://localhost:3000/api`). Set via `quasar.config.js` `build.env.API_URL` for different environments.

## Keylogger stub

The typing session keylogger for NOTE nodes is stubbed at the data model:
- DB: `node` table has `content TEXT` and `ancestor_id` for versions.
- A `KEYSTROKES` skeleton kind is *planned* but no service writes to it (see [docs/concepts/skeletons.md](../../docs/concepts/skeletons.md) and [docs/gaps/contradictions.md](../../docs/gaps/contradictions.md) #10).
- UI: the makers' note editors (`MakerDock`, `UploaderDock` Write mode) do not yet track keystrokes.

## Dev flow surfaces (2026-08-01 — docs/concepts/development-flow.md)

| piece | notes |
|---|---|
| `components/dev/GithubPrCard.vue` | The native face of a `GITHUB_PR` instance — GitHub sends `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, so a PR is never an EmbedFrame. Reads the walk's slot rows (`slots` prop, textValues only — never fetches): repo/number head, merge-purple state pill (`#8250df`; closed red, open green), 2-line-clamped title, author / base-branch / `+A −D` facts, and the whole card is ONE outbound `<a>` whose href is derived from `REPO`+`NUMBER` (walks only inline NOTE text, so the URL slot's LINK node is provenance, not the card's source). |
| SkeletonMini / SkeletonTable delegation | `walk.name === 'GITHUB_PR' && !is_schema` → renders GithubPrCard instead of the generic field table (both faces carry the same branch since 2026-08-10 — the flyout's generic face is SkeletonTable now). This is what makes a `![[pathos:skeletons/…]]` block ref in a PR post's body bloom into the card on feed cards, viewers, and the flyout. SkeletonPage mounts the card above the slot table in both branches (owned + read-only), the ClaimBand pattern. |
| FeedStream label lens | Head band, after the trust lens: a funnel button opens a `q-menu` with the compact `LabelPicker`; an active filter renders as a dark indigo-8 chip (name + ×, click to clear). Rides `GET /feed?label=` and syncs `/#/feed?label=<id>` (router.replace; `watch` on `route.query.label` BOTH ways — hash-router gotos reuse the mounted stream, so an onMounted-only read misses in-app arrivals). Every rail chip also grows a hover-revealed funnel (`.post-square__label-filter`) that filters by that chip without leaving the feed. |

## Integrity surfaces (2026-08-08 — docs/plans/integrity-debt-mitigation.md)

| piece | notes |
|---|---|
| `.micro-chip__integrity` / `.info-chip__integrity` / `.node-mini__integrity` | The traffic-light dot, one grammar at three scales (7/8/8px, claim-dot palette: `#2e6a3a` green, `#a03d3d` red + a 2px halo on red only). GREEN = the element's chain proof verified on the last read (tooltip "proof verified", no click). RED = a check contradicted — tooltip names it, click routes `/feed?flyout=<report-ref>` (Talavero's narrated report; until narration lands the ref is the raw INTEGRITY_INCIDENT evidence — SkeletonFlyout renders either). NO dot = lawful-unproven (draft / pre-epoch / unsigned-legacy): green must mean verified, so the law draws nothing. |
| data flow | The verdict rides the API payloads, never computed client-side: `node.integrity` on enriched nodes (NodeMini reads it directly), `summary.integrity` on `/refs/summary` rows (InfoChip's self-resolve; RefMicro passes it down to MicroChip as the `integrity` prop beside `claim-status`). |
| `.node-mini__withheld` | The violated node's body face: the API already nulled `content`/`file.text`/`file.url` (`content_withheld: true`), and instead of pretending emptiness the panel says "body withheld — integrity check failed · open Talavero's report" (red ink, `report` icon, whole line clicks through to the flyout). Media/embed branches never render for a violated node because the API withheld their sources. |
| deferred | The full-page Talavero integrity banner (NodeDetailPage third face beside 404/AccessDenied — needs its own art treatment worthy of the mascot) and an `.integrity-dot` on LockedChip (a locked element's proof state is the owner's business, undecided). |
